## Wizard: use an existing API client for the write back — 2026-10-16
- Branch: `feat/wizard-existing-client` (worktree `../whoobe_cms-wizard-client`)
- Step 3 of *Connect a flow* offers **Create a new API client** (as before) or **Use an existing one**. The list has only the active key clients that may write the chosen class (`data:write:<Class>` or `data:write:*`; `usableClients`, `clientCanWrite`); with none, the option is disabled and says so. With an existing client nothing is created and no key is shown (the flow already has it); the client goes in the subscription's *ignore changes made by* and is never deleted, even if a later call fails. The result names whose key to use instead of inventing one (the HTTP-step block and the Node-RED environment). `planConnection` refuses a client that is gone, suspended, a token client, or cannot write the class, saying why.
- Why: one engine often runs several flows with one key (a Node-RED container has one `WHOOBE_KEY`); a new client per connection forced a new key each time.
- 53 wizard tests (7 new, one existing expectation updated for the new `useClient` field), 71 with the other unit tests, `vue-tsc` and the docs gate pass. Tried in Chromium on a copy of the development database: with a class the client cannot write the option is disabled; with Contact it is offered; connecting created no client (7 before and after), the subscription ignores exactly that client, the result shows the note and no key box, the test event and the create were delivered, and a write with the existing key (200) was not sent back to the flow. The copy and the processes were removed.
- Docs: `communication/object-events.md` (step 3 of the wizard) and `communication/node-red.md`.

## Node-RED as a ready integration: preset, wizard script, guide — 2026-10-16
- Branch: `feat/node-red-integration` (worktree `../whoobe_cms-node-red-int`)
- **Preset** `apps/gateway/presets/node-red.json`, "Node-RED (this machine only)": `http://localhost:1880`, no authentication, a starter route `POST whoobe-events`; the description says it works only for a Node-RED on the same machine as the Gateway. Listed in the guide's preset table; the presets unit test checks it loads.
- **Wizard** (`object-events-wizard.ts`, `ObjectEventsWizard.vue`): an *Engine* list (detect / Node-RED / other); Node-RED is also guessed from port 1880. For Node-RED with *the flow writes back* the result shows the **code of a function node** that updates one field, with `<FIELD>` and `<NEW VALUE>` placeholders, and the two environment variables it reads (the key and the Gateway address stay out of the flow file). The hint for Node-RED says that this machine is the right place and warns about any other address; a 404 says to compare the route's path with the *http in* node's, letter by letter (the typo met in practice). An existing route is reused whether it was saved with or without the leading slash (presets save it without).
- **Guide** `docs/release/communication/node-red.md` (model: same machine, not exposed, no editor in production; connecting a flow; what the flow receives; developing and deploying; securing the entry; troubleshooting), indexed in `communication/README.md`, linked from `integrations.md`, `object-events.md` and the example's README.
- Tests: 46 wizard tests (11 new: engine choice and guess, one route, route reuse, hints, the 404 sentence, and the generated script is **executed** to check what it builds), presets 7, `vue-tsc` passes, the docs gate passes. Tried in Chromium on a copy of the development database: the preset appears in *Add Endpoint* and fills the base URL, with Node-RED chosen the dialog shows the script block with the placeholders and the environment, and the test event is reported as received. The copy and processes were removed.

## Node-RED example: develop locally, run unexposed — 2026-10-16
- Branch: `feat/node-red-deploy` (worktree `../whoobe_cms-node-red`)
- `examples/automation-engines/node-red/`: the flow moved to `data/flows.json` with `data/settings.js` (editor and admin API only when `NODE_RED_EDITOR=1`, flow file written one node per line so a change is a readable git diff), `docker-compose.dev.yml` (editor on 127.0.0.1, saves into `./data`) and `docker-compose.prod.yml` (no published port, no editor, flow and settings mounted read-only, read-only root file system, capabilities dropped, on Whoobe's Docker network; refuses to start without the credentials and the signing secret), `.env.example`, `.gitignore` (only the flow and the settings are tracked), and a README with the development to production workflow. Not part of the distribution.
- Tried on a copy of the development database with Node-RED 4.1: in development the editor and API are on 127.0.0.1 only, a flow deployed through the API is written to the file and a one-node change is a one-line diff, a signed event is delivered and the record gets the note; in production `docker port` is empty and the host's localhost refuses, from another container `/`, `/flows`, `/settings` and `/auth/login` answer 404, the flow file and the root file system are read-only, the Gateway delivers to the container's address on the private network and the record gets the note, and copying a changed `flows.json` then restarting applied the change. Scratch containers, networks and database were removed.
- Found on the way: Node-RED's default file format is one line (a one-node change rewrites the file): `flowFilePretty: true` fixes the diff. A flow function that builds new messages loses `msg.res` (fixed earlier).

## Object events, phase 0: implementation plan — 2026-10-16
- Branch: `feat/object-events` (worktree `../whoobe_cms-object-events`, from `master` at `fb60a8f`)
- `docs/plans/datamodel/object-events-phase0.md` (new): the plan, nothing implemented. Inventory of the data-object write paths and their callers (core-api, gateway API clients, bundle import, migration tool); finding: the write functions take no actor yet, so phase 0 adds an optional `opts.actor`; tables and one migration after `0058`; seven steps (baseline, pure event module, tables, emit inside transactions, actor, gateway worker, subscriptions API, end-to-end with Activepieces); risks.
- Step 0 (baseline): `packages/core` suite on a disposable Postgres 16: 340 tests passed, 30 files (the `@whoobe-cms/schema` package must be built first in a fresh worktree). `packages/core/src/db/data-objects-records.integration.test.ts` (new, 10 tests, skipped without `PORTABILITY_TEST_DATABASE_URL`): characterises create / update / delete / delete by class / bulk import / Pimcore upsert (drafts, unique clashes, locale group and non-localized sync, `publishedAt` set once, state kept by the upsert) so the coming transactions and events can be checked against it.
- Step 1: `packages/core/src/db/object-events.ts` (new, pure, no database) and `object-events.test.ts` (19 tests): `changedFields` (deep compare of attributes, key order ignored, plus key / path / parentId; names only, never values), `classifyUpdate` (which events one update produced, fixed order updated, transitioned, published / unpublished; nothing when nothing changed; a pure publish is only `object.published`), `buildEvent` (the envelope, actor defaults to `system`), `buildBulkEvent` (one summary, at most 10 ids). `published` / `unpublished` are classified here already (cheap, avoids a publish that emits nothing); whether the first delivery sends them is a subscription matter. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- Step 2: migration `0059_object_events.sql` (hand-written like the recent ones, expand-only, journal entry added) and `object-events-schema.ts`: `object_events` (the event, no foreign key to class or object so it outlives them), `object_event_subscriptions` (class or all, event types, Gateway endpoint and route with `ON DELETE SET NULL`, payload mode, ignored actors, signing secret reference), `object_event_deliveries` (status, attempts, next attempt, truncated error; unique per event and subscription). `object-events-records.ts`: `insertEvent(runner, event)` writes the event and one delivery per enabled subscription that matches class, type and actor, with the caller's transaction runner, idempotent per event id. `object-events-records.integration.test.ts` (8 tests: matching rules, repeated insert, no delivery for older events, bulk summary, rollback with the transaction, cascades). Whole `packages/core` suite and `tsc --noEmit` green. Nothing emits events yet.
- Step 3: `createDataObject`, `updateDataObject`, `deleteDataObject`, `deleteDataObjectsByClassId` and `bulkImportDataObjects` (`packages/core/src/db/data-objects-records.ts`) now write the change, the sync of the language siblings and the object events in ONE transaction (the search index is still updated after the commit). `updateDataObject` reads the row `FOR UPDATE` first, so the events describe exactly what that call changed (it also replaces two extra reads). Each function takes an optional last argument `{ actor }` (`WriteOptions`; the bulk import takes it in its options); without it the actor is `system`. Create emits `object.created` (+ an `object.updated` with actor `system` for each sibling whose shared value changed); update emits `updated` / `transitioned` / `published` / `unpublished` as classified (nothing when nothing changed); delete emits `object.deleted` for the row as it was; "Empty data" and the bulk import emit ONE `object.bulk`. `upsertDataObjectByPimcoreId` emits nothing (the migration tool will send one summary per run).
- `insertEvent` now stores the event only when at least one subscription matches (an event nobody listens to is not written: no table growth on instances that do not use the feature), and `OBJECT_EVENTS_ENABLED=0` (kill switch) stops emitting. `data-objects-events.integration.test.ts` (13 tests): no subscription = nothing stored, actor, loop guard, locales, every update kind, delete, bulk summaries, **rollback of create / update / delete / bulk when the event cannot be written** (a trigger refuses the delivery), kill switch. The 10 characterisation tests of step 0 pass unchanged. Whole `packages/core` suite 391 passed; `tsc` clean for core (the apps show only the usual missing-package errors of an unbuilt worktree).
- Step 4 (actor and guard): every caller now says who wrote. core-api: `eventActorOf(req)` (the signed-in user) on the data-object create / update / delete / import / "Empty data" / batch publish / batch delete / workflow transition routes; the public form submission is `system` with id `form-submission`. Gateway client router: `{ type: 'api-client', id }` for the client's create and patch (so a subscription can ignore the engine's own client). New `WriteOptions.silent` for writers that are bulk by nature: the bundle import (`packages/core/src/portability/import.ts`) and the Pimcore migration (`packages/migration-tool/src/cli.ts`) write silently and send ONE `object.bulk` per class (import) or per run (migration) through `emitBulkSummary`, after the whole import succeeded; "clear before import" is a `delete` summary with actor `import`. `packages/core/src/server.ts` exports the event types and `emitBulkSummary`.
- `object-events-guard.test.ts` (9 tests): a scan of the source fails when a function of `data-objects-records.ts` writes `data_objects` without an event or outside a transaction (only `upsertDataObjectByPimcoreId` and the sibling sync are silent by design, with the reason in the test), or when ANY other file in `packages` / `apps` writes the table. Checked by adding a sneaky writer: the guard fails. One more integration test for `silent` and `emitBulkSummary`. Whole core suite 401 passed; core-api, gateway and migration-tool typecheck (apart from the missing-package errors of an unbuilt worktree).
- Step 5 (delivery): `packages/core/src/db/object-events-delivery.ts` (new): `deliverClaim(claim, deps)` delivers one claimed event and records the outcome; `object-events.ts` gets `BACKOFF_MS` / `nextAttemptPlan` (waits 1 min, 5 min, 30 min, 2 h, 12 h after the 1st to 5th failure, the 6th failure ends `dead`) and `signBody` (`sha256=` HMAC over `timestamp.body`). `object-events-records.ts`: `claimDueDeliveries(limit)` (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, leases each taken row for 2 minutes instead of holding a transaction, so a crashed worker's deliveries come back), `markDelivered`, `markFailed` (error text cut to 300 characters; the consumer's answer body is never stored). Headers sent: `X-Whoobe-Event-Id`, `X-Whoobe-Event-Type`, `X-Whoobe-Timestamp`, `X-Whoobe-Signature` (when the subscription names a signing secret: `signingSecretRef` is the NAME of an environment variable on the instance; a missing variable fails the attempt without sending). A removed endpoint or route, a route that is not POST / PUT / PATCH and a route with path parameters are `dead` at once; an inactive endpoint retries. `packages/core/src/server.ts` exports the schema, records and delivery modules.
- `apps/gateway/src/object-event-worker.ts` (new) and `server.ts`: the worker polls every 2 s (`OBJECT_EVENTS_CONCURRENCY`, default 5; `OBJECT_EVENTS_ENABLED=0` turns it off) and sends with the endpoint's credentials (`buildAuthHeaders`) through `proxyFetch` (10 s timeout, `safeFetch` SSRF guard, outbound allow-list); request plugins are not applied (there is no caller request). Tests: 3 more unit tests (backoff, signature) and `object-events-delivery.integration.test.ts` (9: claim and lease, two workers never share a delivery, due times, 2xx, retry with backoff, dead after the last, permanent causes, missing secret, long error). Smoke test with a real HTTP receiver (not committed): in production a loopback target is refused (`blocked: loopback address`, delivery retried), with `OUTBOUND_ALLOW_HOSTS=127.0.0.1` it is delivered; the signature verifies, the actor is in the payload and no attribute value is. Finding: `OUTBOUND_ALLOW_PRIVATE` defaults to true, so private ranges are open unless it is set to false (noted in the proposal).
- Step 6 (subscriptions API): permission `object_events:manage` (`packages/core/src/auth/permissions.ts`; the idempotent seed gives it to `admin` on the next start, no other default role has it); `/object-event-subscriptions` and `/object-event-deliveries` are refused in the public sandbox by path (`sandbox.ts`). Core-api routes (`apps/core-api/src/server.ts`): `GET/POST /object-event-subscriptions`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /object-event-subscriptions/:id`, `POST /object-event-subscriptions/:id/test` (a synthetic `object.updated` with `"test": true`, queued for that one subscription), `GET /object-event-subscriptions/:id/deliveries` (newest first, `status`, `limit`; only what happened to a delivery, never the event's content or the consumer's answer), `POST /object-event-deliveries/:id/retry` (only a `dead` one; 409 otherwise). A subscription must point at an existing Gateway endpoint and a route of it that is POST / PUT / PATCH with a fixed path; the class must exist; unknown or malformed ids are 404; a duplicate name is 409.
- `validateSubscriptionInput` (`object-events.ts`): `payloadMode` is `"minimal"` only (the field allow-list is phase 2); `signingSecretRef` must be `null` or an environment variable name starting with `OBJECT_EVENTS_SECRET_` so a subscription can never use another secret of the instance (the database password, a session key) as its HMAC key; the worker enforces the same prefix. `scripts/security/routes.expected.json`: the 8 new routes. Tests: 4 unit tests of the validation, 5 integration tests (CRUD, endpoint deletion, test event, log filter and order, retry) and `scripts/security/api/object-events.test.ts` (10 tests, run against the throwaway stack built from this branch: access, validation, targets, lifecycle, an object created through the API reaching the worker with a missing signing secret failing closed, the Gateway route called and its status logged, the log without content, retry, delete) plus the sandbox paths in `sandbox.test.ts`. On that stack `route-drift`, `authz-matrix` (965) and `granular-permissions` (188) pass with the new routes. The permission is `object_events:manage` (underscore, like `data_objects:list`): the security harness reads permission names with a pattern that does not accept a hyphen.
- Step 7 (end to end): `docs/plans/automations/object-events-activepieces.md` (new): the recipe. Tried on a COPY of the development database (a scratch database made with `pg_dump`, class Contact; the live database was not touched because migration `0059` carries a later date in the drizzle journal than master's next ones would, which could make drizzle skip them) with core-api and gateway of this branch on ports 3203 / 3201 and the user's local Activepieces: a Contact created, updated and deleted through the API plus a test event gave 4 deliveries, all `delivered` on the first attempt; with a plain HTTP receiver the payload carried the changed field names and the actor and no attribute value. Phase 0 is complete on this branch.

## Flow Orchestrator: proposal for an optional app for request / reply flows — 2026-10-16
- Branch: `master`
- `docs/plans/automations/flow-orchestrator-app.md` (new): a proposal, nothing implemented. A separate optional (enterprise) app, outside the core, that manages **runs** for flows such as "record inserted, processed, external API called, answer returned to the original caller": entry via the app or via object events, one step only (the engine's webhook), the engine calls back `/runs/:id/complete`, the result is written on the record as a workflow transition. The caller chooses per request **synchronous** (with a `202` fallback on timeout) or **asynchronous** (polling or signed callback). API draft, data of the app, idempotency, SSRF and allow-list rules, operations, phases, 10 open questions, risks. Complements `docs/plans/datamodel/data-objects-automations.md`.
- Update 2026-10-16: owner's decisions recorded (section 12): separate app; synchronous wait configurable per flow (30 s default, operator ceiling, for services over a minute; nginx / Gateway timeouts and `Prefer: wait`); `storeInput` off; entry via events in the first version (core outbox becomes a phase 0 dependency); callback auth HMAC and OAuth client credentials; inbound IDM tokens verified by the Gateway and forwarded as identity (synchronous replies need the caller to come through the app or a Gateway route); engine calls `/complete`; payments out of scope; open licence. Phases reordered; 3 questions left. Name chosen: **Whoobe Flows** (`apps/flows`, `FLOWS_ENABLED`, compose profile `flows`); "Flow Orchestrator" stays only as the working title of the page.
- Update 2026-10-16 (later): Gateway forwarding confirmed; the 300 s ceiling confirmed and shown in a callout. First flows added (section 12.1): A "forward and confirm" (record sent unchanged to the engine; reply `{ "success": true }` or `{ "success": false, "error": "<text, cut to 500 chars>" }`), B "acknowledge with an identifier" (reply with the record id or an allow-listed field). New flow options `replyWhen` (`completed` | `created`), `responseShape` (`outcome` | `fields`), `payloadMode`. Failed run: `502` with the agreed body (decided). No open questions left.
- First engine: **Activepieces** (n8n is excluded because of its licence); the spike sections of both proposals say so.

## Data Objects Automations: proposal for object events (listeners) — 2026-10-15
- Branch: `master`
- `docs/plans/datamodel/data-objects-automations.md` (was a 5-line stub): a proposal, nothing implemented. Object events (created, updated, published, unpublished, deleted, transitioned, one summary for bulk runs) delivered to an external engine through the existing Gateway endpoints and routes, from a transactional outbox with retries, a delivery log, loop and flood guards; why no in-process listeners / controllers / plugins; what stays as class rules; synchronous hooks left as a separate decision; phases, security, 9 open questions for the owner.
- Update 2026-10-16: transports (webhook default, optional MQTT publish from the same outbox), "PostgreSQL triggers" as a considered alternative (safety net only; LISTEN/NOTIFY only as worker wake-up), "Engine on the same instance" (internal network, `OUTBOUND_ALLOW_HOSTS`), open questions 10 and 11.

If the form has unsaved edits (key or attributes differ from the loaded row) it asks before discarding them. Disabled for a record not saved yet. `loadTranslations()` tested the placeholder id of the tab's props, so a record created and saved in the same tab could never be reloaded: it now tests the current (real) group id.

## Object edit form: field classes really override the defaults; "Input styles" per control type — 2026-10-15
- Branch: `master` (not committed yet)
- Bug: a field's custom class (Class > Fields) was appended to the built-in classes of the control, and Tailwind does not give the later class in an attribute priority, so `py-1` lost against the built-in `py-2`. It was also put on the outer wrapper AND on the control (padding and background twice), against the comment in the file.
- `apps/admin-dashboard/src/utils/field-control-classes.ts` (new): built-in classes per control type (`text`, `textarea`, `select`, `checkbox`), `resolveControlLayout()` = the class's string for the type (or the built-in one) with the field's classes merged OVER it with `tailwind-merge`; arbitrary values still become inline style.
- `utils/custom-classes.ts`: `splitPlacementClasses()`: `col-*`, `row-*`, `order-*`, `self-*` go on the field wrapper (the item of the section grid), everything else on the control.
- `DataObjectFieldControl.vue` (+ `DataObjectForm.vue` passing `viewConfig.inputClasses`), `MasterClassTab.vue`: Class > View Layout > "Input styles" (one string per type, filled with the built-in value, Reset per row and for all; only values that differ are stored). `packages/core/src/schema/dynamic.ts`: `ClassViewConfig.inputClasses`.
- Follow-up: the URL slug, array (tags), link and JSON inputs and the "+ Add block" selector had their own fixed classes (`.field`) and ignored both the class's Input styles and the field's classes; they now use the text / textarea / select styles (JSON keeps monospace and its red border on a parse error), and the rows of a block or field collection are edited with a nested form that now receives the same input styles. Still outside the styles: rich text and Markdown editors, and the image / video / asset pickers (their own components).
- Markdown / rich-text editors ignored a height set on the field (`h-[600px] min-h-[500px]`): the class lands on the editor's outer box but the writing area had fixed `rows=8` and `max-h-96`. `MarkdownEditor.vue` and `WysiwygEditor.vue` get a `fill` prop (set by `DataObjectFieldControl.vue` when the field's classes contain `h-`, `min-h-` or `max-h-`, or the inline style they become): the box becomes a flex column and the writing / preview / HTML-source area takes the whole height. Without a height class nothing changes.
- New dependency: `tailwind-merge` 3.7.0 in `apps/admin-dashboard` (exact version, MIT, no dependencies, published 2026-09-12); `pnpm-lock.yaml`.
- `scripts/security/unit/field-control-classes.test.ts` (new, 7 tests). `docs/release/data/data-model.md`.
- Objects list: "Rows per page" showed every remaining record from page 2 on (the `<select>` gives the size as a string, `10 + "10"` is `"1010"`), and "All" produced NaN. `effectivePageSize` is now always a finite number.

## Class fields: `unique` — 2026-10-14
- Branch: `master`
- `ClassFieldDefinition.unique` (core schema) and a "unique" checkbox next to required / localized in the class field editor (`FieldListEditor.vue`).
- Enforced where objects are written: `createDataObject` and `updateDataObject` (so the dashboard, the form submit, the client API and the portability import all go through it) throw `UniqueViolationError` (`code: 'UNIQUE_VIOLATION'`) when another object of the class has the same value (JSONB containment check). An empty value (`''`, null, `[]`) never clashes, `0` and `false` are values; a localized field is compared within the same language; the language rows of one object never clash with each other.
- Answers: core-api `POST` / `PATCH /data-objects` and the gateway client API `409` with the message and `field`; a form submit answers `409 "<field> is already in use"` (never the stored value).
- Bulk import (`POST /data-objects/bulk`): a row whose value another object, or an earlier row of the same chunk, already has is left out and listed in `rejected` (`{ key, message }`); the rest of the chunk is saved. The class import counts them as failed and shows the first message.
- Not checked: the Pimcore migration upserts (migrated data goes in as it is), and existing duplicates are not detected when the flag is turned on: the second of two equal objects cannot be saved again until one of them changes.
- Tests: `uniqueValuesToCheck`, `UniqueViolationError` (4); `tsc` of core, core-api, gateway and `vue-tsc` pass. The database check itself has not been run against Postgres here. Restart core-api and the gateway.

- Branch: `master`
- Core API: `GET /data-classes/:id/object-count` and `POST /data-classes/:id/empty`; the second deletes every object of the class (all locales, published or not) and its search-index entries, keeps the class, and answers `{ deleted }`. Irreversible, so the body must carry `confirm` = the class name (`400` otherwise), enforced on the server, not only in the dashboard; the action is logged (`logger.warn`). Same permissions as deleting an object (`data_objects:delete` or `objects:write`). Core: `countDataObjectsByClassId`; `deleteDataObjectsByClassId` now returns how many it deleted (the migration "clear" option is unchanged).
- Dashboard: `EmptyClassModal.vue` shows how many objects will go and asks to type the class name; opened by "Empty data" in Settings > Classes (each class) and in the Data Objects list toolbar (the list reloads afterwards).
- `tsc` (core, core-api) and `vue-tsc` pass; not clicked through in the browser, nor run against a database. Restart core-api.

The old "Import Classes" (everything as parsed) stays.

## Pimcore object migration: text class ids ("Unknown column 'NaN'") — 2026-10-14
- Branch: `master`
- Problem: importing objects failed with `Unknown column 'NaN' in 'where clause'`: `runObjectMigration` did `Number(dataClass.pimcoreId)`, which is `NaN` for a Pimcore 10/11 class id such as `Car` or `CU`, and the id went into `WHERE o_classId = NaN`.
- `pimcoreClassIdOf` (`source-adapter.ts`): all digits -> number, otherwise the text as it is; `PimcoreClassId = number | string` in the adapter interface (`listObjects`, `getObjectAttributes`, class and object summaries) and the MySQL adapter (the id names the `object_<id>`, `object_localized_<id>_<locale>`, `object_relations_<id>` and `object_collection_*_<id>` tables unchanged). 2 new tests.
- Not run against the user's Pimcore database here (only types and unit tests): if the tables of the install differ (Pimcore 11 drops the `o_` column prefix of `objects`) the next error will name the column.

## Pimcore class migration: the fields were not read (only the system dates) — 2026-10-14
- Branch: `master`
- Problem: importing a class gave only `o_creationDate` / `o_modificationDate`. `parseFieldsSummaryLines` expected summary lines like `- name [input]`, but Pimcore writes them as ` * - name [input]` (the hand-built test fixture had no ` * ` prefix, so the tests never caught it): no field was found. The `' * '` prefix is now stripped; a new test uses the real format (top-level, localized and block children).
- Checked on the 18 real `definition_*.php` files of `<samples>/classes`: every file gives its id (numeric or text: `CU`, `DEALER`, `Car`...), its fields and relation targets (for example Car: `brand -> Brand`, `segment -> CustomerSegment`).
- Known limits: field types of Pimcore plugins (`gender`, `firstname`, `lastname`, `time`, `table`, `targetGroup`, `objectbricks`) are kept by name and are not editable types in the dashboard yet; importing the objects of a class with a text id is not supported yet.

## Pimcore class migration: class id/name parser accepts string ids — 2026-10-14
- Branch: `master`
- Problem: "Could not find the class's own id/name near the top of the file" for 13 of 18 `definition_*.php` files. `parsePimcoreClassMeta` (`packages/migration-tool`) only accepted a numeric `'id' => '12'` within 10 simple `'key' => value,` pairs after `ClassDefinition::__set_state(array(`: Pimcore 10/11 write string ids (`'CAR'`) and some classes have longer or comma-containing properties (description, implementsInterfaces) before the id.
- Parser: any quoted or numeric id, then the name, inside a 600-character window at the top (a nested layout panel's id/name is still not taken). 4 new tests; the error message now says what was expected.
- Not verified on the user's files (not available here). Known limit: importing the OBJECTS of a class still assumes a numeric Pimcore class id (`Number(pimcoreId)`, `object_store_<id>` tables in the adapter): classes now import, objects of a class with a string id need a follow-up.

## Class import: data in chunks, one transaction each — 2026-10-14
- Branch: `master`
- Core: `bulkImportDataObjects(classId, rows, { onConflict, locale })` (`db/data-objects-records.ts`): a chunk of rows is inserted (and, with `onConflict: 'update'`, existing objects with the same key updated) in ONE transaction, all or nothing; drafts, default locale; attributes the class has no field for are dropped; search index updated after the commit (an indexing failure never undoes the import). `planBulkImport` (pure, 4 tests) decides insert / update / skip.
- Core API: `POST /data-objects/bulk` (`classId`, `rows: [{ key?, attributes }]`, `onConflict`), at most 500 rows, same permissions as creating an object; 400 on a bad body, 404 for an unknown class.
- Admin dashboard: the file and Integration imports send chunks of 100 rows one after the other (`utils/class-import.ts`) instead of one request per row (1000 rows: 10 requests, not 1000); a failed chunk is counted and the next one still goes. New choice "Object key": generated, or the value of a column (a repeated value is one object, the last row wins).
- `tsc` of core and core-api, `vue-tsc` and the core unit tests pass; the endpoint and transaction have not been run against a database here.

## Editor Preview: a gateway form is submitted with the admin session — 2026-10-14

- Gateway: `POST /api/v1/gateway/preview-submit` (`router.ts`), the same handler and checks as the public submit (binding, field allowlist, limits, outcome) behind the admin session and `gateway:manage`, without the site-key step (`createSubmitHandler({ adminPreview })`).

- Checked on the running gateway with a temporary admin session (revoked after): public submit `401`, preview-submit without a session `401`, preview-submit with the session passes the site-key step (reaches the field validation, `400`, nothing sent to the automation).

## Data bindings read GET routes only; failed previews are not repeated — 2026-10-14
- Branch: `master`
- Problem seen in the editor: `POST /api/v1/gateway/preview-resolve` answered `502` ("aborted" in the gateway log) for bindings on the Activepieces webhook route. The preview forces a GET; a sync webhook (`POST .../sync`) answers only when its flow ends, so the GET waited for the 15 s timeout (checked: `curl` GET on that route does not answer in 20 s), and the editor repeated it at every change.
- Core: `unreadableRouteReason()` (`gateway/route-readable.ts`, 3 tests). Gateway `preview-resolve` answers `400` with the reason at once when the route is configured only with another method.
- Editor: the Gateway panel and the binding picker list GET routes only; `data-resolver.ts` does not ask again for 30 s for a binding that just failed and logs one warning (with the endpoint and route and the reason) instead of an error with a stack.
- Docs: `communication/integrations.md` ("A data binding reads GET routes only").
- The Gateway panel and the picker say, for an endpoint that has only POST routes, that those routes are used from the form (Advanced > Form > Target > Gateway endpoint). Checked in the running editor with a real session: that form control lists `POST /w11…/sync` for Activepieces; `preview-resolve` on it now answers `400` in 8 ms.
- Not changed: an existing binding on a POST route stays in the page (it shows nothing); remove it or rebind it to a GET route.

## Linked bindings: a selection as a route parameter (dynamic bindings) — 2026-10-14
- Branch: `master`
- A binding whose `params` hold `{selection.name}` (for example `catalogue/category/{id}` with `id = {selection.category}`) is loaded in the browser when the visitor chooses an item of another list, and at load when the choice is in the address. Plan and guide updated (`docs/plans/cms/linked-bindings.md`, `docs/release/content/linked-bindings.md`).
- Schema: `selectionRefOf`, `bindingSelectionNames`, `substituteSelectionParams`. Renderer: the walker renders such a container empty and marks it (`dynamicList`), `walkGatewayValue` shared with the browser, new `resolveDynamicChildren` (`dynamic-list.ts`), `resolveGatewayBinding` with a same-origin proxy path, `ApiClient.gatewayResolveProxyPath`, `track` / `subscribe` on the selection handle. 6 + 2 tests.
- Adapters: React (`useDynamicChildren`), Vue (`WhoobeNode`), Svelte (`WhoobeContainer`, `dynamic.js`); `createWhoobeApi` takes the resolve path; one SSR test each; checked in a real browser with each adapter (start empty, click, change, clear, `?category=13` at load, resolved through the proxy without a key header).
- Generated sites (`packages/publish`): Next.js `app/api/whoobe-gateway-resolve/route.ts` and Nuxt `server/api/whoobe-gateway-resolve.post.ts` (forward only `bindingId` and `params`, adding the site key); the layouts pass the path.
- Editor: Preview loads the list when the selection is made (tested with mocked gateway: no call with an empty choice), `resolveTree` skips it, the gateway picker suggests `{selection.name}` and explains; such a binding is never made relative to an item (2 tests).
- Not done: a Data Class query with a `{selection.x}` filter, mobile app.

## Nested bindings: a list inside the item of another list — 2026-10-13
- Branch: `master`

- Schema: `GatewayBinding.scope = 'item'` (+ `limit`), `nested-binding.ts` (`withParent`, `readItemPath` with `^.` for the item above, `nestedGatewayList`, `nestedDataList`, `applyItemFilters`, `MAX_NESTED_DEPTH` 5); the Data bindings list ignores nested bindings. 12 tests.
- Renderer walker: a container with `scope: 'item'`, or `dataBinding` `parent` + `target: 'list'` + `field`, repeats over the list of the item it sits in (nothing fetched; one object = one record; no item = empty list); elements read relative fields and `^.field`; linked bindings work on nested lists. 7 tests (three levels, limit and filters, Data Object blocks).

- Editor: live canvas and Preview render nested lists (`scripts/nested-context.ts`, tested in the running editor with mocked data: three levels, one gateway call; Data Object blocks); binding an Integrations field to an element inside a list of the same response is saved relative automatically (`scripts/nested-binding-author.ts`, drag from the Gateway panel, its click, and the picker; 7 tests); Advanced > "Nested list" for Data Objects (`NestedListControl.vue`) and the data picker offers the fields of the items.

## API clients: write Data Objects as drafts (scope data:write, POST/PATCH /client/v1/data) — 2026-10-13
- Branch: `master`
- Core: scope `data:write:<Class | *>` (own scope, `data:read` does not write); new `gateway/client-write.ts` (`validateClientWrite`: strict validation against the Data Class, only `attributes` / `key` / `locale`, rich text cleaned, `writtenAssetIds`).
- Gateway (`client-router.ts`): `POST /client/v1/data/:class` creates a draft (`201`, `409` on a duplicate key), `PATCH /client/v1/data/:class/:id` merges into a draft (`409` if published, `412` with a stale `If-Match`); linked assets must be readable by the client; nothing publishes.
- Dashboard: read / write checkboxes per Data Class in the API client editor, with a "write all classes" toggle.
- Tested on the running stack with a temporary client (create, duplicate key, unknown field, `published` in the body, asset outside the scopes or unknown, draft invisible to `GET`, patch, stale and fresh `If-Match`, changing `key`, published object, malformed id, other class, wrong content type); the test object and client were deleted. 10 new unit tests (`client-write.test.ts`, scopes in `client-data.test.ts`).
- Docs: `communication/api-clients.md` (5.2, 6.3c, errors, limits), plan `client-access-hub.md` section 15.

Compared with the real plugin in Chromium (same initial and final states in four scenarios) and in the running editor.

- New effect `collapse` in the animation list (`animations.ts`): opens the element from nothing to its measured height when it scrolls into view, then returns to its natural size; the scroll observer no longer pauses an animation that already finished. Test added (17 in `animations.test.ts`).

## GSAP replaced by the Web Animations API — 2026-10-13
- Branch: `master`
- New `packages/sdk/renderer/src/animations.ts` (shared by the editor and every output; exported from `@whoobe-cms/renderer`): 19 effects, 14 eases, `createAnimation`, `previewAnimation`, `mountAnimation` (scroll into view with restart, or hover with reverse; cleanup; reduced motion). Tests `animations.test.ts` (16, eases against values measured on GSAP). Parity with GSAP checked in Chromium on all 19 effects and 10 eases (30 cases, largest deviation 0.3 px).
- Editor (`apps/whoobe-one-spike`): `scripts/gsap-actions.ts` renamed `animation-actions.ts` and rewritten on the module; `scripts/animations.js` exposes the name lists only; imports updated in the toolbar, the animation panel and the four preview components; `gsap` dependency removed, `@whoobe-cms/renderer` added. `pnpm-lock.yaml` updated.
- Outputs: `packages/sdk/react` uses the module instead of a dynamic import of `gsap`; `packages/sdk/vue` (directive `vWhoobeAnimation`) and `packages/sdk/svelte` (action `animate`) now play animations too (they did not before); `packages/publish` no longer adds `gsap` to generated Next.js sites.
- Schema: `trigger` added to `ElementGsap` and `elementGsapSchema` (the hover flag the editor writes was not in the schema).
- Fixes found on the way: the effect `growh-eight` could never play (now `grow-height`); the editor's default duration was effectively 0 (effect defaults were never applied), so animations without a set duration jumped; an empty duration now means 0.7 s. `rough`, `slow` and `steps` get a curve (they never worked).
- `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` regenerated (617 packages, GSAP gone) and the GSAP exception removed from `scripts/release/third-party-notices.py`; docs updated (plan `docs/plans/cms/replace-gsap.md` section 8, credits, supply chain, user manual, content guides).

## Analysis: replacing GSAP — 2026-10-13

Hooked into the licence gate of `scripts/release.sh`.

## Client access: start automations from an API client — 2026-10-13
- Branch: `master`
- New scope `automation:run:<endpoint>:<route | *>` (`client-scopes.ts`: `parseAutomationScope`, `automationScopeAllows`; `validateClientScopes` accepts it). Separate from `gateway:call`: neither allows the other's address. No prefix patterns.
- Gateway `client-router.ts`: `POST /client/v1/automations/:endpoint/*` calls the matched POST route (shared `callRoute`), waits (endpoint timeout, at most 30 s) and answers `{ ok, outcome?, response }`; `outcome` is validated with `extractOutcome` (same rule as form submissions); engine `5xx` answers `502` without the engine's body; out of scope, unknown endpoint, non-POST all `404`. Any other path under `/client/v1` now answers a JSON `404` instead of an HTML page.
- Dashboard `MasterApiClientTab.vue`: an *Automations (start a flow)* API in the select, listing the forms that submit to an endpoint (from pages and snippets, by form name, with endpoint and route); the list shows `RUN automations /<endpoint>/<route>`.

- 2026-10-13, step 2a: the `public` client kind is removed. `packages/core/drizzle/0057_api_clients_auth_mode.sql` (adds `auth_mode`, default `key`; any old `public` client is suspended; applied to the development database), `validateClientScopes` no longer depends on a kind, scopes of any method are valid. The gateway refuses a key sent with an `Origin` header (`403`, logged). `authMode` is `key` only for now (`jwt` answers 400). Dashboard: no kind selector, a note that keys are for servers. Docs updated (guide, threat model, API reference regenerated).
- 2026-10-13, steps 2b and 2c: identity-provider tokens for browsers and apps. `packages/core/src/gateway/client-jwt.ts` (registration, claim rules, `validateJwtConfig`), `apps/gateway/src/jwt-verify.ts` (signature against the provider's JWKS with a cache and rate-limited refresh, algorithm allow-list, `iss`, `aud`, required `exp` and `sub`, optional `azp`; discovery and keys through `safeFetch`), `client-router.ts` (a bearer that is not a `wbk_` key is a token; scopes from claim rules; `403` for a valid token without access; per-user rate limit; `sub` in the log), `client-admin-routes.ts` (create and update token clients, no keys for them), migration `0058_api_clients_jwt.sql` (`jwt_config`, applied to the development database), dependency `jose` 5. Dashboard: choice of key or token, issuer, audience, algorithms, authorized party, required claims and claim rules. Tests `scripts/security/unit/gateway-jwt.test.ts` (24, with generated keys) and a run against Keycloak 26 in Docker. Guide section 4.7 (Keycloak steps; Entra ID, Cognito and Auth0 values noted, not tested), threat model, API reference, authentication document.
- Phase 1 of `docs/plans/api-gateway/client-access-hub.md`. Decisions recorded in the plan (both client kinds, no end-user accounts in Whoobe and identity-provider tokens later, phase 1 first, Valkey for shared counters, dev and prod as two workspaces).
- Database: `packages/core/drizzle/0056_api_clients.sql` (expand only: `api_clients`, `api_client_keys`; applied to the development database), schema and records in `packages/core/src/db/api-clients-*.ts`. Keys `wbk_<8>_<secret>`, stored as HMAC-SHA-256 with `SITE_API_KEY_PEPPER`, several per client (rotation). `packages/core/src/gateway/client-keys.ts`, `client-scopes.ts` (scope language, route matching with templates, path checks). `packages/core/dist` rebuilt.
- Gateway: `client-router.ts` (`/client/v1/me`, `/client/v1/gateway/:endpoint/*`; key from `Authorization: Bearer` or `X-Whoobe-Key`; scopes checked against the matched route; uniform `404`; address list; per-second limit and daily quota; failed-key lock; one log line per call), `client-admin-routes.ts` (`/api/v1/gateway/clients`, `gateway:manage`), `route-call.ts` (the one place a route is called, shared with `/api/remote`), `client-ip.ts`.
- `/api/remote` now matches routes with `{param}` templates and passes the query string (it matched the exact path only before).
- Rate limiting: `limiter.ts` (sliding-window counter, in-process or Redis protocol with one atomic Lua script, fallback to memory with a warning and `degraded` in `GET /health`); `rate-limiter.ts` uses it, so `GATEWAY_RATE_LIMIT_BACKEND=redis` now works. New dependency `ioredis` (apps/gateway, `pnpm-lock.yaml`).
- Deployment: optional `valkey` service in `docker-compose.yml` (profile `ratelimit`, no port, password, no persistence); `GATEWAY_RATE_LIMIT_BACKEND` read from the env file; `.env.example`; `provision-workspace.sh` generates `VALKEY_PASSWORD` and `GATEWAY_REDIS_URL`; nginx template gets `location /client/v1/`.
- Dashboard: **API clients** page (`ApiClientsView.vue`, `gateway-api.ts`, nav in `DashboardLayout.vue`, `tabsStore.ts`): create with route picker, limits, addresses, expiry; key shown once; add, revoke, suspend, delete.
  - The *Allowed* column lists each scope as `METHOD endpoint /route`; clicking copies the full address an application calls (endpoint name URL-encoded), and the page header explains `<endpoint>` and `<route>`.
- Tests: `scripts/security/unit/client-keys-scopes.test.ts`, `gateway-limiter.test.ts` (the Redis part runs with `WB_TEST_REDIS_URL`; passed against a real Valkey 8 container). Verified live against the running gateway (key, scopes, tie routes, path tricks, `429`, revoke) and the dashboard in headless Chromium; also with the Valkey backend, including the fallback when it stops.
- Docs: Integrations guide section 4, API reference (`gen-api-docs.py`, regenerated), configuration reference, threat model, authentication document.

## [Gateway: the inferred response schema now describes all the items of an array, not only the first] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `master`
- `apps/gateway/src/schema-inferrer.ts`: an array was described by `value[0]` alone, so a field the first record lacks (or has as `null`) never reached the schema and the table column / field pickers did not offer it (e.g. `email` of the HubSpot contacts; typing the name by hand worked because the page reads the real response). Items are now merged: fields are the union of the keys (in order of first appearance), the type and sample come from the first record that has a value, a field missing or null in some record is `nullable`; nested objects and arrays are merged the same way; the first 500 items are read (a field that first appears after that is still not seen). Same output shape as before.
- Run over the schemas stored in the database: 2 of them were incomplete (HubSpot contacts missing `contacts.email` and 6 `attributes.*`; a files route missing `files.url/text/title`).
- Test: `scripts/security/unit/gateway-schema-inferrer.test.ts` (6); gateway typecheck clean.
- Schemas already stored keep the old shape until the route is tested again (Integrations > route > Test); nothing was rewritten in the database.

## [Generated Nuxt site: page no longer turns into "Page not found" after hydration] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `master`
- `packages/publish/src/cli.ts` (`nuxt.config.ts` template): `experimental.payloadExtraction: false`. With the default extracted `_payload.json` (prerendered by `crawlLinks`), the page showed for a second and then `pages/index.vue` threw its 404: on the client `payload.data` was empty during hydration (the file is served 200 and complete, no Nuxt warning; cause inside Nuxt 3.21.11 not found), `useAsyncData` stayed `idle` and `data.value` was null. Reproduced in headless Chromium on two builds of the same site: without the flag it ends on the error page, with the flag the page stays. Data is inline in the HTML now (bigger HTML, no extra request).
- `packages/publish/dist` rebuilt. Regenerate the site and re-run `deploy.sh`.
- Not fixed here: the service worker precache fails with `bad-precaching-response` for `/safelist` (404; the file is `public/safelist.html`), which only breaks the SW install.

## [Renderer: a failing gateway binding on a container no longer replaces the page with the error screen] — 2026-10-12

A gateway 401 (site without a site key) or an unreachable gateway threw out of the whole walk, and the generated Nuxt site showed its `error.vue` ("You're offline") right after hydration. The container now logs a warning and renders its children unbound. Typecheck and the 76 renderer tests pass; no new test yet. Regenerate the site to pick it up (the SDK is vendored as tarballs).

## [Generated site Dockerfile: the container runs pnpm 9, not the latest pnpm] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `master`
- `packages/publish/src/cli.ts` (`scaffoldDockerfile`): `corepack prepare pnpm@9.0.0` cached pnpm under root's home, but the run stage switches to `USER 1000`, so corepack fetched the latest pnpm there, which ignores `package.json` `pnpm.overrides` and made `pnpm start` crash-loop with `ERR_PNPM_VERIFY_DEPS_BEFORE_RUN`. `COREPACK_HOME=/opt/corepack` (world-readable) now holds the prepared pnpm 9. Regenerate the site and re-run `deploy.sh` to get it.

- Nuxt `nitro.prerender.failOnError: false`: a content link to a page that is not published (here `/sycore`, 404 from the CMS) is logged but no longer stops `pnpm build` (and the Docker build).

## [Slider rendered for real (React and Vue), and the home page gets its title and meta tags] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Page "aaaa" on the Next site: the slider showed unlabelled tabs, empty slides and arrows that its settings did not ask for, and the tab title / meta tags were missing. Causes (found in the code, then checked in `next dev` with Playwright):
  - **React slider** rendered each slide with `registry[kind]` and no children (so every slide was empty), left the tab buttons without text, and showed the arrows in tabs mode. **Vue** had the labels and the home button missing. Both now follow the editor's preview: the slides are the node's children, tabs are named from each slide's `tabLabel` (else "Slide N"), tabs mode shows only the tabs, standard mode the arrows (`arrowIcon` glyph) and the home button (`showHome`) over the viewport, dots when `showDots`. What is shown is decided once in the new `packages/sdk/renderer/src/slider.ts` (`sliderView`, with the editor's defaults), used by both adapters; test `slider.test.ts` (4) and two SSR tests in `react/components.test.tsx`. `SLIDER_DEFAULT_CLASSES` (the classes the generated site's CSS must contain) now lists the new defaults and the test reads `slider.ts`.
  - **Schema**: `autoplay`, `interval`, `loop`, `showNav`, `showHome` of a slider were required, so a slider saved before one of them existed made the WHOLE page fail validation (blank page). Optional now (`schema/validators.ts`, `node.ts`); the editor falls back to defaults the same way.
  - **Title and meta**: the Next home page (slug `/`) and the locale root page had no `generateMetadata` (only the catch-all had), and the layout is a client component, so the tab had no title. Both export it now. `generateMetadataFromPage` treated an empty `metaTitle` / description as set (`??`); `||` now. The Nuxt pages only set the title: they also set description, Open Graph title/description/image, robots and canonical link now (`useSeoMeta`).
  - Checked on a regenerated Next site: title `aaaa`, `og:title`, the slider with its four named tabs, no arrows, slide texts present, no page errors. Not tried: clicking the tabs, Nuxt in a browser, `next build`. Regenerate and `pnpm install` to get it.

## [Generated Next site: Alpine starts, and the page hydrates (React error #418)] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Two reports on the Next site: Alpine directives did nothing, and the console showed "Minified React error #418" (hydration mismatch). Reproduced in `next dev` (full messages) with Playwright against a copy of the generated site; every cause below was seen there:
  - **Alpine was never started on Next** (only on Nuxt). New client component `src/lib/AlpineStarter.tsx` in the generated site (mounted in the layout; starts Alpine and the Collapse plugin after hydration, never before, since Alpine rewrites the DOM); deps `alpinejs`, `@alpinejs/collapse`; `src/lib/alpine.d.ts` for the types. Checked in the browser: `Alpine` 3.17, `x-data` initialised, `x-show` hidden, no page errors.
  - **`<a>` inside `<a>`** (a linked container holding another link): the HTML parser splits it on the server, React cannot hydrate it. `walk.ts` drops an inner link inside a linked element (the outer keeps the area clickable). Applies to all adapters.
  - **`<p>` holding `<p>`** (a paragraph whose rich text already has `<p>`/lists): same split. `walk.ts` renders such a text in a `div`.
  - **HTML attribute names in React**: `for`, `playsinline`, `tabindex`, ... were passed as written ("Invalid DOM property `for`", boolean attributes like `disabled=""` read as false). `packages/sdk/react/src/style.ts` `baseProps` maps them to React props, coerces boolean attributes, and merges a `class`/`style` attribute with the descriptor's. Test `style.test.ts` (3).
  - **Alpine shorthands** `@click` / `:class` cannot be attribute names (the DOM refuses `@`, React drops it): `buildAttributes` writes `x-on:click` / `x-bind:class` (all adapters).
  - Tests: renderer 72 (3 new), react 16, vue/svelte/next/nuxt pass. After the fixes the home page loads in `next dev` with no hydration error or page error; the only remaining console line was the table's 401 (no site key in that test). Not tried: `next build` + `next start`, the other pages of the site, and a click on an Alpine-driven control.
- Left as is: `semantic: "hero"` on three containers renders `<hero>` (the editor does the same); React warns "tag unrecognized" in dev only. Rebuild/regenerate the site (`pnpm install` too) to get all of this.

- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. With `pnpm dev` a modal "You are offline" appeared 1-2 s after the page loaded. ## [Published site: container background images, and the video source really reaches the page] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. (1) A container's or leaf's editor-set background image (`image.url`, drawn as `background-image` by the editor and the mail renderer) was dropped by the SDK: only the `background-color` of `style` survived. `packages/sdk/renderer/src/walk.ts` now puts `background-image:url(<DAM address>)` first in the node's style (the node's own `style` after it, as in the editor), for every element except img, video, audio and Download, whose `image.url` is their source. The address is only used if it is CSS-safe (no quotes, parentheses, semicolons, backslashes, angle brackets or spaces; `data:` only for base64 raster images), so an editor value cannot close the `url()` or add declarations. (2) The empty video `src`: the previous fix was not in what the site had been built with (the adapters' `dist` and the vendored tarballs); all `packages/sdk/*` rebuilt. Checked by running the real home page through `walkTree` against the dev API: the video resolves to `http://localhost:3002/dam-asset/video/public/…mp4` and the container gets `background-image:url(http://localhost:3002/dam-asset/images/ai/…png)`. Tests: renderer 69 (2 new), adapters pass. Not tried: the generated site in a browser; generate it again to pick up the rebuilt SDK.

## [Published site: a video gets its DAM address (Alpine "no supported sources")] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. In Preview a page was fine; the published site raised `NotSupportedError: The element has no supported sources` from an Alpine `x-effect` calling `$refs.vid.play()`. Cause (checked in the page content and the renderer): the SDK resolved a DAM path to its address for `img`, `audio` and Download only, so the `<video>` kept `/video/public/….mp4`, relative to the site, which does not exist there (the asset processor answers 206 for the full address). `packages/sdk/renderer/src/walk.ts` now resolves `video` too, and the Vue, React and Svelte video elements use that address (`image.url`, then `node.src`). Test added in `walk.test.ts`; adapter tests pass. Not tried: the published site in a browser. The generated site carries the SDK as vendored tarballs: generate it again (Run from here) to get the fix.

## [Publish from the dashboard: the gateway site key travels with the run (bound tables were empty)] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. A table bound to the gateway showed 0 records on a site generated from "Run from here". Cause (checked): the website has a gateway site key, so `/api/v1/gateway/resolve` answers 401 "Site authentication required" to every binding (`apps/gateway/src/site-auth.ts`), and the generated site had no key (phase 1 did not send it; the renderer swallows the error). Now `POST /publish/jobs` accepts `siteApiKey` (checked with the generator's rule), stored in `publish_jobs.site_key_enc` (migration `0055`, encrypted when `INTEGRATION_SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is set), returned once to the runner at claim and erased in the same statement (also on cancel); the runner gives it to the generator in `WHOOBE_SITE_API_KEY` (new fallback in `whoobe-publish`, no key in the process arguments) and masks it in the log. The tab sends the key when it is shown (right after generating one) and warns, when the pages use the gateway and a key exists but is not shown, that the key must be regenerated first.
- Tests: `publish-runner.test.ts` now 14 (key by environment only, not in the log; key kept out of the stored options). Not tried: a run with the real key from the browser. To fix the site you have: Publish tab > regenerate the gateway site key > Run from here (the old key stops working, so the site on another server needs the new one).

A `publish_jobs` row from the test run (slug `e2e-test`) is left in the dev database.

## [whoobe-publish --clean: start from an empty output folder] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Publishing again into the same folder only overwrote the files it writes, so pages removed in the CMS and the `.nuxt`/`.next` caches stayed. New `--clean` (`packages/publish/bin/whoobe-publish.js`, `cleanOutDir()` in `src/cli.ts`, used by the Next and Nuxt generators after the CMS data was fetched and before anything is written; `publish-site.sh --clean` / `PUBLISH_CLEAN=1`; docs sites ignore it, they already prune). Keeps `node_modules`, `.git`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `.pnpm-store`. Refuses a non-empty folder without `package.json` and `vendor/whoobe-cms-*.tgz` and deletes nothing. Off by default. Security unit test `publish-clean.test.ts` (3 tests) passes; the publish package builds. Not run: a full generation with `--clean` against the live CMS. The Publish tab does not offer it yet. Guide `website-publishing.md` updated.

## [Publish: the asset base URL defaults to the asset processor, not the CMS API] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Images of a generated site returned 404 (`http://localhost:3003/dam-asset/...`): `/dam-asset/*` is served by the asset processor (3002 in dev), the CMS API (3003) has no such route. The Publish tab proposed the API URL as "Asset Base URL" (`views/PublishView.vue`: now `defaultServiceUrl(3002)`; behind nginx the host is the same, so production is unchanged) and `publish-site.sh` did the same (`:3003` becomes `:3002` in the proposed value). Already generated sites need `NEXT_PUBLIC_ASSET_BASE_URL` / `NUXT_PUBLIC_CMS_ASSET_BASE_URL` set to `http://localhost:3002` (or a regeneration). Checked with curl: 3002 answers 200 for the asset, 3003 and 3001 answer 404. Not tried in a browser.

## [Publish checks: no false alarms for forms and repeater fields; the output folder can be anywhere] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Running the generated command stopped with "Gateway bindings do not resolve" for a binding that was fine. Found: (1) the binding was a **form's submit binding**; `/api/v1/gateway/resolve` only knows read bindings and answers 404, so forms are no longer checked there (`scanGatewayUsage`, `scripts/publish-preflight.mjs`); (2) the "6 bindings not tied to a route" were **fields of a repeated container** (only a `fieldPath`), indistinguishable from a broken binding in the public content (the endpoint name is stripped), so a `gatewayBinding` without `bindingId` is no longer counted; only a form that names an endpoint but has no binding id is reported; (3) a 401/403 without the site key is reported as "the gateway asks for the site key" (exit code 4 of `check`), not as a failed binding.
- `whoobe-publish` found the monorepo (whose SDK packages are vendored into the site) as `<outDir>/../..`, right only for `./sites/<slug>` inside the repo; with any other `--out-dir` it failed with `spawnSync pnpm ENOENT`. New `defaultMonorepoRoot()` finds it from where the generator lives (fallback: the old guess). `packages/publish/src/cli.ts`.
- Tests: schema 5, security unit `publish-preflight.test.ts` 10 (usage, candidates, parity, monorepo root). Tried: the exact command from the Publish tab, out dir outside the repo, generated a Nuxt site with `NUXT_PUBLIC_GATEWAY_URL=http://localhost:3001` and the four vendored packages. Not tried: `pnpm install` / `pnpm dev` of the generated site.

## [Publish tab: the generated script no longer breaks on the CDN placeholders] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Pasted in a terminal, the script failed at step 1 (`-bash: service-account-email: No such file or directory`): the unquoted `<service-account-email>` placeholders were read by bash as redirections. Step 1 now reads `WHOOBE_SYNC_EMAIL` / `WHOOBE_SYNC_PASSWORD` from the environment (`export` them first) and stops with a message when they are missing; the remote-deploy step is off by default (its placeholders would make `rsync` fail). `views/PublishView.vue`. Checked that the new lines are valid bash; the rest of the script was not run end to end.

## [Dashboard: Publish is a main tab, with the gateway checks] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Phase 0 of `docs/plans/website/publish-from-dashboard.md`. The Publish modal (`WebsitePublishModal.vue`, removed) is now a main tab: new section `publish` (sidebar, group Content; `tabsStore.openPublish(websiteId)`, used by "Publish" in the Websites list), `views/PublishView.vue`. It lists the websites (documentation sites keep their own Publish tab), shows **checks** (the published pages' use of the API Gateway, whether a gateway answers at the URL, bindings never tied to a route, whether every binding resolves; "Try http://host:3001" buttons when the URL is the CMS API or nothing answers) and builds the command, now `./publish-site.sh --yes ...` with the site key in `PUBLISH_SITE_KEY` instead of an argument.
- The checks run in the browser (`utils/publish-check.ts`), so a gateway URL typed by the user is never fetched by the server (no SSRF); CORS of the gateway allows it. Shared rules `scanGatewayUsage` and `gatewayCandidates` in `@whoobe-cms/schema` (`publish-preflight.ts`, 5 tests); `scripts/publish-preflight.mjs` keeps its own copy and a parity test compares the two (security unit test, 9 tests).
- `vue-tsc` passes; the module compiles in the running dev server. **Not tried in a browser** (Chrome for Playwright is not installed here, and the dashboard needs a login). Guide and plan updated.

## [publish-site.sh: pick the website from a list, ask for the gateway only when it is used] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Found on a generated site whose bound table showed headers and "No data": the site had been generated with the CMS API (port 3003) as gateway URL, where `/api/v1/gateway/resolve` does not exist; the gateway is on 3001. `publish-site.sh` defaulted the gateway to the API URL and the renderer swallowed the failed call.
- `publish-site.sh` rewritten: numbered list of the websites (`GET /public/websites`) or a pasted UUID, validated against the list; the pages are scanned and the gateway URL / site key are asked only if they hold gateway bindings or gateway forms; the gateway is proposed only if one answers (`http://<api host>:3001`, then the API host), probed again on the typed value, and every `bindingId` is resolved against it before generating (stops with the failing ids, bindings never tied to a route are reported); the API URL is checked first; flags / `PUBLISH_*` variables for every answer and `--yes` for non-interactive use (`--help`). The site key is read without echo.
- New `scripts/publish-preflight.mjs` (sites, scan, probe, suggest, check) holds the network work. `packages/sdk/renderer/src/table.ts`: a table whose data cannot be loaded, or whose gateway binding has no `bindingId`, logs a warning on the server instead of failing silently.
- Tests: `scripts/security/unit/publish-preflight.test.ts` (7), renderer 67 green. Tried against the running stack: it listed six websites, found 19 gateway usages on the default site with 6 unpinned bindings and one that does not resolve (404). Not tried: a full generation through the new prompts; `shellcheck` is not installed here (`bash -n` passes). `guides/publish/website-publishing.md` updated.

## [SDK: the Table element is drawn by the React/Next, Vue/Nuxt and Svelte adapters (phase 4)] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Phase 4 of `docs/plans/cms/table-element-sdk-plan.md`: generated sites and the headless SDK now draw the Table (a typed grid, or a bound list with columns, formats, classes, pagination, sorting, search, row counter, empty text). The server renders the first page; the controls work after hydration. New: `react/src/table.tsx` (`DefaultTable`, `useTableView`) and `react/src/style.ts` (`baseProps`, `cssToReactStyle` moved there, still exported from defaults), `vue/src/table.ts` (`DefaultTable`, `useTableView(() => bound)`), `svelte/src/lib/WhoobeTable.svelte`; registry key `table` in the three default registries; option `tableMaxRows` in the three adapters and in `getPageData` of `@whoobe-cms/next` and `@whoobe-cms/nuxt`.
- The state machine of the controls is shared: `initialTableState`, `reduceTableState`, `TableViewAction` in `@whoobe-cms/schema` (`table-view.ts`); a note "Showing the first N of M rows" appears when the list was cut at the cap (1,000).
- Tests: schema 86 (5 for the state machine), React 13 (7 new), Vue 14 (7 new), Svelte 9 (6 new), renderer 67; all server-rendered (first page, counter, pager, `aria-sort` buttons, search box, More button, empty text, cap note, hostile cell content inert, a registry override). `svelte-check` 0 errors. Not tried: a real generated site in a browser; GSAP is not applied to a table in the SDK.
- Docs: `guides/headless-sdk.md` (the Table element section, the `table` kind), `guides/table-element.md` (limits rewritten: generated sites draw the table), `guides/publish/website-publishing.md` (what the safelist covers), release notes.

## [Table: one controller for the editor and the SDK (phase 3)] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Phase 3 of `docs/plans/cms/table-element-sdk-plan.md`. New `packages/sdk/schema/src/table-view.ts`: `buildTableRows` (one pre-formatted row per record: text or checked link, raw sort key, normalised search text) and `computeTableView` (search, then sort, then page, plus the counter text, `needsControls`, the applied sort), `ariaSortOf`, `sortMarkOf`. The editor's `useBoundTable.ts` is rewritten on top of them (it keeps only the state: search words, sort, page, rows revealed); the SDK descriptor builder (`renderer/src/table.ts`) calls the same `buildTableRows`. So a table cannot behave differently in the editor and in a generated site. Placed in the schema package, not the renderer as the plan first said, because the editor depends on the schema only.
- Editor components: the cell shape is now `{ text, href?, target?, className?, cellClass? }`. Behaviour unchanged, checked state by state with a render of both components (17 checks: pages and counter, sort asc / desc / off, unsortable column, accents in the search, column left out of the search, switches off, link and label cells).
- Tests: `table-view.test.ts` (12, schema), a parity test in `renderer/src/table.test.ts` (descriptor rows and views equal the direct call for four states), the security unit tests updated to the new structure; schema 81, renderer 67, unit 323 green.

## [SDK: the Table element has a kind and a descriptor (phase 2)] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Phase 2 of `docs/plans/cms/table-element-sdk-plan.md`. The SDK used to turn a Table into an empty `<table>`. Now: `ElementTable`, `TableCell`, `TableColumnConfig` and tolerant Zod schemas (`tableSchema` etc.) in `@whoobe-cms/schema`, wired into the node schema (a wrong type fails page validation, unknown fields are kept); `normalizeKind` returns `table`; `RenderDescriptor.table` (new `packages/sdk/renderer/src/table.ts`, `buildTableDescriptor`): a typed grid with sanitised cell HTML and merged cells dropped, or, for a table with a `dataBinding` (Data Class query) / `gatewayBinding` (array), pre-formatted rows (text or checked link per cell, raw sort key, normalised search text), the columns (configured, or the scalar fields of the first record), the options of sorting / search / counter, the pagination settings, `totalRows` and `truncated`. Cap: `RenderOptions.tableMaxRows`, default 1,000 (decision D4). A failing API gives an empty bound table, not a broken page.
- Fix in the walker: a table's `gatewayBinding` was also resolved as the element's text content (a second gateway call); skipped for tables.
- Tests: `renderer/src/table.test.ts` (17: kind, typed grid, hostile HTML, missing parts, Data Class rows / columns / sort keys / search text, detected columns, formats number / link / label with classes and an unsafe address, the cap, empty list, failing API, single-field binding, prototype keys, JSON round trip, gateway rows with credentials, unsaved binding), `schema/src/table-schema.test.ts` (4); all SDK packages (renderer 66, schema 69, react, vue, svelte, next, nuxt) green. Classes of "map value to label/class" formats are now collected for the generator's safelist.
- No adapter draws it yet (phases 3 and 4).

## [Generated sites: the CSS safelist learns every class field] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. Phase 1 of `docs/plans/cms/table-element-sdk-plan.md`, decisions D1-D6 recorded there (pre-formatted rows; React/Next, Vue/Nuxt, Svelte; page 1 only in the HTML; cap 1,000 rows; class fix first; repeater pagination after the table adapters). The generator built its Tailwind safelist from `css.css` and `css.container` only, so classes kept anywhere else were missing from the CSS of a generated site. - A test reads the node types and fails when a `*Class` property is added without being listed; it found four slider fields no generator collected (`tabContainerClass`, `dotClass`, `activeDotClass`, `dotContainerClass`), so sliders also lacked those classes. A second test checks the slider defaults against the React and Vue sources.

## [Table element: row counter] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. A bound table can show "Showing 11–20 of 87" under the rows (**Row count** in the Columns panel; `ElementTable.showCount`, `countText` with `{from} {to} {total} {all}`, `countClass`). It follows the page, the search and the "More" button, says "(filtered from N)" after a search and "No records" when empty; the text is interpolated, never `v-html`. The `tfoot` is drawn when there is a counter or pagination controls; in lazyload mode the counter replaces the small "n / total" figure.
- Rules in `table-binding.ts` (`rowRange`, `formatRowCount`, the default texts); `useBoundTable.ts` exposes `countLabel`, `showCount`, `hasFooter`. Tests: 5 more in the schema package, 1 in the security unit test; a render of both components checked each state (no pages, each page and the short last one, after a search, no match, lazyload before and after "More", empty list, custom text escaped). **Not tried in a browser.** Guide updated.

## [Table element: sorting by column and search] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding`. A bound table can let the visitor sort by a header (click: ascending, descending, off; `aria-sort`, an arrow) and search the list. Switches in the Columns panel: **Sortable columns**, **Search box** (placeholder and classes), and per column **sort** / **search** (`TableBoundColumn.sortable` / `searchable`, `ElementTable.sortable` / `searchable` / `searchPlaceholder` / `searchClass`). The search box sits in the table's `<caption>` (the element keeps one root).
- Rules in `packages/sdk/schema/src/table-binding.ts`: `nextSort`, `compareCellValues` (numbers as numbers, natural order for text, case and accents ignored), `sortRecords` (stable, empty values last, never the prototype chain), `normalizeSearchText`, `searchTerms` (all words, at most 8), `matchesTerms`. `useBoundTable.ts` applies search, then sort, then the pages, over the whole list; the page counter, "n / total" and the pagination controls follow the result; with no match the table says "No matching rows". A sort on a column that was removed or switched off is ignored.
- Tests: 7 more in `table-binding.test.ts` (schema), 1 in the security unit test; a render of the component with data checked the order, accents ("caffe" finds "Caffè"), the AND of words, the columns left out of the search, and that switching the options off ignores a typed query / a sort. **Not tried in a browser.** Guide `table-element.md` updated.

## [Table element: data binding and pagination] — 2026-10-12
- Branch: `feat/table-data-binding` (from `master`). A Table can now be bound to a list: the element's own `dataBinding` (a Data Class query) or `gatewayBinding` (an array in a gateway reply), already offered by the toolbar buttons, was resolved but ignored by the table components. New `table.columns` (header, field or path, format, classes) and `table.emptyText`; one row per record; `Fill from data` proposes the columns; per-column format uses the existing transformers (date, number, truncate, link) with their default options. Pagination: standard (Prev / Next) or lazyload (More) over the resolved list, in the browser.
- Pure rules in `packages/sdk/schema/src/table-binding.ts` (`tableCellValue` reads an attribute or a path and never the prototype chain, `columnsFromRecords`, `paginationOf`, `pageRows`, `pageCount`); the reactive part in `whoobe-one-spike/src/composables/useBoundTable.ts`, used by `BlockTable.vue` (canvas, with a Columns panel) and `preview/BlockTablePreview.vue` (rows are interpolated text or a checked link, never `v-html`; the pagination sits in the table's `tfoot`, so the element keeps a single root). A bound table has no static footer.
- `GatewayBinding` gets the pagination fields (`rowsPerPage`, `paginationMode`, `loadMoreText`, `loadMoreClass`, `paginationContainerClass`) in the schema (`node.ts`, `validators.ts`), `ElementsClass.d.ts`, `GatewayBinding.d.ts` and a Pagination section in `GatewayBindingPicker.vue`; `BlockContainer.vue` and `BlockContainerPreview.vue` now read the pagination from the gateway binding too (before: only from a Data Class binding).
- Columns panel, second round: **Fill from data** replaced the columns with every field, losing the author's choices. It is now **Choose fields** (a check box per field found in the first record, the last column cannot be unchecked) and **Add missing fields** (only appends; headers, formats, classes and order are kept); the panel can be folded; each column has **Cell classes** and **Header classes** (Tailwind, applied on top of the table's own). The rules are pure functions in `table-binding.ts` (`toggleColumnField`, `missingColumnFields`, `appendMissingColumns`); "+ column" on a table with no column set no longer drops the fields that were shown.
- Tests: `table-binding.test.ts` in the schema package (14 + 3 for editing the columns) and `scripts/security/unit/table-binding.test.ts` (5: public content keeps the pagination and the columns, the schema accepts / refuses it, the components); type-check and a build of the editor clean. **Not tried in a browser.** Guide `docs/release/guides/table-element.md`, release notes, README. **Known gap:** the Table element is not rendered by the SDK or the generated Next.js / Nuxt sites (it never was); written in the guide.

## [Script: toggle the local automation test containers] — 2026-10-11
- Branch: `feat/form-result-event`. New `scripts/automation-test.sh [activepieces|n8n]`: stops the container(s) if running, otherwise starts them (creating network, volume and containers when missing). `activepieces` also manages `whoobe-smtp-forward` (socat 2525 -> Mailhog, `MAILHOG_ADDR` to change), because the Activepieces SMTP piece only offers ports 25/465/587/2525. Secrets are read from `~/.config/whoobe/activepieces.env` (outside the repo, mode 600).

Both are now read again every time the form settings modal opens (`loadEndpoints`, routes cache cleared).

## [Forms: result event and outcome key from the Gateway service (prototype)] — 2026-10-10
- Branch: `feat/form-result-event` (from `docs/marketing-automation-plan`). A form now tells the page how a submission ended: it dispatches a bubbling DOM event `whoobe-form-result` with `{ ok, outcome }` on its `<form>`, from the editor preview and from the Vue/Nuxt, React/Next and Svelte runtimes (success and failure). The page reacts with the AlpineJS directives every element already has. Guide: `docs/plans/automations/form-result-event.md`.
- Outcome: a Gateway form's service (an n8n workflow) can answer `{ "outcome": "qualified" }`. `apps/gateway/src/submit-outcome.ts` (`extractOutcome`) accepts only a plain key (1-32 of letters, digits, `_`, `-`) and `submit-handler.ts` adds it to its reply; `ApiClient.submitForm` (`@whoobe-cms/renderer`) returns `{ success, outcome? }` and checks the key again. Nothing else of the service's reply reaches the page. **The Gateway still also returns the whole body as `response`** (unchanged; dropping it is a decision in the plan).
- New `form-result.ts` in the renderer (`FORM_RESULT_EVENT`, `sanitizeOutcome`, `dispatchFormResult`, `showsResultMessage`) and an editor-side copy in `whoobe-one-spike/src/scripts/`. Optional `formConfig.showResultMessage` (absent = true) in the schema, the editor types and the form settings (*Show the message popup*), so a page that shows its own blocks can turn the popup off.
- Tests: renderer `form-result.test.ts` and two new `api-client` tests (52 renderer tests), unit `form-result-event.test.ts` (14: extraction, same key pattern in the three copies, all six runtimes dispatch and honour the option, schema). Vue 7, React 6, Svelte 3 existing tests and `vue-tsc` (editor) / `tsc` (schema, renderer, vue, react) / `svelte-check` pass. **Not tried in a browser yet** (checklist in the guide, section 7).
