## [Editor, Isometric: connection ports on all four sides] — 2026-10-10
- Branch: `feat/isometric-ports-all-sides` (from `master`). Every box of an Isometric canvas now has a port on the top and bottom sides as well as left and right, and any port can start or receive a cable (before: one IN on the left, one OUT on the right).
- New `apps/whoobe-one-spike/src/isometric-ports.ts`: port positions, cable path and arrow head, shared by `BlockIsometric.vue` (editor) and `preview/BlockIsometricPreview.vue` (preview and published pages), which used to carry their own copy of the bezier. Boxes now also track their measured height (ResizeObserver) for the bottom port.
- Data: `IsometricEdge` gets optional `fromSide` / `toSide` (`ElementsClass.d.ts`). Connections saved before have neither and read as right -> left: their cable is byte-for-byte the old horizontal bezier (tested). Dragging between two ports of the same box is ignored, as before; the same pair of ports is not connected twice.
- Visible change for existing pages: **every cable now ends with a small arrow head** pointing into the box it enters (colour and size follow the connection's colour/thickness), also in the preview and in published sites.
- Fix after the owner's first look: the arrow tips floated ~8 px off the box side. The tip is now exactly on the side (the port position); in the editor the arrow heads are drawn in their own layer above the boxes and ports (the port circle is centred on that side and covered them), in the preview they are drawn with the cables.
- Test: `unit/isometric-ports.test.ts` (7). `vue-tsc --noEmit` clean. Not looked at in a browser yet.

## [MinIO removed from the repository; dev data moved to Garage] — 2026-10-10
- Branch: `chore/remove-minio`. Garage is already the default and the only store the tests and Oracle use; MinIO (upstream no longer publishes images, F-048/F-050) was kept only as an opt-in overlay. Trigger: the development stack came up after a Docker restart with the Garage default while `.env` still held MinIO-format keys (Garage crash-loop) and the stopped `whoobe_minio` left the documentation sites empty.
- Removed: `docker-compose.minio.yml`, `docker/minio/`, the `S3_BACKEND=minio` switches in `scripts/security/stack.sh` (build/up/down simplified), the console port in `gen-env.sh`, the MinIO advisory entry in `run-trivy-image.sh` and the golang/`/docker/minio` entries in `.github/dependabot.yml`, the "MinIO overlay" test in `scripts/security/unit/compose-hardening.test.ts` (the no-well-known-credentials checks stay; 10 tests green). UI/comment wording "S3/MinIO" -> "S3" (admin DAM screens, asset-processor, core, migration tool). The `MINIO_*` environment aliases are still read as fallbacks (compatibility, not documented as a feature).
- Docs (`docs/release`): storage-backends (MinIO section removed, "not supported" row), creating-workspaces (legacy MinIO workspaces must move to Garage), configuration-reference, deployment + miniguide (bare-metal guides now offer Garage as a native systemd service (single node, loopback only; binary URL checked, checksum of the arm64 v2.4.1 download recorded; commands for layout/key/bucket mirror `docker/garage/entrypoint.sh`) or OCI Object Storage, instead of a MinIO service; **the native Garage steps were not run on a VM**), architecture overview, asset guide, publishing guide, backup/encryption, release notes, status summary, supply chain, security test suite summary, findings F-048/F-050 (removed 2026-10-10), API reference regenerated. `docs/plans/*` and old notes keep their historical mentions.
- Data (owner's dev machine, not in the repo): bucket `whoobe-cms` copied from the MinIO container to Garage with `mc mirror` (599 objects, 137 MiB, `mc diff` empty), `.env` S3 keys replaced with Garage-format keys (backup of the old `.env` in `~/backups/dotenv-dev-2026-10-10.bak`), `whoobe_minio` stopped without restart policy; the `whoobe_cms_miniodata` volume is kept on purpose. The owner checked the documentation sites afterwards.

## [Function-level permissions enforced on the content routes (F-058)] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/granular-permissions`. Decision: the model is about **functions**, not divisions or sites (one workspace per organisation; a division that must not see a site gets another instance and uses export/import).
- `packages/core/src/auth/express-guards.ts`: `requireAnyPermission(<function key>, <broad key>)` - the route passes with the function key (`pages:edit`) **or** the broad key that always covered it (`objects:write`), so no existing role loses access and a role built from function keys is limited to those functions.
- `apps/core-api/src/server.ts`: 90 of the 172 permission-guarded routes mapped by the request (list / view / create / edit / delete) to pages (21, incl. the page templates gallery), websites (21, incl. menus, themes catalogue, site key, export/import), data objects (18), snippets and kits (18), themes (6), components (6). 82 routes stay on the broad keys on purpose (settings, mail, AI, imports, users, defining data classes, search reindex).
- Contract and tests: `routes.expected.json` records `mode: any` and both keys for these routes; `extract-routes.ts`, `lib/routes.ts`, `route-drift` and `authz-matrix` understand any-of; new `api/granular-permissions.test.ts` (182 tests: creates one role+user per function key; the owner of a route's key passes, users with another action of the area or a key of another area get 403). Suites: unit + API 1635, browser 30.
- Docs: `architecture/authentication-and-authorization.md` (permission model rewritten: the two levels, the mapping rule, the six areas, what stays broad, how to build a restricted role), findings F-058 fixed, status summary, threat model, release notes (behaviour change), test suite summary (1359 API tests, 2033 in total).

## [Documentation: deployment model stated explicitly (one organisation per instance)] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `docs/deployment-model`. Decision: Whoobe is not a shared multi-tenant platform; divisions of one entity share a workspace (roles), different entities get their own workspace.
- `architecture/architecture-overview.md` section 7 now states the rule and why; `security/threat-model.md` assumption 1 says deployments outside the model are not covered and the CSP residual risk is reworded; the "SaaS mode" wording in `status-summary.md`, `release-notes-0.1.0.md`, `security-test-suite.md` and `security-ci-for-forks.md` and the "multi-tenant" wording in the configuration reference are aligned; `docs/release/README.md` carries the rule at the top; the authentication page says roles describe function, not division, and that per-website permissions do not exist.

## [Security fixes: F-056 sessions, F-057 database password] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `fix/sessions-and-db-password`.
- **F-056**: `sessions.id` now stores `sha256(cookie value)` (`hashSessionToken` in `auth/session.ts`); the token is never written to the database, and the stored hash cannot be replayed as a cookie. Expired or revoked sessions older than a week are deleted at core-api start and every six hours (`purgeStaleSessions`). Migration `0053_sessions_hashed` deletes the old rows (data change only, no schema change). **Behaviour change: everyone logs in once more after the upgrade.**
- **F-057**: `resolveDbPassword` - the `postgrespassword` default exists only when `NODE_ENV` is `development` or `test`; anywhere else a missing `DB_PASSWORD` stops the service at start with a clear message. Compose workspaces already set it.
- Tests: `auth/session.integration.test.ts` (5, on a disposable database: only the hash is stored, the token validates and the hash does not, logout and revoke-all, a pre-change raw id no longer validates, purge keeps recent rows), `db/client.test.ts` (+3), and the existing `auth`/`mfa` API suites.
- Docs: findings F-056/F-057 fixed, status summary, release notes (behaviour changes), threat model, authentication, data and storage, encryption, data protection, observability, configuration reference, deployment note.

New `docs/release/security/test-suite-summary.md`: six boxes (API and authorization, browser and content, static analysis, logic and unit, workspace isolation, DAST) with the counts taken on 2026-10-09, how each number was obtained, and what the claims do and do not mean (the route-contract tests use a text scan, not an AST). Linked from `docs/release/README.md`.

## [Security fixes from the documentation review: F-059 (High), F-055, F-058] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `fix/credentials-and-roles`. Request: fix the credential storage and the default roles found by the review.
- **F-059 (High, found while verifying F-055)**: the OneSignal REST API key saved in a website's PWA configuration was returned by the unauthenticated `GET /public/apps/:websiteId` and, to every `objects:read` user, by `GET /whoobe-one/websites[/:id]` (reproduced). Now the website record never carries the key (`websites-records.ts`: `redactPwaConfig`; records show `push.onesignalApiKeySet`), the public route also drops the flag, the key is stored encrypted, a save without the key (or a blank one) keeps the stored one, `clearOnesignalApiKey: true` removes it, and the push send route reads it separately (`getWebsitePushApiKey`). Dashboard: the key field shows "saved - leave blank to keep it". **Operators: rotate a push key that was saved on an internet-reachable installation.**
- **F-055**: AI provider keys (text, image, translation), the Figma token and the push key are encrypted with AES-256-GCM (`INTEGRATION_SECRETS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `enc:v1:`), in the records layer (callers unchanged), with a startup backfill (`backfill-integration-secrets-encryption.ts`, idempotent, no-op without the key). Without the key behaviour is as before plus a one-time warning (an existing installation keeps working). New variable in `.env.example` (commented), `provision-workspace.sh`, the test stack; documented in the configuration reference, secrets and encryption pages.
- **F-058 (part)**: `DEFAULT_ROLE_PERMISSIONS` - the editor gets `objects:read` and `objects:write`, the viewer `objects:read` (they had neither and got 403 on most routes); the seed adds missing links at every start, so existing installations are corrected at the next start. The 34 granular keys are still not enforced (finding stays open for that).
- Tests: `api/third-party-credentials.test.ts` (6: the key is in no response - public route, reader, admin, list, export; the flag; keep/blank/replace/clear; a client cannot plant the flag; users with the seeded editor and viewer roles can and cannot do what they should), `db/integration-secrets.integration.test.ts` (6 on a disposable database: AI/Figma/push stored encrypted, legacy clear text works and is backfilled, fails closed without the key), `unit/integration-secrets-crypto.test.ts` (7). Full suites: unit+API 1453, browser 30, core 295 (+ integration on a disposable DB).
- Docs: findings (F-055 fixed, F-058 partly fixed, F-059 new), status summary, threat model, data and storage, authentication, encryption, secrets and keys, configuration reference (the new variable; the documentation gate caught it), data protection, release notes (two behaviour changes).

- Tooling: `scripts/release/check-docs.py` now (a) ignores link syntax written as an example inside backticks - it was failing on `documentation-sites.md` - and (b) fails when an environment variable read by the services or listed in `.env.example` is not in the configuration reference; `scripts/release/sbom.sh` writes CycloneDX SBOMs (source tree, and the service images with `--images`) to `.release/sbom/` (verified: 669 components in the source SBOM).

## [Form upload, phase 3: the File Upload element and the sending] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/download-element`.
- Editor: **File Upload** in the palette (group form; `InputFile()` in `ElementsClass.js`, label in `helpers.ts`); the **File upload** section of Advanced (`UploadFieldControl.vue`): accepted types (also written as the input's `accept`), number of files (more than one = `multiple`), a folder for this field (`uploadConfig`, which can only narrow the website's rules). The preview marks the input with `data-whoobe-upload=<node id>`. `form-actions.ts` (the editor's live form and the published pages it serves) uploads the chosen files first and sends `{uploadId}` in their place; a refusal shows the server's words.
- SDK: `renderer` - `ApiClient.uploadFile` (intent, then the bytes to the address returned), `collectFormValues` / `FormUploadError` / `submitErrorMessage` (`form-values.ts`), the renderer marks file inputs with `data-whoobe-upload` and no longer mutates the node's attributes when it builds them. Vue, React and Svelte forms use them; `WhoobeApi.uploadFile` is optional. A server refusal is shown HTML-escaped, other failures keep the form's own error message.
- core: a published page no longer carries an Upload field's `uploadConfig.targetFolder` (`sanitize-public-content.ts`).
- Test stack: `UPLOAD_PUBLIC_BASE_URL` points at the stack's asset service.
- Docs: guide `docs/release/guides/form-uploads.md` (switching it on, adding the field, which class fields hold files, what happens, installation notes).
- Robustness: the same node id can be in several pages (a page duplicated into another website keeps its ids). The upload field lookup now keeps every match; the page can name its website (`websiteId` in the intent) and the files are attached for the field and website they were approved for. A field the server does not know yet (a page published a moment ago) makes it re-read the pages, at most every 2 seconds.
- Tests: `browser/form-upload.spec.ts` (a published page: the file input names itself and offers only the accepted types; a not-allowed type is refused with the reason; a fake `.pdf` is refused; a real one is uploaded, the submission is created and references the asset; the File Upload chip is dragged onto the page and configured in Advanced), `unit/form-values.test.ts` (files replaced by references, several files, empty input, refusal, escaped messages), Vue and React SSR tests for the file input. Not done: the uploaded files are not yet listed in the submissions view (the object shows the asset reference), and the form editor does not warn when the class field cannot hold a file.

## [Form upload, phase 2: the endpoints] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/download-element`. Option B' as decided: S3 is never exposed. Plan updated with "As built".
- core-api: `POST /public/uploads/intent` (public, rate limited) - the field named must be a file input of a form in a page; the website's limits narrowed by the field's own `uploadConfig`; refusals with their own status (403 off, 415 type, 413 size, 429 quota/rate, 404 unknown field, 503 no secret); answers a signed ticket (10 minutes) and the address for the bytes. `POST /public/forms/:classId/submit` now turns the `{uploadId}` values of the form's Upload fields into library assets before the class validates them (every check made before the first file is moved: row staged, same field, same website, allowed type, number of files, the class field can hold it). New `form-uploads.ts` (authored-field cache, upload base URL). `FORM_SUBMIT_RATE_LIMIT_PER_WINDOW` makes the form submit limit configurable (default 10 unchanged).
- asset-processor: `PUT /public/upload-data/:token` (public, ticket-bound): body read with a hard cap (declared size and ticket cap), first 4 KB must match the approved type, pictures decoded and written again (metadata and appended data gone, pixel cap), staged in `private/_uploads/...` (no asset record), a ticket works once, a refused upload is spent; `POST /api/v1/dam/form-uploads/attach` (service token): copies to `<target folder>/<uuid>.<ext>` (folder checked again: private unless a public one is allowed), records the asset with the original name as metadata only, removes the staging copy, claims the row once; cleanup of stale uploads every 30 minutes. JSON parsing skipped for the raw upload route; CORS: PUT allowed, any page origin without credentials.
- core: `uploads/fields.ts` (authored Upload fields, `effectiveUploadLimits`, `readUploadRefs`, staging key), `uploads/images.ts` (`sanitizeUploadedFile`), `uploadTokenSecret()` (placeholder or short = not set).
- Config: `UPLOAD_TOKEN_SECRET` (in `.env.example`, `provision-workspace.sh`, the test stack), optional `UPLOAD_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, rate-limit variables; nginx template routes `/public/upload-data/` to the asset service without request buffering (existing workspaces: re-render with `render-workspace-nginx.sh`).
- API reference: `POST /public/uploads/intent`; route contract updated.
- Tests: `api/form-uploads.test.ts` (25: ticket, every refusal - type, double extension, size, reused/forged/altered ticket, wrong content, bytes larger than declared, fake picture, empty; attaching - private asset with a generated name readable by an admin and not by the public, picture re-encoded, once only, other field, never sent, made-up ids, too many files, uploads switched off, daily quota; the attach endpoint is not public; CORS), unit tests for the pure parts (262 unit tests in all). Not tested automatically: the periodic cleanup and the nginx route on a real proxy.

## [Form uploads: the website settings] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/download-element`. Request: the upload settings per website were missing in the dashboard (phase 1 had the logic only).
- core-api: `GET` and `PUT /whoobe-one/websites/:id/upload-settings` (settings:manage). ## [Form upload, phase 1: limits, content check, ticket, table] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/download-element`. Decision taken: option B' (a signed ticket from the "intent" step, bytes streamed through our own endpoint, S3 never exposed). Plan: `docs/plans/forms/form-upload-plan.md`.
- `packages/core/src/uploads/limits.ts` (browser-safe): the per-website limits (`settings.uploads`: enabled, allowed extensions, max file size, max files, daily bytes, target folder, allow public folder) with `normalizeUploadLimits` (defaults off/private/small, numbers clamped to ceilings of 25 MB / 10 files / 1 GB a day, only verifiable and non-active extensions kept, a public folder only with `allowPublicFolder`, unsafe folders replaced); `checkUploadIntent` (disabled, bad name, blocked kinds including double extensions like `a.pdf.exe`, not in the allow-list, empty, too large, daily quota); generated stored names, cleaned display names.
- `uploads/sniff.ts`: the content must be what the extension says (magic bytes for pdf, images, wav/mp3/mp4, zip and Office/OpenDocument containers, legacy Office, rtf; txt/csv/md must be valid UTF-8 without control bytes); the browser's Content-Type is never used; only the first 4 KB are needed, so a stream can be refused on its first chunk. Extensions the check does not know (html, svg, js, php, exe, xml ...) can never be allowed.
- `uploads/token.ts` (server): the ticket for one file, HMAC-SHA256, short-lived, constant-time compare, malformed/forged/expired = null; refuses to sign without a real secret.
- Database: migration `0052_form_uploads` (`form_uploads`: website, form, status pending/staged/attached/rejected/expired, extension, original name, sizes, storage key, asset, submission) + records (`createFormUpload`, `websiteUploadBytesToday`, `markFormUploadStaged` (once), `markFormUploadAttached` (only for the website and form it was issued for), `markFormUploadRejected`, `expireStaleFormUploads`).
- Test: `scripts/security/unit/form-uploads.test.ts` (36 checks). Migration verified on the security stack. Nothing is reachable by visitors yet (no endpoint): phase 2.

## [Plan: file upload in forms] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/download-element`. `docs/plans/forms/form-upload-plan.md`: two-step upload, limits per website, private target folder by default, content checks, phases. Open point: B (pre-signed S3 URL, needs S3 reachable by visitors) vs B' (signed token, bytes streamed through our own endpoint, recommended).

## [Download element tied to a file] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/download-element`. Request: the download must be associated with a file, hence an asset (or a URL); the element keeps the same customization.
- Data: the file is kept in `image.url` (like Audio, Image, Video), so asset resolution and the website export/import reference rewriting need no change.
- SDK: the renderer (`walk.ts`) resolves a `tag: 'file'` node into `descriptor.link` `{href, target, download: true}` (asset or URL, no `<img>` as well); `descriptor.link.download` is new. Vue, React and Svelte emit `<a ... download>` with the button's classes and icon (Svelte's button now renders a link when it has one). No file: an inert button, as before.
- Editor: `advanced/DownloadControl.vue` (the **Download** section of Advanced: file path/URL with the asset picker, open in a new tab, a warning when no file is chosen or the asset is private); the canvas and the Preview render the button with its icon, the Preview as a link that saves the file.
- Tests: renderer-based tests in the Vue and React suites (asset and URL links, `download`, `target`, class kept, empty source = button, no image); `browser/download-element.spec.ts` (source set in Advanced, warnings, preview link). Unit 208, browser suite 27 pass.
- Docs: guide `download-element.md`; the page-builder manual links to it.
- Fix (reported): "Open in a new tab" did nothing in Preview for images and media (the preview never set `target`, and `download` makes the browser save a file or ignore it cross-origin). Now "open in a new tab" means open it: `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"` and no `download` attribute, in the Preview and in the SDK renderer (`link.download` is false then); the option's label says so. Tests updated (Vue, React, browser spec).
- Decided for the next step (form upload field): file goes through a two-step upload with a pre-signed URL (option B), limits set per website, private target folder by default, content checks.

## [Snippets: lock (no autosave); search inputs no longer collapse] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `feat/snippet-lock`. Request: a lock option on snippets - when locked, autosave is off.
- Database: migration `0051_snippet_lock` (`whoobe_one_snippets.locked boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`), schema and records (`setWhoobeOneSnippetLocked`).
- core-api: `PUT /whoobe-one/snippets/:id/lock` (`{locked}`, objects:write, 400/404); `PATCH /whoobe-one/snippets/:id` flagged `autosave: true` answers **423** `{locked: true}` while the snippet is locked (a second tab that still thinks it is unlocked cannot overwrite it); a manual save always works. Route contract and API reference updated.
- Website bundle: `locked` travels with exported snippets and is restored on import.
- Editor (`whoobe-one-spike`): a Lock/Unlock toggle in the top bar while a snippet is edited (`data-testid="snippet-lock"`, `aria-pressed`); the autosave timer skips a locked snippet, manual Save still saves, a 423 is explained and switches the editor to locked.
- Dashboard: Snippets list and grid show a lock toggle (amber when locked) per snippet; `snippetsStore.setLocked`.
- Fix (reported): the search input of Snippets and Pages was ~5 px wide with invisible text. `.field` has `width:100%`, so a `select.field` beside the flex-1 search input took the whole row. `ui.css`: a `select.field` directly inside a flex container sizes to its options (also fixes rows-per-page and the mail log filters); the two selects also get `sm:w-auto`.
- Follow-up: the editor's lock button is shown for every snippet, disabled (with a hint) until the snippet has been saved; before, it was hidden for a new unsaved snippet and looked missing.
- Tests: `api/snippet-lock.test.ts` (400/404/permission, 423 on autosave while locked and nothing saved, manual save works, unlocked autosave works); `browser/snippet-lock.spec.ts` (search inputs > 200 px, lock toggle from the list).

## [Navigation: a collapsed sidebar shows the children of an item as a menu to its right] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `ui/consistency`. Request: with the main navigation collapsed to icons, the children of the main items must appear as a popover menu to the right of the item/icon.
- `layouts/DashboardLayout.vue`: when the sidebar is collapsed, hovering (or focusing) an item that has children opens a menu next to its icon - **Assets**: the folders (Root first, then every folder indented by depth, the current one marked); **Data Objects**: the classes; **AI**: agent / image generation / translation; **Settings**: its sections. A click on an entry does what the same entry does in the open sidebar and closes the menu. The pointer can travel from the icon to the menu (220 ms grace), Esc closes it wherever the focus is, it is kept inside the window (nudged up when it would run off the bottom), scrolls when long, and never survives expanding the sidebar. Items without children keep their tooltip; the tooltip is replaced by the menu's title for those with children. The entries come from one function (`navChildren`), the same data the open sidebar renders.
- `styles/ui.css`: `.ui-popover` (+ title, item, active, empty), dark and light, above the page and below windows; `ui-contrast.test.ts` also checks its text, muted text, active item and hovered item (43 checks).
- Test: `browser/nav-collapsed.spec.ts` - the menu opens to the right of the icon and close to it, the pointer can reach it, entries open DAM settings / the AI translation card / a nested folder of Assets (indented), Esc and moving away close it, an item without children shows no menu, expanded again there is none. The whole browser suite (25 specs) passes.

## [UI consistency, part 1: one look for buttons, fields, windows and font; an AI section in the navigation] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `ui/consistency` (a branch for the UI work, kept in step with `master` by merging it in; created from `feat/docs-sites`, which holds `master` plus the documentation sites). Request: more uniform UI - some windows differ, the font changes between windows, light-mode buttons are not always clear (contrast of background/text/hover) - and an AI section of the navigation for the AI settings.
- **Font**: `<body>` carried `font-mono`, which overrode the Inter/sans stack set in `style.css`, so the whole dashboard was monospaced and only what did not inherit from `<body>` was not. One token now (`--ui-font`: Inter, loaded in `index.html`, then the system's) on `html`/`body`, form controls inherit it, and a monospace face (`--ui-font-code`) stays for code, identifiers and paths (`font-mono`, still winning where an element asks for it).
- **Shared styles** (`src/styles/ui.css`): `btn` with variants `primary`, `secondary`, `ghost`, `danger`, `success`, `warning`, `soft-*`, `link` (+ `-danger`, `-success`, `-info`, `-warning`, `-muted`) and sizes; `field` (+ `field-error`, `field-label`, `field-hint`); modal classes; each with its dark and its `.light` colours (hover darkens in both themes). `scripts/security/unit/ui-contrast.test.ts` parses the stylesheet and fails if any text/background pair - normal, hover, over the page and the card colour, both themes - drops below WCAG AA (4.5:1), or the outline of a ghost button / the edge of a light field becomes too faint (41 checks). A reference page, `/ui-kit` (not in the navigation), shows everything in both themes.
- **Windows**: `components/ui/BaseModal.vue` (+ `modal-stack.ts`) - one overlay (dimmed in light too, it used to be a white wash in some, black/40, /60, /70 in others), panel, header (title, subtitle, actions, close), footer, sizes `sm`...`2xl`/`full`, rendered in `<body>` above everything (one z-index instead of 50 / 220 / 10000 / 10001), Esc and a click outside to close (not for `persistent` forms, which is what the windows without a click-outside handler became), the focus kept inside and given back, the page behind not scrolling, only the topmost window answers Esc. 21 hand-made windows converted by a codemod (Websites, Publish, Brevo, AI wizard, Mail, Templates, Pages, Assets, Objects, Snippets, Users, Roles, Gateway endpoints ...); two with their own header controls (the asset picker) and the full-screen editors/drawer are left as they were.
- **Buttons and fields**: 294 `<button>`s whose colours were written by hand (the common signatures: indigo/emerald/rose/amber solid, slate secondary, tinted, and the text-only actions in indigo/rose/emerald/sky/amber/slate - several of which had no light-mode colours at all, e.g. indigo-400 text on white) now use `btn btn-*`; 248 inputs/selects/textareas use `field`. Layout classes on the element (flex, gap, w-full, ml-auto ...) were kept. 157 buttons with other colour combinations (sidebar, tab strip, toggles, icon buttons) were not touched.
- **AI section**: the navigation has an **AI** entry (before Settings, in the System group) with a submenu - *AI agent*, *Image generation*, *Translation* - and its own view (`views/AiView.vue`); the three cards no longer sit in Settings (`SettingsSubSection` lost `ai`, `ai-image`, `ai-translate`; new `AiSubSection`, `tabsStore.openAiSubSection`).
- Tests: the documentation spec follows the image dialog now being a window in `<body>`; the whole browser suite passes on the throwaway stack otherwise unchanged.
- Not done yet (next on this branch): the rest of the buttons, headings/text sizes, the editor's own windows, the right sidebar of the editor (see below).

## [Container images: tiff CVE-2026-4775 (the image scan of the 8 October push run failed)] — 2026-10-09
- Branch: `master`. The `security` run of the push of 8 October (08650d4, before the CI change below it) failed on its last step, `trivy image`: both nginx-based images (`admin-dashboard`, `whoobe-one-spike`) carry `tiff 4.7.1-r0` with a fixable HIGH, CVE-2026-4775 (fixed in 4.7.2-r0) - published after the pinned `nginx-unprivileged:alpine` digest was built; everything else in that run passed (API 1201, browser 19, isolation, TLS). Same cure as for pcre2: `apk upgrade --no-cache pcre2 tiff` in both stages of the `Dockerfile` (checked that the package upgrades on that digest; the comment says to drop it when the upstream tag catches up).
- Also: committing `.github/dependabot.yml` made Dependabot start four update runs at once (a changed config is a trigger); they were cancelled to save minutes - the updates will come with the monthly schedule.

## [CI: far fewer Actions minutes] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `ci/save-minutes`. Report: the monthly quota of Actions minutes of the private repository is almost used up; the usage page shows about 2.50 a day.
- Where the minutes went (read from the runs since 1 October): every push to `master` ran `static` (3 min) + `stack-tests` (15) + `isolation` (5) + `tls` x2 (1 each) = ~25 minutes of runner time, ~30 billed (each job rounds up); 21 pushes; 10 manual runs started on top of pushes that had just had theirs; the nightly run does the same work every night whether or not anything changed; six Dependabot runs of ~26 minutes each (they run again whenever a manifest or the lockfile changes) - roughly 1,100+ billed minutes in 8 days.
- **`.github/workflows/security.yml`**: documents-only pushes (`docs/**`, `**/*.md`, `CHANGES.md`) start nothing; a new cheap `changes` job (seconds) decides what is worth running; `stack-tests` and `isolation` no longer run on push - they run **nightly only if code changed in the last 26 hours**, and by hand (the release gate is unchanged: a manual run on the release commit, `dast` ticked); `tls` runs only when `deploy/nginx/`, `scripts/security/tls/` or the workflow changed; `static` still runs on every code push and pull request. The same suites are run locally on a throwaway stack before pushing, as in every change of this project.
- **`.github/dependabot.yml`**: npm and Docker update runs monthly instead of weekly, fewer open pull requests.
- Estimate: a day with 5 code pushes went from ~150 billed minutes to ~20 (static) + ~25 (the nightly run, if code changed) ; a day with only documents to nothing. Not done (possible next steps): Docker layer caching for the stack build (about half of the 15 minutes of `stack-tests` is building images), sharing one image build between `stack-tests` and `isolation`.
- Docs: `scripts/security/README.md` (the policy, "Actions minutes"), `docs/release/security/security-ci-for-forks.md`, `security-testing-plan.md`.

## [Documentation websites can be exported and imported] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `feat/docs-sites`. Request: documentation websites need an export, because part of their work is done in the dashboard (index, texts, images).
- **Export** (`portability/export.ts`): a website with `settings.kind = "docs"` has no pages, so the bundle adds every asset under its source folder (`documentationFolderOf`) - documents, images, `_nav.yml`, other files - with path, visibility and metadata; the dashboard zips their files with the rest. The website's settings (title, folder, home document) travel as always. **Export** is on the website's row (Settings > Websites) and in the header of the site's tab (`downloadWebsiteZip`, shared); for a documentation site nobody is asked about including the files.
- **Import** (`portability/import.ts`, `POST /whoobe-one/websites/import`): the files go back to the folder they came from when it is free; when it is taken the copy gets a free folder of its own (`<folder>-import`, `-import-2` ...) so editing the copy never touches the original (the documents use relative paths, which stay valid because the folder moves as a whole). The asset plan is made on the new paths; the new website's `settings.docs.sourceFolder` points at the new folder; the report has `docs: { sourceFolder, targetFolder }` and a warning. The client that stores the files first (the dashboard) asks in a dry run and gives the same folder as `docsFolder` in the final call (validated: absolute, no `..`, not the root; an empty value means "not given"). The import dialog says where the files go.
- Not rewritten: absolute `/dam-asset/<key>` addresses written by hand in a document (use relative paths; *Insert image* writes them).
- Tests: API `docs-site.test.ts` 15 (bundle content and visibility, no pages; dry run with the free folder, a chosen one, bad folders refused; a full import with the files stored first gives a working website in the new folder with the same documents); browser `docs-export.spec.ts` (export from the dashboard without the question, the zip holds the five files byte for byte with their visibility; the site and its files deleted, then imported: files back in the same folder, index, titles, image attributes and no warnings; imported again: a copy in `<folder>-import`, independent of the original after an edit). Throwaway stack: API 37 files / 1284 tests, browser 23 specs, all passing (one earlier API failure was the edge rate limit answering 429 to a test that reuses an address within a minute, after many reruns on the same stack).
- Docs: `guides/documentation-sites.md` (Export and import), `guides/website-bundle.md`.

## [Trees: a tree editor for the documentation index, and a reliable element tree in the editor] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `feat/docs-sites`. Request: the index of a documentation site as a tree editor; and, while at it, the editor's own element tree, whose moves were difficult and often did not work.
- **Shared rules** (`packages/core/src/ui/tree-dnd.ts`, exported from `@whoobe-cms/core`, 14 unit tests): a drop is always *this node before / inside / after that node* - never a pixel or a DOM index. `dropPositionAt` (container: top quarter before, bottom quarter after, the rest inside; leaf: halves), `moveNode` (in place; refuses a node into itself or its descendants, the root, a leaf as a container; the off-by-one of moving down in the same list is handled; a test moves every node to every place and checks nothing is lost or duplicated), `canDrop` (for the highlight while dragging), `siblingMove` (up, down, indent into the previous sibling, outdent after the parent), `locate`, `inSubtree`.
- **Editor element tree** (`DocumentTreeNode.vue`, `DocumentTree.vue`, `tree-drag.ts`): why it failed - the rows were items of the canvas's dragula instance, the rows of nested containers were flat siblings with only padding for depth, so a drop meant whatever container dragula believed was under the pointer (INTO a container, or at a precise place among rows of different depths, often went elsewhere or nowhere); and selecting a container re-rooted the tree on it, so you could not drag anything out of it. Now: native drag and drop with the shared rules, a line above/below the row or a highlight for "inside" while dragging, closed containers open when hovered, **Alt+arrows** and four buttons on the selected row (the focus follows the row after a move), the **whole page is shown by default** with the selection highlighted, its branch opened and its row scrolled into view; a **Page / Selection** switch (remembered) gives back the old branch-only view. The tree is no longer part of the canvas's dragula: a palette item cannot be dropped onto a tree row and a tree row cannot be dragged onto the canvas (the palette still adds to the canvas, the canvas still moves its own elements).
- **Index editor** (`DocsNavEditor.vue`, `DocsNavNode.vue`, `utils/docs-nav-edit.ts`, `docs-nav-drag.ts`; replaces the left half of the text view): the same drag rules, Alt+arrows/F2/Delete, + Section / + Link, rename (a document's title is changed in the index only and marked *custom*), the list **Not in the index** to drag documents from, **Visual / Text** switch (text -> tree is read by the server, so a broken text keeps you in the text), Check / Save / Use the folders again as before. The tree is written as `_nav.yml` by `editToYaml` and read back by the real parser (unit tests, including quoting of odd titles).
- Tests: core `tree-dnd.test.ts` 14; `unit/docs-nav-edit.test.ts` 7; browser `editor-tree.spec.ts` (drag a loose element INTO a container, before/after at the edges across levels, moving down in the same list, refusals leave everything untouched, selecting a container keeps the page in view, Page/Selection switch, Alt+arrows, buttons, the canvas follows the tree and nothing is lost) and `docs-site.spec.ts` (index edited by dragging: section, rename, drag into it, reorder by keyboard, section into itself refused, take a document out and drag it back from the list, save, text view and broken text). Throwaway stack: API 37 files / 1281 tests, browser 22 specs, core 295, all passing.
- Docs: `guides/documentation-sites.md` (the index editor).

## [Documentation sites: inserting images with width and alignment] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `feat/docs-sites`. Request: a way to put images (from an address or from the assets) in a document, with width and justification.
- **Markdown** (`packages/core/src/docs/markdown.ts`): `![alt](path "title"){width=50% align=center}` - `width` = digits with `px`/`%`/`rem`/`em`/`vw` (a bare number is px, `%`/`vw` up to 100), `align` = left/center/right, `float` = left/right (text wraps). Safe by construction: the braces are parsed by `parseImageAttrs`, only these keys and values reach a `class` and a `style="width:..."`, everything else (a CSS injection, a quote break-out, an unknown key) is dropped with a warning and the image is still shown; the address goes through the same checks as any image (folder file, `https:` address, no `javascript:`/`data:`). Works inside a link. Every image now carries `class="doc-img ..."`; the three viewers (editor application, dashboard preview, the static site) have the CSS for left/center/right/float and clear floats at headings and code.
- **Dashboard** (`DocsImageDialog.vue`, **Insert image** in the document editor): four sources - the documentation folder (thumbnails), the **asset library** (search; an image outside the folder is *copied* into `<folder>/images/` so the path stays relative and the published site is self-contained), **upload** (into a folder you name, default `images`) and an **address**; description (prefilled from the file name), width (25 % ... 100 %, original or custom) and position (inline, left, centered, right, text wrapping left/right) and a line showing the exact Markdown; it inserts at the cursor, on its own line, and refreshes the preview. `relativeDocPath`/`imageMarkdown` in `utils/docs-sites-api.ts`; `GET /docs-sites/:id/tree` also returns `fileAssets` (path, key, visibility) for the thumbnails.
- **Fix found on the way**: the files of the documents are written as `/dam-asset/...`; where the asset processor is not on the origin of the viewer (local development, a separate host) the images did not load. The docs layout and the dashboard preview now put the asset processor's address in front (`getDefaultAssetProcessorUrl`).
- Tests: core `docs.test.ts` 29 (width/align/float, units, injection attempts, link, missing file, dangerous address, braces after non-images); `unit/docs-images.test.ts` 11 (relative paths from any depth with spaces/`&`, the Markdown written for each choice read back by the real renderer); browser `docs-site.spec.ts` (upload through the dialog with 50 % centered, the image loads in the preview and in the docs layout with `width:50%`; a library image copied into `images/` with a custom width and float; an address; both files appear in the folder) and `docs-static.spec.ts` (the static page centers a 40 % image in the article). Throwaway stack: API 35 files / 1274 tests, browser 21 specs, all passing.
- Docs: `guides/documentation-sites.md` (the syntax and the dialog).

## [Documentation sites, phase 2: search and static publishing; wider layout] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `feat/docs-sites`. Feedback after trying it ("an excellent job, a killer feature"): the content area was too narrow for technical documentation.
- **Layout** (`DocsView.vue`, `DocsNavTree.vue`): container up to 100rem (was 80), article up to 62rem (was 48), index 14rem and "On this page" 12rem with smaller type (13 px) - more room for tables and code.
- **Search** - one MiniSearch index, built on the server and searched in the browser. `packages/core/src/docs/search.ts` (browser-safe subpath `@whoobe-cms/core/docs-search`; `minisearch` added): an entry per page and one per h2/h3 section (a result can open the page at that heading), fields title/headings/description/text with boosts, prefix, accents folded, fuzzy for longer words, all words must match. core-api: `GET /public/docs/:id/search-index` (public documents only) and `GET /docs-sites/:id/search-index` (`ASSETS_VIEW`, private included), built only when a document, a version or the index changed (cached by a signature), `ETag`/304. The docs layout's box downloads the index on first focus and shows results as you type (arrows, Enter, Esc, **Ctrl/Cmd K**). No hooks and no `search_documents` rows: the index cannot drift from the folder (the plan's section 6 says so).
- **Static publishing** - `packages/publish/src/docs-site.ts`, `whoobe-publish --kind docs` (`--base-path`, `--site-url`, `--asset-base-url`, `--no-copy-assets`, `--strict`) and **4) Documentation** in `publish-site.sh`: reads the PUBLIC API only (so nothing private can reach the output), writes one `index.html` per page, `404.html`, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt`, `assets/` (CSS, one vanilla script for menu/scroll-spy/copy buttons/search, MiniSearch, `search-index.json`, the images and files the documents use - copied, or linked with `--no-copy-assets`), code highlighting with highlight.js loaded only on pages that have code (`@highlightjs/cdn-assets` added), light/dark by preference, print styles. Broken links are listed (an error with `--strict`); a rerun removes the pages of documents that no longer exist (`.whoobe-docs.json`).
- **Dashboard**: a *Publish* tab in the documentation window with the command filled in (path, public address) and a copy button.
- Tests: core `docs.test.ts` 23 (search); `unit/docs-search-parity.test.ts` 14 (the options in the generated script give the same results as the server's); API `docs-site.test.ts` 12 (public index has no private text, ETag/304, login for the editors' one); browser `docs-site.spec.ts` (search with a typo opening a section, private page searchable only when signed in, nothing for an anonymous visitor, the Publish tab) and the new `docs-static.spec.ts` (generates a site from the stack, serves it under `/docs/` with a plain web server and uses it in a browser: navigation, "on this page", highlighting, copy button, search from the index file, copied image loads, 404, the private document absent from every output file, sitemap). Whole suites on the throwaway stack: API 35 files / 1263 tests, browser 21 specs, all passing.
- Docs: `guides/documentation-sites.md` (search, publishing), plan section 6, API reference (210 routes).
- Not done: a drag-and-drop editor for the index; languages and versions; the layout as a template of ordinary websites; highlighted matches in search results.

## [Documentation sites, phase 1 (part 2): dashboard manager and the docs layout] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `feat/docs-sites`. Completes phase 1 of `docs/plans/docs-sites/docs-site-plan.md`.
- **Dashboard** (`DocsSiteManager.vue`, `utils/docs-sites-api.ts`, `WebsiteSettingsCard.vue`): **+ New Documentation** (name + source folder of the asset library) creates a website with `settings.kind = "docs"`; its row says *documentation* and offers **Documents**. The window has three tabs: *Documents* (the files with title, address, public/private/hidden badges; **+ New document**, **Upload files**, **Upload folder** keeping the structure, edit, delete; warnings and the list of other files), an **editor** with the source on one side and the live rendered page on the other (rendered by the same server pipeline through the new `POST /docs-sites/:id/render`, so broken links are reported while writing; Ctrl/Cmd+S; the *Public* box), *Index* (the current index, a YAML text of `_nav.yml` with **Start from the folders**, **Check** via `POST /docs-sites/:id/nav/check`, **Save index** - a broken text is never saved -, **Use the folders again**), and *Settings* (title, source folder, home document). Files are written with the ordinary DAM upload (`.md` as `text/markdown`, `_nav.yml` public).
- **Docs layout** in the editor application (`views/DocsView.vue`, `components/DocsNavTree.vue`, `views/SiteEntry.vue`, `docs-api.ts`): the catch-all route now goes through `SiteEntry`, which asks `GET /public/docs-site/:segment` (new, public, cached per session) whether the first segment of the address is a documentation website's root path; if so the docs layout is shown (header, the index on the left, the page, "On this page" with scroll-spy on the right, breadcrumbs, previous/next, last updated, responsive drawer), otherwise `PublicPage` exactly as before. A signed-in session with asset permissions gets the preview endpoints (private documents too, marked *preview*, with the document's warnings); anybody else the public ones. The pipeline marks addresses inside the site with `data-doc="1"` so the viewer prefixes only those with the site's root path (an absolute address the author wrote for the host is left alone). The header search box looks titles up for now; real search is phase 2.
- **core-api**: `describeDocsSite` also returns `autoNav`, `navFile`, `navText`, and `s3Key` per document; the index file is read whatever its own visibility (it only names documents that are present); 3 more routes (`render`, `nav/check`, `public/docs-site/:segment`) in the contract and the API docs (208 routes).
- Guide: `docs/release/guides/documentation-sites.md`, linked from the release README.
- Tests: `api/docs-site.test.ts` now 10 (adds the root-path lookup), core `docs.test.ts` 19, and a browser spec `browser/docs-site.spec.ts` through the real UI (open the manager, edit with live preview and a reported broken link, new document, index from the folders, check, save, a broken index refused, the docs layout with the hand-written index, "On this page", previous/next, a link inside a document staying in the site, an editor seeing the private document, not signed in getting "Document not found" and no leak). Throwaway stack: API 34 files / 1241 tests, browser 20 specs, core 271 - all passing.
- Found while testing: a link inside a rendered page read its already-prefixed `href` after the viewer rewrote it and pushed a doubled path (404); the original address is now kept in `data-doc-route`.
- Not done (phase 2): search (MiniSearch static + live), static generator `--kind docs`, a drag-and-drop tree editor for the index (it is a YAML text with a check for now), languages/versions, the layout as a template of ordinary websites.
