## [Mobile app: the Audio element renders with its waveform and title] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Report: the audio element (waveform, title) was not rendered by `publish-mobile.sh`; the app showed the browser's default player.
- Cause: the mobile renderer (both the dynamic and the static `PageRenderer.vue`) emitted a plain `<audio>`; the waveform player and the title exist only in the editor (`components/AudioPlayer.vue`), and the guide listed this as a limitation.
- `packages/mobile-app/src/cli.ts`: the generator copies the editor's `AudioPlayer.vue` and `scripts/audio-waveform.ts` into the project (`src/components`, `src/scripts`; `copyAudioPlayer`, a clear error if the generator is not run from the monorepo) and both renderers use `<AudioPlayer>` with the element's `src`, `source` and `options`. A copy at generation time, not a second implementation, so the editor and the app cannot drift. Nothing new to install (`@lucide/vue` is already a dependency). Checked: a project generated from the local CMS contains both files and the renderer, and the SFCs compile (parse, script, template) with no errors; not played in a device/browser here.
- `docs/release/guides/audio-element.md`: the "where it works" table updated; projects generated before today need to be generated again (after `pnpm run --filter @whoobe-cms/mobile-app... build`).

## [Encryption guide, TLS to Postgres, encrypted backups, date on every log line] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Request: assess database encryption (an enterprise acceptance condition); guide + encrypted backups + check TLS to Postgres in the code; show the date, not only the time, on each log record.
- **TLS to Postgres was not possible** (`getConnectionConfig` had no ssl at all; `?sslmode=` in `DATABASE_URL` was ignored). Now `DB_SSL=disable|require|verify-full` (+ `DB_SSL_CA` path or PEM) or `sslmode` in `DATABASE_URL`; applies to the pool, to the bootstrap client that creates the database and (via `sslmode`) to `drizzle.config.ts`. An unreadable CA or an unknown value stops the start, never a silent plain connection. The core-api start log says `tls: off | encrypted, certificate not checked | verified`. `packages/core/src/db/client.ts`, `client.test.ts` (8 unit tests); checked live against PostgreSQL 16 with `ssl=on` and a private certificate: plain -> `pg_stat_ssl.ssl=false`, `require` and `verify-full`+CA -> true, `verify-full` without the CA is refused.
- **Encrypted backups**: `scripts/backup-workspace.sh <slug> [--out-dir] [--no-env] [--keep N]` (`pg_dump | gzip | age`, or GPG; refuses to run without `BACKUP_AGE_RECIPIENT` / `BACKUP_GPG_RECIPIENT`, nothing in clear on disk, files 600 in a 700 directory, the workspace's `.env.<slug>` included because it holds the encryption keys) and `scripts/restore-workspace-db.sh` (`--check` decrypts and tests without Docker; a restore tests first, then asks to type the slug). `backups/` is git-ignored. Exercised here with GPG and a stub `docker` (age is not installed on this machine; the age branch is the same pipeline with `age -r/-R` and `age -d -i`): no clear text in the files, `--keep` rotation, restore feeds the dump to psql, wrong confirmation cancels. Unit tests added to `workspace-scripts.test.ts` (26).
- **Guide** `docs/release/operations/encryption.md`: the four levels (disk/volume, TLS, engine TDE, field level), what Whoobe encrypts (TOTP, gateway auth, migration sources, mail secrets), what it does not (content; stated plainly, with the reason), per-environment steps for level 1, `DB_SSL`, backups, keys and their (manual) rotation, answers for a security questionnaire. Linked from the release README, deployment and workspaces guides; `.env.example` has the two new variables.
- **System logs** (`SystemLogsView.vue`): each record shows `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` (viewer's time zone) instead of the time only; the full original value is the tooltip.
- Not done: key rotation command / KMS source for the field keys; the age path was not run against a real `age` binary.

## [Publishing guides: a section on styles and themes] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. `docs/release/guides/publish/website-publishing.md` and `capacitor-mobile-app.md`: new "Styles and themes" section (what a theme gives the generated project, body rules inside `@layer base`, how layers/utilities/plain classes interact, `@import` handling, regenerate after a theme change; for the mobile project where the theme and the defaults live).

## [Generated projects: default body styles go in a layer below the theme's (the :where() fix was not enough)] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Report with the real `<head>` of a generated mobile project: the theme wrote `body { background-color; color; font-family }` inside `@layer base`, the page stayed white.
- Cause: any **unlayered** rule beats every **layered** one whatever its specificity or position, so the project's `main.css` (`html, body, #app { background: #fff }`) won over the theme's `@layer base` body rule; `:where()` (previous entry) only lowers specificity and changed nothing here.
- `packages/mobile-app/src/cli.ts`: the defaults of `main.css` are in `@layer defaults`, declared first by `<style>@layer defaults;</style>` ahead of the theme, so they are the lowest layer. Verified in Chromium with the user's theme shape (`@layer base { body {...} }` in the compiled theme style): body background, colour and font are the theme's, `text-brand` works.
- `packages/publish/src/cli.ts` (Next and Nuxt `globals.css`): the default `body` font was an unlayered rule written after the theme and beat the theme's own `@layer base` font; it is now inside `@layer base` placed before the theme's raw styles, so the theme's later rule wins.
- Second part (the same report, after regenerating): the body was still white because the default background was also on `html` and `#app`; `#app` (full height) painted white over the dark body and `html` stopped the body's colour from reaching the canvas. The default background is now on `body` only (`html`/`#app` keep just `min-height`), so the theme's body colour fills the page. Checked in Chromium: body `#0F1115`, `html` and `#app` transparent.

## [Editor: the theme's custom CSS stays the last stylesheet of the head] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Report: with equal specificity the last `<style>` of the head wins, and the theme's rules (`body { background-color: black }`) were being placed before the ones Tailwind's runtime writes, so they lost.
- `apps/whoobe-one-spike/src/composables/useThemeStyles.ts`: `<style id="whoobe-custom-styles">` is injected at once, while `@tailwindcss/browser` adds its own style when its script has loaded, so the theme ended up earlier. One `MutationObserver` on the head (shared) moves the theme's custom style back to the end whenever something is appended after it (a Tailwind style, a font link). Not reproduced with a real theme from here: only the order logic was checked.

## [Mobile/PWA project: the theme is compiled by Tailwind (colours were ignored)] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Follow-up of the previous entry: the theme of a generated mobile project still did not behave.
- Cause (reproduced in Chromium with the project's own order: theme `<style>` in the head, Tailwind browser runtime added by script afterwards): `<style id="theme-styles">` was a plain style, which the runtime never compiles, so its `@theme { --color-* }` block was invalid CSS and ignored: theme colours never became utilities (`text-brand` stayed the default colour) while plain rules like `body { ... }` applied.
- `packages/mobile-app/src/cli.ts`: the theme goes in `<style type="text/tailwindcss" id="theme-styles">` (compiled; `@theme` and the custom rules work), the `@import` lines (Google Fonts) in a separate plain `<style id="theme-imports">` because Tailwind's compiler drops the output when it has to resolve a remote import. Checked in the browser with and without an import. Together with the `:where()` defaults of the previous entry.

## [Theme CSS no longer overridden by the generated stylesheets' body defaults] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Report: a theme with `body { background-color: black; }` had no effect because a generated stylesheet that comes after it declares the body again.
- `packages/mobile-app/src/cli.ts` (`mainCss`): the white `html, body, #app` background and the `body` font-family came after `<style id="theme-styles">` with the same specificity and won; they are now `:where(...)` defaults (specificity 0), so what the theme says wins.
- `packages/publish/src/cli.ts` (Next scaffold `globals.css`, 2 places): the `body { font-family }` written after the theme's raw styles overrode the theme's own body font; now `:where(body)`.
- Editor/preview unchanged: the custom styles there are unlayered, so they already beat Tailwind's layered output.
- Not checked: the Outgoing-mail warning on the local server stays until `./dev-cms.sh --stop && ./dev-cms.sh` (the running core-api started 7 Oct).

## [Outgoing mail: a configuration per website, HTTP mail providers (OneSignal), credentials encrypted at rest (F-054)] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Request: a connector from the outgoing e-mail to the OneSignal API, as a configuration-based system like the gateway presets (each external service has its own API); several recipients belong to a gateway endpoint instead; the provider chosen **per website**; the existing SMTP password to be encrypted.
- **Providers as data** (`packages/core/mail-providers/*.json`, `MAIL_PROVIDERS_PATH` for yours, read again when a file changes, no restart; a malformed file is skipped and logged): a definition names its config fields (secret or not), the HTTP request with `{{placeholders}}` (url fixed and https, no placeholder in the host), how to read the answer (accepted statuses, `failWhen` conditions such as OneSignal's `200` with an empty `id`, the field holding the provider's message id and the error text), retries on 429/503 with `Retry-After`, and what the provider cannot do (CC, text part, attachments, at most N blind copies - a message that needs more is refused, never silently cut). One executor (`mail/http-transport.ts`) runs any of them: values are put into the JSON structure, never concatenated into it; the call goes through `safeFetch`; errors never contain a secret; the same idempotency key on every retry. **OneSignal** is built in, written from its current documentation (`POST https://api.onesignal.com/notifications?c=email`, `Authorization: Key ...`, `email_to`, sender, BCC, `include_unsubscribed`, `idempotency_key`).
- **Per website**: `mail_settings` has a row for the installation's default (`website_id` null) and may have one per website; `sendMail({ websiteId })` uses the website's own configuration if it has one, otherwise the default. A form notification uses the website of the page the authored block is in; password reset uses the default; the log remembers the website, the provider and the provider's message id, and "resend" repeats with the same website. Deleting a website deletes its configuration.
- **F-054 fixed**: the SMTP password was stored in clear text (`mail_settings.password`). SMTP passwords and provider secrets are now AES-256-GCM encrypted (`MAIL_SETTINGS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, own variable; generated by `provision-workspace.sh`, in `.env.example` and the test stack); an old plaintext password keeps working and is encrypted by a startup backfill; without the key a secret cannot be saved and the screen says so. Secrets are write-only everywhere (`hasPassword`, `secretsSet`; blank = keep). Migration `0050_mail_providers_per_website.sql` (expand only: new columns, host/port/from nullable, partial unique index per website; hand-written like the recent ones because `drizzle-kit generate` cannot load every schema file).
- **API**: `GET/PUT /mail-settings` take `websiteId` and a new body (`provider`, `smtp`, `providerConfig`; the old flat SMTP body still works); new `GET /mail-providers` (definitions without their request internals) and `DELETE /mail-settings?websiteId=` (the default cannot be removed); `POST /mail-settings/test` takes `websiteId`. 201 routes documented, contract updated.
- **Dashboard** (`MailSettingsCard.vue`): "Settings for" (default or a website), "Send with" (SMTP or a provider; its fields come from the definition), warnings (no encryption key; "this website uses the default"), "Give this website its own configuration" / "Use the default again", the test sends with the chosen scope, the log shows the provider.
- **Live check**: `scripts/mail/live-check.mjs --provider onesignal --to ... [--dry-run]` sends one real message with credentials read from a chmod-600 file (`~/.config/whoobe/onesignal.env`); prints the request with the key masked, or the result and the provider's message id; never the key. Not part of any test run.
- Tests: core `http-transport.test.ts` (16), `providers.test.ts` (16), `mail-settings-crypto.test.ts` (5), `mail-settings.integration.test.ts` (12, disposable database: encryption at rest, blank keeps, backfill, no key, per-website precedence, one row per website, sending through the right configuration with a stub and the log); API `mail-settings.test.ts` (11), `unit/form-notify.test.ts` (website of an authored block, 20), browser `mail-settings.spec.ts` (a website gets its own OneSignal configuration from the screen and the key is never shown again). Whole suites on the throwaway stack: core 244 (+20 on a database), API 1199 / 32 files, browser 19, all passing. Nothing calls OneSignal in CI.
- Docs: new `docs/release/guides/mail-providers.md` (who sends what, OneSignal setup, secrets, adding a provider, live check, why several recipients are a gateway job), release notes (behaviour change: `MAIL_SETTINGS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`), F-054 in `findings.md`, status summary, `.env.example`, workspace guide.
- Not done: the real OneSignal service has not been called yet from here (the account's key is not shared; the live check is the way); attachments; more than one provider active at a time per scope; dropping the old plaintext column (a later migration, after every environment has been through the backfill).

## [Website previews at their own root preview path, not under /whoobe-one/] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Report: on the production CMS the website `whoobe website` has the root preview path `/whoobe-website`, its menus and links point to `/whoobe-website/{slug}`, but any such address showed an empty page; the pages only rendered at `/whoobe-one/whoobe-website/{slug}`, which broke every link and made the preview impossible to check. Expected: the whole site previewed under its root preview path, without `/whoobe-one/` as base, as on a local installation. (My previous change of this day, which prefixed the links with the editor's mount point, kept links working but left the address under `/whoobe-one/`; it stays for pages opened there.)
- Cause: behind the reverse proxy the dashboard owns `/` and the editor (which renders the pages) is mounted at `/whoobe-one/`; a root path is any name the user chooses, so the proxy had no way to send `/whoobe-website/...` to the editor, and the dashboard answered with an empty page. Locally the editor runs at the root of its own port, which is why it worked there.
- **Proxy** (`deploy/nginx/_locations.conf.template` and the tracked `_locations-ws-demo.inc`): `location /` now does `auth_request` to an internal location that asks core-api; 204 = the first segment is a website's root path -> proxied to the editor container (it answers `index.html` for any path, the app renders the page from the URL, the request URI untouched); 403 -> `@whoobe_dashboard`. `/assets/` goes straight to the dashboard, no question asked; the names routed by the other locations (the APIs, `/whoobe-one/`) are unchanged because they match before `/`. If core-api cannot be asked (5xx), the request goes to the dashboard, as it always did. The check location is `internal`.
- **core-api**: `GET /site-root` (public, in the route contract; called by nginx with `X-Original-URI`): `204`/`403` from a 5 s cache of the websites' root paths (`createSiteRootCache`, `matchSiteRoot`, `packages/core/src/routing/site-roots.ts`), cleared when a website is created, changed or deleted. A root path equal to a name the host answers to itself - the editor, the APIs, `assets`, `login`... (`RESERVED_ROOT_SEGMENTS`, kept in step with the proxy's regex by a test) - is refused with 400 when creating or changing a website; the check never answers 204 for such a name.
- **Editor** (`whoobe-one-spike`): the router's base is chosen at load (`chooseHistoryBase`): the mount point (`/whoobe-one/`) for its own screens, `/` when the page is opened outside it. Before, a page opened at `/whoobe-website/x` made the router rewrite the address bar to `/whoobe-one/whoobe-website/x` right after loading. The link prefixing of the previous change now applies only to pages opened under the mount point.
- **Dashboard**: the previews of a website's pages (Page > Preview, the external-link icon, Website > Paths) are built on the dashboard's own origin plus root path and slug (`getSitePreviewBaseUrl`, `utils/site-preview-url.ts`); the editing screens keep `/whoobe-one/`. `VITE_WHOOBE_ONE_URL`, if set, loses its `/whoobe-one` suffix for this purpose.
- **Existing workspaces** keep their rendered include. New `scripts/render-workspace-nginx.sh <slug> [--no-reload]` renders it again from the template and the workspace's own `.env.<slug>` (ports, `WS_MAX_BODY`, `WS_EDGE_LIMITS`), keeps the old file as `.bak`, shows the diff, runs `nginx -t` (restoring the old include if it fails) and reloads; it replaces the two hand edits made on the production host.
- Tests: core `site-roots.test.ts` (23); `unit/nginx-site-root.test.ts` (6, **a real nginx in Docker** - `nginx:alpine` and `nginx:1.18-alpine`, the version of the production host - with the real template and four stub upstreams: a website path goes to the editor with the URI untouched, the dashboard's routes and assets to the dashboard without asking core-api, `/whoobe-one/` and the APIs as before, the check is not reachable from outside, core-api down falls back to the dashboard); `unit/render-workspace-nginx.test.ts` (4); `unit/preview-links.test.ts` (+5), `unit/site-preview-url.test.ts` (3); `api/site-roots.test.ts` (13: reserved names refused on create and change, 204/403, immediate effect); browser `preview-links.spec.ts` (+1: a site opened at `/<root>/first` keeps that address, its links are followed as they are, in both element and text links); `authz-matrix` leaves `/site-root` out of its sweep like `/health` (it answers 403 on purpose). Whole suites on the throwaway stack: API 1177 tests / 31 files (one fixed, see above), browser 18, all passing.
- Docs: `operations/creating-workspaces.md` (new section "Website previews and root paths" with the upgrade command), `getting-started/manual/04-first-page.md` (where the preview is, reserved names; replaces the paragraph written earlier today), a behaviour-change row in the release notes, the route in `gen-api-docs.py` (199 routes).
- **Production updated** to `90ac322` (CI green on it, with DAST): database backup first, code copied with `git archive`, the five application containers rebuilt (healthy), then `./scripts/render-workspace-nginx.sh dashboard` (diff checked, `nginx -t`, reload) - the first use of the script, replacing the two hand edits of the include. From outside: `/whoobe-website/` and `/whoobe-website/blog` -> editor, `/`, `/login`, `/other-name/x` -> dashboard, `/auth/me` 401 and `/whoobe-one/` as before, `aura.example.com` unchanged. In a real browser (headless Chromium, anonymous) `https://dashboard.whoobe.dev/whoobe-website/` renders the home ("WhooBe Project", its menu), the address stays as typed, its 47 internal links are all `/whoobe-website/...`, and following `/whoobe-website/compare` stays there. Noted, not changed: the editor's page view asks authenticated endpoints for the theme (`/whoobe-one/themes/..`, `/whoobe-one/websites/..`), so an anonymous visitor of the preview gets those 401s and no theme; the preview is meant for a logged-in editor, the published site does not use it.

## [Docker build context: deploy/certbot is excluded (a rebuild of a workspace with HTTPS failed); production updated] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Updating the production workspace (`docker compose -p ws-dashboard --env-file .env.dashboard up -d --build`, the documented way to pick up a code change) stopped at once with `open .../deploy/certbot/conf/accounts: permission denied`. Cause: `enable-tls.sh` has certbot (root, in its container) write its state into `deploy/certbot/conf` inside the repository - account and certificate **private keys**, mode 700 - and the root `.dockerignore` did not exclude it, so the build context included a directory the build user cannot read. The first build of a workspace never met it (the directory does not exist yet); every rebuild after enabling HTTPS did. Nothing had been changed on the host when it failed (the running containers were untouched).
- Security side of the same line: had the build run as root, the private keys would have been copied into the build context and from there into image layers (the pattern of F-020 for `.env.*`). `deploy/certbot` is now in `.dockerignore`; `scripts/security/unit/docker-context.test.ts` (4) pins it together with `.env.*`, nested `.env` and `logs`.
- **Production updated** to `d71d580` + this change, after CI (push and manual run with DAST, both green on `d71d580`): backup of the production database first (`dashboard-before-update-2026-10-08.dump`, 42 tables checked), code copied with `git archive`, the five application containers rebuilt and recreated (core-api, asset-processor, gateway, admin-dashboard, whoobe-one-spike - healthy), Postgres and Garage untouched; data counts unchanged (12 pages, 5 Blog objects, 5 assets). `repairMissingFolders()` run inside the new asset-processor: 0 created (the folder `/images` had been created by hand meanwhile) and idempotent. Dashboard, editor, `/health` and `www.whoobe.dev` answer; `aura.example.com` unchanged.
- Fixes now live in production: DAM folder chain and repair on listing, lists refreshed after a website import, preview links kept inside the editor, the F-053 notification rule, the nginx body cap (already applied by hand), direct submit of forms in a static site, bundle bindings.

## [Editor preview: links of a page stay inside the editor when it is mounted under /whoobe-one/] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Report from the production CMS: a website imported with its root preview path `/whoobe-website`; the preview of its pages opened from *Website > Paths* is at `/whoobe-one/whoobe-website/...` (where does `/whoobe-one/` come from?), while going to `/whoobe-website/` shows an empty page with no error. Answer: `/whoobe-one/` is where the editor application is mounted in the Docker/nginx stacks (`VITE_BASE_URL=/whoobe-one/`, nginx `location /whoobe-one/`, `getWhoobeOneSpikeUrl()` in the dashboard); on a local development setup the editor runs at the root of its own port, which is why `/whoobe-website/` worked there. The root preview path is the part *after* the base; `/whoobe-website/` on the dashboard's host belongs to the dashboard, which has no such page and shows an empty one. Not a bug in the URL, but it exposed one:
- **Defect**: the links stored in a page are the site's own absolute paths, root path included (`/whoobe-website/blog`), and the preview used them as they are. Mounted under `/whoobe-one/`, following one left the editor and landed on the dashboard (the empty page); in local development, mounted at `/`, it never showed. New `src/preview-links.ts` (`prefixInternalHref`, `installPreviewLinkPrefix`): the page view (`views/PublicPage.vue`, what the Page preview and the Paths preview open) rewrites the `href` of the link under the pointer just before the browser uses it - hover, click, middle click, ctrl+click and "open in a new tab" - so every kind of link is covered (element links, links inside rich text, dynamic list-to-detail links), by one rule: an absolute path of the site that does not carry the base yet gets it (`/whoobe-website/blog` -> `/whoobe-one/whoobe-website/blog`); `https://`, `//cdn`, `#anchor`, `mailto:` and relative links are left alone; idempotent; nothing at all when the base is `/`.
- Tests: `scripts/security/unit/preview-links.test.ts` (14) and browser `preview-links.spec.ts`: a website with a root path and two pages, one linking to the other by an element link and by a link in the text; at `/whoobe-one/<root>/first` the `href` becomes `/whoobe-one/<root>/second`, the click lands there and the second page's text shows. Whole browser suite 17 passing.
- Docs: `getting-started/manual/04-first-page.md` explains where the preview lives (local `http://localhost:3006/default/about-us`, workspace `https://host/whoobe-one/default/about-us`) and that links are followed inside the editor.

## [DAM: the folder of an uploaded file always has a record; the dashboard refreshes its lists after a website import] — 2026-10-08
- Branch: `master`. Report after importing the website on the production CMS: assets and the Blog data object seemed not imported. The database said otherwise (12 pages, class Blog with its 5 published objects, 5 assets, 2 snippets, the template); two display problems, both found by looking at the production database and at how the dashboard builds its lists:
  1. **Assets invisible**: the DAM tree is built from the `folders` table, and uploading a file (`/api/v1/dam/upload`) stored the file and its record but never created the folder record. A file in `/images` therefore existed in the database and in the store and was shown nowhere. The dashboard's own upload flow creates folders first, which hid the gap; the import uploads straight to the paths of the original installation. `ensureFolderChain()` (`packages/core/src/db/asset-records.ts`, "mkdir -p": the folder and every ancestor, never the root, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, an existing record is left as it is) now runs in `upsertAssetRecord`, so every upload, move or processed copy leaves a complete chain. `repairMissingFolders()` creates the records that already-stored assets lack and runs when the folder list is requested (`GET /api/v1/dam/folders`), so assets stored before this fix show up with no migration step.
  2. **Stale lists**: after an import the dashboard refreshed only the websites list; classes, objects, snippets, components, pages, menus and the DAM folders/assets were whatever had been loaded at start, so the Blog class was missing from the Objects view until the page was reloaded. `WebsiteSettingsCard.vue` now refreshes all of them (`refreshAfterImport`, each independent so one failure does not hide the others).
- Tests: core `roundtrip.integration.test.ts` (+3: the chain is created once and the root is not a record, a customised folder is not touched, `repairMissingFolders` fills the gap and is idempotent; 8 in the file), `api/uploads.test.ts` (+1: uploading to a nested path puts the folder and its ancestors in `/api/v1/dam/folders` with the right parent and name), browser `website-bundle.spec.ts` (the imported file's folder is in the DAM tree). Whole suites on the throwaway stack: API 1128 tests / 25 files, browser 16, all passing.
- **Production**: the running production workspace still has the old asset-processor. Until it is updated, creating the folder `/images` in the dashboard (Assets > New folder) makes the five files appear; after the update `GET /api/v1/dam/folders` does it on its own.

## [CI: the MFA replay test no longer depends on the clock] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. The manual run with DAST on `c04237d` failed once in `api/mfa.test.ts > a code cannot be replayed on a second login` (401 where 200 was expected); the push run of the same commit, and every local run, passed. The test computed the TOTP code *before* the first-factor login, which takes seconds on a slow runner; when the 30 s window ended in between, the code was already stale. Not a product defect. The code is now computed after the first factor, right before it is used (`scripts/security/api/mfa.test.ts`); the other tests of the file already computed it at the call.

## [F-053: a public form's notification mail is sent only for a notify block an author saved] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Found while deciding how the public site should get its contact form: `POST /public/forms/:classId/submit` (anonymous by design) took the form's `notify` block - recipient, subject, mail template or template page - from the **visitor's request body**, so anyone could make the server send mail to an address and with a subject of their choosing through the installation's mail settings (a mail relay; only the 10 submissions per 15 minutes per client limited it). The plan `gateway-auth-authz.md` had named it a later task; it was never registered. Dormant until mail is configured, which it is not on the new production host.
- **Fix** (`apps/core-api/src/form-notify.ts`, wired into the route in `server.ts`): the block counts only if it equals one an author saved in a form of a page - same target class, template, template page, recipient and subject, read from the pages and cached 30 s. Anything else is ignored: the submission is stored and answers 201, a warning is logged. A recipient must be a single plain address (no list, display name, angle brackets or CR/LF) also when it comes from a template's `{form.email}`, and each address gets at most 3 mails an hour. The recipient is resolved with the same `{ form: ... }` context the mailer renders with (a first version resolved `{form.email}` against the bare fields and sent nothing; the API test caught it).
- **Behaviour change**: a form's notification works once its page is saved (published or not); a block that exists only in the browser's request, or belongs to a form that was removed, sends nothing. A confirmation template addressed to the visitor can still be pointed at a third party's address, within 3 an hour per address and 10 submissions per 15 minutes per client.
- Tests: `unit/form-notify.test.ts` (18) and `api/form-notify.test.ts` (5; mail settings point at a closed port, the mail log records every attempt: the authored block is attempted, an invented recipient/subject is not, a legacy template page and an unknown template id are not, a list and header injection are refused, the 4th and 5th mail to one address are refused). Whole suites on the throwaway stack: API 1127 tests / 25 files, browser 16, all passing.
- Docs: F-053 in `findings.md`, counts and a behaviour-change row in `status-summary.md`, a row in the release notes, the route description in `gen-api-docs.py` (api reference and OpenAPI regenerated).

## [nginx: one body cap for the whole server (a website import answered 413)] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Report: importing the exported website zip on the production CMS answered **413**. Cause: `client_max_body_size` was set only inside `location /api/v1/` (the asset upload route) of `deploy/nginx/_locations.conf.template`; every other location, core-api's included, fell back to nginx's own default of **1 MB**, so any JSON body above 1 MB sent to core-api was refused before reaching it - the manifest of a website import, but equally a large page save, an AI-generated page or a kit. Reproduced on the production host before the change (3 MB POST: 413 on `/whoobe-one/websites/import` and `/data-objects`, 401 on `/api/v1/dam/upload`).
- The directive now sits at server level, right after the optional edge limits (`__MAX_BODY__`, default 100M, `WS_MAX_BODY`; the sandbox keeps 6M); the location-level copy is gone. The tracked rendered copy `_locations-ws-demo.inc` got the same change. `scripts/security/unit/nginx-routing.test.ts` (+1): the directive is present once, before the first location, in the template and in the tracked copy.
- **Already provisioned workspaces keep their rendered `_locations-<slug>.inc`**: add `client_max_body_size 100M;` at the top (outside any `location`), delete the one inside `location /api/v1/`, `sudo nginx -t && sudo systemctl reload nginx` (or re-provision). Done by hand on the production workspace `dashboard`: the same 3 MB POST now reaches authentication (401); a 30 MB one still gets 413, now from core-api's own 25 MB JSON limit (as designed).
- Docs: size limits of a bundle (manifest 25 MB of JSON, per-asset `ASSET_MAX_FILE_BYTES`, the nginx cap) in `docs/release/guides/website-bundle.md`; a behaviour-change row in `docs/release/release-notes-0.1.0.md`.

## [Gateway forms on a static site: direct submit without a site key, bindings rebuilt on bundle import] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Question that started it: a form on the static site, one endpoint in the production Gateway - does the build still need the secret? `docs/plans/api-gateway/gateway-auth-authz.md` settles it: the **binding** (an opaque id to one fixed endpoint/route, the form's own fields only, per-address limits) is the mandatory protection; the **site key** is an optional second layer and is only a real secret because the generated site has a server route that holds it (`/api/whoobe-gateway-submit`). That plan covers sites with a server; it says nothing about the static export (Option C), which came later.
- **Defect found**: the generated Nuxt page always built the API client with the same-origin proxy path, so in a static export (nginx serving files, no Nitro) a Gateway form posted to a route that does not exist and could never submit, although the Option C text lists the Gateway as the one runtime dependency. Fixed in `packages/publish/src/cli.ts`: the proxy path is used only when the site was generated with `--site-api-key`; without a key the form posts straight to `--gateway-url` (the Gateway already answers any origin without credentials on `/api/v1/gateway/submit`, no CORS setting needed). `deploy-static.sh` warns when the site has a key (a static export has no server to hold it). Next is unchanged (it always has a server).
- **Bundle import** (`packages/core/src/portability/import.ts`): the endpoints are not in a bundle, and a binding row belongs to one website, so a form imported with its old `gatewayBindingId` pointed at nothing. The import now calls `bindPageGatewayContent` for each page with the new website id: bindings are made again where an endpoint with the form's name and route exists; where it does not, the stale id is dropped and the report (also the dry run) names the missing endpoint (`collectGatewayEndpointNames`).
- **Consequence for a site authored on one installation and served by another's Gateway**: the binding ids baked into the pages must belong to the Gateway the browser posts to. Generate the site from the installation that runs that Gateway (import the bundle there first, after creating the endpoint with the same name). Written down in `docs/release/guides/publish/website-publishing.md` (Option C, "Forms in a static site") and `docs/release/guides/website-bundle.md`.
- Tests: core `portability.test.ts` (+1) and `roundtrip.integration.test.ts` (+1: a form gets a binding of its own for the new website, a missing endpoint is reported, a stale binding id is dropped; core integration suite 5 + 15); `scripts/security/api/publish.test.ts` (+3: without a key the generated page passes `undefined` and never mentions the proxy, with a key it keeps it and the server route, the deploy script warns only with a key). Whole API suite on the throwaway stack: 1102 tests, 23 files, all passing.
- Correction of an earlier statement in this session: no `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is needed on the Gateway for a form posted from another domain; `/api/v1/gateway/submit` and `/resolve` are public paths in its CORS policy.

## [Markdown element: paste Markdown, it renders as HTML] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. New element **Markdown** (palette group *text*): its `content` is the Markdown *source*; the canvas, the preview mode and the published page show it as HTML.
- **Editor** (`apps/whoobe-one-spike`): `ElementsClass.js` `Markdown()` (a `div` with `tag: 'markdown'`, editable, a sample text, and Tailwind arbitrary-variant classes - `[&_h1]:text-3xl`, `[&_ul]:list-disc` ... - that restore the heading/list/quote styles the page reset removes; they are ordinary classes on the element, editable like any other); palette entry and icon, `friendlyElementName`. `BlockElement.vue` renders `renderMarkdown(content)` (the `marked` renderer the data-bound `markdown` fields already used) and is never `contenteditable` for it (a blur would write the rendered HTML over the source); `BlockElementPreview.vue` does the same. **Edit content** opens `MarkdownPanel.vue` in the toolbar: the source in a textarea (paste there) next to a live preview drawn with the element's own classes, sanitised like all rich text.
- **Published output** (`packages/core/src/gateway/sanitize-public-content.ts`, `marked` added to `@whoobe-cms/core`, lockfile +3 lines): when the public API serves a page, a node with `tag: 'markdown'` is converted to HTML, becomes an ordinary element (`tag: 'element'`) and goes through the usual rich-text sanitizer afterwards. So the React/Vue/Svelte adapters, Next/Nuxt projects and the mobile app need nothing of their own, and the editor's public route cannot convert twice. The stored page, `?editor=true` and the website export keep the Markdown source.
- Tests: core `sanitize-public-content.test.ts` (4 new: conversion, the caller's copy keeps the source, hostile Markdown/HTML is sanitised after the conversion, tables/code/empty) - core 183 passing; browser `markdown-element.spec.ts` (3): canvas renders h1/list/bold/link with the element's classes and is not editable in place, *Edit content* shows the source, a pasted replacement previews (script in the source does not run) and the canvas follows, the published `/public/page` carries `<h1>` and no `markdown` tag nor `onerror`/`javascript:` while the stored page keeps `# ...`, the palette chip (dragged) inserts a working element. Whole browser suite 16, `route-drift` and `public-surface` passing.
- Docs: new `docs/release/guides/markdown-element.md` (indexed in `docs/release/README.md`), mentions in the page-builder manual and in the SDK guide's kind table.
- Not done: the search index of a page holds the raw Markdown text (punctuation included); task-list checkboxes are not shown (inputs are not on the rich-text allow-list); the Studio/admin document previews (a different node shape) do not know the element.

## [Editor: elements hidden by `lg:hidden`, `md:hidden` ... stay visible and selectable on the canvas] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Report: a snippet put in a "Responsive Header Menu" and given `lg:hidden` disappeared from the canvas and could not be selected or edited again, while the preview rendered it correctly; only the bare `hidden` class was handled.
- Two causes. (1) `stripEditorHidden` (the canvas removes the classes that hide an element in the published view so it can still be edited, and shows a badge) compared whole class names with `hidden`, `invisible`, `sr-only`; a variant such as `lg:hidden` stayed, and Tailwind runs for real on the canvas, so it hid the element at the editor's width. It now looks at the utility after any variants (`lg:`, `max-md:`, `dark:`, `group-hover:`, `[&:hover]:`, `!hidden`, `hidden!`), moved to `src/editor-hidden.ts` (no imports; `helpers.ts` re-exports it). (2) In `BlockElement.vue` the "hidden" badge was a `v-else-if` branch of the chain that renders the element: a **leaf** element with a hiding class rendered only the badge (the same happened with the bare `hidden` before this change). `BlockContainer.vue` always had the badge as a separate element, which is why a container-rooted snippet showed up and a leaf did not. The badge is now a sibling `v-if` in `BlockElement.vue` too.
- The badge now says what hides the element (`lg:hidden`, `max-md:hidden`, `hidden`) and its title explains that it stays visible on the canvas so it can be edited. The published view and the preview are unchanged: the classes stay on the element.
- Tests: `scripts/security/unit/editor-hidden.test.ts` (6: variants, important marker, arbitrary variants, look-alikes such as `overflow-hidden`); browser `hidden-variants.spec.ts`: a container with `lg:hidden`, a leaf with `hidden` and a leaf with `max-md:hidden` are visible at 1400 px with the right badge, one can be selected (its classes show in Advanced), and the Tree lists them (the leaf case failed before the second fix). Whole browser suite passing (13).
- Not reproduced: the report said the Tree did not show the element. The Tree roots at the selected container, or at the page when nothing is selected, and has no class-based filter; with the element unselectable the usual way to reach it was missing. If the Tree still omits it after this, a page that does it is needed.

## [Fresh install on the Oracle host (arm64, dashboard.whoobe.dev): three script defects found and fixed] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. The manual *fresh install* gate of `release.sh check`, run on the Oracle host (`ssh oracle`: Ubuntu 22.04 aarch64, 4 cpu, 23 GB) following `docs/release/operations/creating-workspaces.md`: workspace `dashboard` (index 1) on **dashboard.whoobe.dev**, Garage as the store, nginx on the host (it also serves another site, which was left untouched), `--scheme https`, Cloudflare DNS only. Before it, the old non-Docker installation on that host was backed up (pg_dump, object-store data, old env files, vhost) and its Whoobe parts removed; Docker CE 29 and Compose v5 installed. All seven containers built on **arm64 at the first attempt** and came up healthy.
- Checked (guide, section 4): `https://dashboard.whoobe.dev` answers 200 with a trusted Let's Encrypt certificate (staging first, then the real one), HTTP redirects with 301, HSTS one year, `frame-ancestors 'none'`; seed admin logs in; an asset uploaded under `/public` is served to anonymous requests (200) and a private one is not (404, 200 with a session); Postgres and the S3 port answer on loopback only (S3 unreachable from outside); `renew-certs.sh` finds the certificate and a weekly cron runs it. Not repeated there: `pnpm security:isolation` (runs on throwaway stacks, green in CI).
- **Defects found by doing it** (all in the host-nginx path, which the project's own test stack never uses):
  1. `enable-tls.sh` and `provision-workspace.sh` created `deploy/certbot` with the caller's umask: with `umask 077` the host's nginx (`www-data`) could not read the ACME challenge and Let's Encrypt got a **403**. Now explicit modes: `deploy/certbot` and `webroot` 755, `conf` (keys) 700.
  2. Certbot runs as root in its container, so `deploy/certbot/conf/live` is root-owned, mode 700. `enable-tls.sh` tested the certificate with `[ -f ... ]` from the operator's account and answered "certbot produced no certificate" right after succeeding; worse, `renew-certs.sh` listed the same directory and, for an operator who is only in the `docker` group, answered **"no certificates to renew" every time**, so certificates would have expired after 90 days without a message. Both now ask the certbot container (`test -f`, `ls -A` through `--entrypoint`).
  3. With nginx on the host `enable-tls.sh` rendered `<slug>-ssl.conf` but only said "reload nginx": the HTTP vhost then redirected to a port nobody served. It now prints the `ln -sf` of the HTTPS vhost to run once; `creating-workspaces.md` (step 3) says so.
- Gaps noted, not changed: a workspace has **one** host name (no alias, no `www` -> apex redirect; the certificate is for that name only); the shared proxy container is not used when the host has nginx (correct, but untested on this host).
- Left to the owner on the host: `pm2 save --force` (the pm2 dump still lists the five removed apps and `pm2-ubuntu.service` would try to start them after a reboot; the action was refused by the permission system), the unused mailhog and PostgreSQL 14 packages, a broken apt source (`postgresql.org jammy-pgdg`), the old `whoobecms.example.com` certificate, and changing the seed admin password (it is in `~/provision-dashboard.log` and `.env.dashboard`, mode 600).
