## [Gateway presets are read per request: adding one no longer needs a restart] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Report: a new preset (`mailchimp.json`) was not in Integrations > Add Endpoint. Cause: `loadPresets()` ran once at gateway startup (the running gateway had started 23 minutes before the file existed). Asked for: a custom preset must be addable on a production gateway without restarting it, also for configurations that cannot be built from the dashboard.
- `apps/gateway/src/preset-loader.ts`: new `createPresetSource(extraPath)` returns a function the route calls on every `GET /api/v1/gateway/presets`; it stats the `*.json` files of both folders and reads them again only when one was added, removed or changed (mtime and size), otherwise it answers from memory. `createAdminRoutes` accepts the list or the function; `router.ts` passes the function. `loadPresets()` stays (same behaviour).
- **Behaviour change**: a preset in `GATEWAY_PRESETS_PATH` with the same `id` as a built-in one now *replaces* it (before, both were listed with the same id, and the picker keys on it); this is how an installation adapts a vendor without touching the product's files. A file that becomes malformed drops out of the list; the others stay. Plugins are code and still need a restart.
- `GATEWAY_PRESETS_PATH` is now in `.env.example` and the presets README explains hot-reload and the volume use. `scripts/security/unit/gateway-presets.test.ts` (7): built-ins and second folder, override by id, malformed/incomplete files, missing folder, add/change/remove without restart, cache while nothing changed.
- The Mailchimp preset itself is committed apart (`apps/gateway/presets/mailchimp.json`, written by the owner; it had been swept into the border-slider commit by `git add -A`, now split). Noted, not changed: its `baseUrl` lacks the account's datacenter (`https://{dc}.api.mailchimp.com/3.0`) and its route path uses `<audience_id>` where the README says `{placeholder}`.

## [Editor > Customize > Border: the sliders can give 1px, and their first step removes the class] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Report: the "all" border slider could not be set to `border` (1px), the lowest value reachable was `border-2`; same for top/right/bottom/left. Cause: the option lists started at `border` itself, which is also the position shown when no class is set, so moving the slider to its leftmost position fired no event (the value did not change); `border` could only be had by going up a step and coming back. Padding does not have this because its first step is `p-0`.
- First reply of the fix used `border-0` / `border-t-0` as the first step; the report that followed showed why that is wrong: next to a `border`, `border-t-0` zeroes the top edge only. The first step of the five border lists (`scripts/tw.classes.js`) is now `' '` - "no class", the neutral step margin already uses - so taking a slider to its start **removes** that class and sets nothing. `RangeControl.vue` shows `none` for that step (margin's neutral step shows it too now, it used to show a blank).
- Browser test `scripts/security/browser/border-slider.spec.ts`: from nothing the first step is `border`, then `border-2` (one width at a time), back to the start removes the class; with `border` on, the top slider goes to `border-t`, `border-t-2`, and back to none leaves only `border` with no `-0` class. Whole browser suite passing (12), vue-tsc clean.

## [Editor: colours made in TipTap are no longer lost when the paragraph is selected and another control is used] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Report: after changing the colour of part of a paragraph in TipTap, clicking Custom Classes (or any other control) left only the classes of the element. Reproduced in the browser before the fix (red span became black, nothing typed). Cause: the canvas paragraph is `contenteditable`, so selecting it gives it the focus, and `onBlur` in `BlockElement.vue` (and `onCellBlur` in `BlockTable.vue`) *always* wrote the DOM back into `element.content`; that DOM is the sanitized rendering of the content, and the write also removed every `style="..."` - exactly where TipTap puts text colour and highlight.
- A blur now commits only after an `input` event (something typed, pasted or cut), so focusing and leaving no longer rewrites anything. When an edit is committed, `dropForeignInlineStyles()` (new, `packages/sdk/schema/src/sanitize.ts`, 3 tests) keeps the inline styles the content already had (compared by declarations, not spelling) and drops the ones the browser added while editing (font-size, a pasted background ...), instead of removing all of them.
- Browser tests `scripts/security/browser/paragraph-colors.spec.ts` (2): select the paragraph and use the Custom Classes field, colour and highlight stay and the new classes apply (failed before the fix); typing still commits the text and keeps the colours. Whole browser suite 11 passing; schema package 29 tests.
- Not changed: the admin dashboard's own editors (`WysiwygEditor`, `MarkdownEditor`) and the "Edit HTML source" mode; a colour typed *by hand* into the canvas after an edit is not offered (there is none: colours come from the TipTap panel).

## [Website export/import as a bundle (version 2, .zip): snippets, module pages, data objects and asset files travel with the site] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. Request: an exported website must be a twin on another installation. Until now the export (version 1) lacked the snippets set in the templates, the module pages of the layouts and the data objects, and kept the asset paths without the files. Found while reading it: the theme of the website and of its pages was lost on import (the export wrote the theme *id*, the import looked it up by *name*), the template `locale` was not exported, and `published`/`workflowState` were not restored.
- **How it works** (`packages/core/src/portability/`, new): the export follows the references starting from the website's content instead of knowing the shapes: every string is scanned for ids (page ids in a layout, `sourceSnippetId`, `objectId`, `{ assetId }`, relation ids) and for asset paths (`image.url`, `/dam-asset/<key>`), and what it finds is added (snippets, module pages of other websites, themes, data objects, assets) and scanned in turn. Every class a `dataBinding` names comes with all its objects (what the site lists; `?dataObjects=referenced` leaves them out), its relation target classes and component types. On import every entity gets a new id *before* any row is written (`createWhoobePage`, `createWhoobeOneSnippet`, `createDataObject`, `createTheme`, `createWebsiteTemplate` accept an optional `id`), and every id found in a page, snippet, layout, menu or object is rewritten through one map; asset paths are rewritten only when a file had to be stored under another path.
- **Routes** (same two, no new route, route contract unchanged): `GET /whoobe-one/websites/:id/export` returns the version 2 manifest; `POST /whoobe-one/websites/import` accepts version 1 or 2, `?dryRun=1` writes nothing and returns the report (counts, class actions, asset plan `upload`/`reuse`/`conflict`, warnings), `assetMap` in the body maps old asset ids to the records the client stored. Classes and components are reused by name (differences in fields are reported), objects by (class, locale, key) and never overwritten; a failure removes everything the import created. **Publishing is gated**: `published` and the workflow state are restored only for a caller with `objects:publish` (`mayChangePublished`, same rule as the page routes), otherwise everything is a draft and the report says how many items were live.
- **Admin dashboard** (`utils/website-bundle.ts`, `WebsiteSettingsCard.vue`): Export builds a `.zip` (`bundle.json` + `assets/`, with or without the files); Import reads `.zip` or `.json`, shows what will happen (dry run), stores the missing files through the ordinary DAM upload (so permissions, limits and the sandbox caps apply; retries on the 429 of the edge limiter), then imports with the id mapping.
- **Docs**: new `docs/release/guides/website-bundle.md` (contents, import steps, permissions, API, how to check a twin), index in `docs/release/README.md`, route descriptions in `gen-api-docs.py` (api-reference and openapi regenerated).
- **Tests**: core 14 unit tests (`portability.test.ts`: id and path rewriting, dependency walk with fake resolvers, asset plan, class diff, v1 parsing) and `roundtrip.integration.test.ts` (4, against a disposable database via `PORTABILITY_TEST_DATABASE_URL`; skipped otherwise): export, import into an empty installation, export again, and the two bundles are equal once ids are numbered by appearance; collision of an asset path, reuse on a second import, drafts without permission, rollback on a failing step. Security suite `api/portability.test.ts` (8: permissions, dry run, references rewritten, drafts vs published, v1) and browser `website-bundle.spec.ts` (export from the UI, the file in the zip equals the uploaded one byte for byte, asset deleted, import from the UI, asset back with a new id and the page linked to it). Whole suites on the throwaway stack: API 1086 tests, browser 9, all passing; core 179 + 4.
- **Behaviour changes**: the export file is now a `.zip` (the old `.json` is still accepted on import); an imported website's pages keep their published state only for users with `objects:publish`; module pages used by a layout are attached to the imported website. Not copied: users, gateway endpoints and keys, mail/AI settings, domains, Pimcore ids, page version history.

## [CI: the TLS template test renders the new nginx placeholders] — 2026-10-07
- Branch: `master`. The `security` workflow has been red on every push since the sandbox commit (`725ac91`): both `TLS template (testssl.sh)` jobs failed with "no Strict-Transport-Security header". Cause: `_locations.conf.template` gained `__MAX_BODY__` and `__EDGE_LIMITS__`, `scripts/security/tls/run-testssl.sh` did not substitute them, nginx refused the config (`unknown directive "__EDGE_LIMITS__"`) and exited, so testssl found nothing to test. The script now renders them like `provision-workspace.sh` does by default (`100M`, no edge limits). Not a product problem.
- Checked locally: `nginx:alpine` and `nginx:1.18-alpine` both PASS (TLS 1.2/1.3 only, HSTS 365 days). The other four jobs of the failed run were green. The CI gate of `release.sh check` still needs a green run on the final commit and a manual DAST run.

## [Asset proxy repairs its S3 client after a connection timeout; reset.sh takes a lock and cleans up after itself] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Two problems met in the same evening.
- **Asset proxy 500s on localhost** (`/dam-asset/...` answered 500 after exactly 5 s: the connection timeout towards MinIO), the third time (21 Sept, this morning, this evening). MinIO and the network were fine (3-8 ms from curl and from a fresh Node process); only the long-running dev process was broken, and a restart cured it. Cause of the broken state not found (`keepAlive: false` is already on). New `packages/core/src/dam/self-healing-client.ts` (`isConnectTimeout`, `createSelfHealingClient`, exported from `@whoobe-cms/core/server`): after a connection timeout the S3 client of the proxy is rebuilt (the old one destroyed), a warning is logged and the call is repeated once; other errors (a missing key) are not retried, a second timeout is reported, calls that fail together rebuild it once. `apps/asset-processor/src/server.ts` creates `s3ClientForProxy` with it; the call sites are unchanged. 9 new unit tests (core: 165 passing).
- **`scripts/sandbox/reset.sh`**: on `develop` the systemd service stalled twice and left `docker compose up --build` clients behind (a snap Docker client is not in the service's cgroup, so the timeout did not stop it), and the next attempts waited on them. Now: `flock` so a second reset exits at once; at start it stops a leftover `docker compose -p ws-<slug> ... up` (matched from the start of the command line, so a shell, an ssh session or `logs -f` that only mentions the project is left alone); on SIGTERM/INT/HUP it terminates its whole process tree; the long steps run as background jobs plus `wait`, because bash runs a trap only after the foreground command ends (a first version of the trap waited for the whole build). Tested with stub scripts: second instance exit 1, TERM stops provisioning and its child at once (exit 143), orphan killed, bystanders alive.
- `docs/release/operations/sandbox.md` step 5 describes it. Not run: the real service on the server (needs a sandbox reset, which deletes its data).

## [Sandbox reset unit: no hard dependency on docker.service] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Starting the service on the `develop` server failed with `Unit docker.service not found`: Docker there is the snap, whose service is `snap.docker.dockerd.service`. `whoobe-sandbox-reset.service` now orders after both names (`After=`) and no longer says `Requires=docker.service`; it wants `network-online.target` (the build downloads packages).

## [Sandbox reset service: timeout and retry in the unit, host-specific files as examples] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Installing the timer on the `develop` server showed three things to know in advance: the example units point to `/opt/whoobe_project` while the clone can be elsewhere, the service did not pass `--scheme http` (so a LAN host would try https and fail), and an existing `/etc/whoobe-sandbox.env` had a misspelled variable name that is silently ignored. The `pnpm install` step of an image build also stalled twice on that host (ECONNRESET from the npm registry) and had to be killed by hand.
- `scripts/sandbox/systemd/whoobe-sandbox-reset.service`: `TimeoutStartSec=15min`, `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=60` (a stalled build is killed and retried). New `override.conf.example` (user and repository path of the host, as a systemd drop-in) and `scripts/sandbox/whoobe-sandbox.env.example` (every variable, with comments).
- `reset.sh`: the scheme can come from `SANDBOX_SCHEME` (default https; `--scheme` still wins), so the unit needs no argument. `docs/release/operations/sandbox.md` step 5 rewritten around the three files.
- Not run here: the units themselves (installing them needs `sudo` on the server).

## [Sandbox = a workspace with a profile: the limits are variables of its env file] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Request: configure the sandbox like a workspace, with the limits as environment variables, and write the start-up procedure.
- **`provision-workspace.sh --profile NAME --set KEY=VALUE ...`**: `--profile` appends `deploy/profiles/NAME.env` to the workspace's `.env.<slug>` (and installs `deploy/profiles/NAME.nginx-http.conf` as `deploy/nginx/00-NAME-zones.conf` if present); `--set` (repeatable) is applied last. `--set` values are validated (upper-case key; no `|`, `&`, `\`, `$` or control characters: the value goes through `sed` and a compose env file); an unknown or path-like profile name is refused. Nginx's body cap and edge limits are read from the env file (`WS_MAX_BODY`, `WS_EDGE_LIMITS`) instead of the calling shell.
- **`deploy/profiles/sandbox.env`**: `COMPOSE_FILE` (activates the hardening overlay: Compose reads it from the env file, so `remove-workspace.sh` and any `docker compose --env-file` command on the workspace use it without exports), `SANDBOX_MODE`, `ASSET_MAX_FILE_BYTES`, `ASSET_MAX_COUNT`, `ASSET_MAX_INPUT_PIXELS`, `SANDBOX_ALLOW_HOSTS`, the public rate limits, `WS_MAX_BODY`, `WS_EDGE_LIMITS`, `SANDBOX_NO_NEW_PRIVILEGES`. `docker-compose.sandbox.yml` is now hardening only (its `environment` block is gone: the product variables reach core-api, asset-processor and gateway through their `env_file`).
- **`scripts/sandbox/reset.sh`** is a thin wrapper: remove the workspace, provision it with `--profile sandbox`, pass the operator's overrides (`SANDBOX_NO_NEW_PRIVILEGES`, `SANDBOX_ALLOW_HOSTS`, `ASSET_MAX_*`) as `--set`, wait for `/health`; same fail-closed behaviour.
- **Docs**: `docs/release/operations/sandbox.md` rewritten as the procedure (variable table, steps 1-6, reset, limits of the design); pointer in `creating-workspaces.md`.
- Checked: a local run of the profile-based reset (index 9): the env file carries the profile, `verify.sh` all green, containers read-only with no capabilities, `ws-sandbox` removed completely by `remove-workspace.sh` (0 containers, 0 volumes). Unit tests for hostile `--profile`/`--set` arguments (24 tests in `workspace-scripts.test.ts` and the 2 routing tests pass).

## [Sandbox: outbound HTTP only to an allow-list of hosts (`SANDBOX_ALLOW_HOSTS`)] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Request: allow the "Fake Products" gateway endpoint (`https://dummyjson.com`, dummy data) in the sandbox to demonstrate the API Gateway, with an environment variable for the permitted APIs.
- `safeFetch` no longer refuses everything in the sandbox: it refuses every host that is not in `SANDBOX_ALLOW_HOSTS` (exact names, lower-cased; no wildcards or sub-domains; an IP only if listed verbatim), checked on every redirect hop and before the DNS lookup, so a redirect cannot leave the list. A listed host still goes through the ordinary policy (no private/loopback/link-local addresses, size and time limits). `docker-compose.sandbox.yml` passes `SANDBOX_ALLOW_HOSTS` (default `dummyjson.com`) to core-api, asset-processor and gateway; empty turns outbound HTTP off. `sendMail` still refuses (mail is off).
- Checked beforehand that the gateway's `trusted` fetch path (no `safeFetch`) is used only by `router.ts` for this deployment's own service URLs, never for operator-defined endpoints, so the list cannot be bypassed from a gateway endpoint.
- Tests: `sandbox.test.ts` (parsing, exact match, look-alike hosts, IPs) and `safe-fetch.test.ts` (unlisted hosts refused with no DNS lookup; a listed host that resolves to a private address is still refused). Cloudinary, Brevo and CDN mirroring stay unusable by design.

## [nginx: /whoobe-one/settings/* is routed to core-api] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Reported on the sandbox: Settings > DAM Configuration > CDN Mirroring showed `Unexpected token '<', "<!doctype"... is not valid JSON`. Cause: the nginx locations template sends only `websites|themes|pages|snippets|templates|kits|website-paths|search` under `/whoobe-one/` to core-api; `settings` was missing, so every `/whoobe-one/settings/*` call (CDN mirror, logging, autosave, strip-html-comments, default placeholder image, and the Alpine presets catalog) got the editor's HTML. Not a sandbox problem: any deployment with this template had it, `ws-demo` included. Added `settings` to `deploy/nginx/_locations.conf.template` and to the tracked rendered copy `deploy/nginx/_locations-ws-demo.inc`; already-provisioned workspaces keep the old regex in their `_locations-<slug>.inc` until it is changed there too (or the workspace is re-provisioned) and the proxy reloaded.
- New test `scripts/security/unit/nginx-routing.test.ts`: every `/whoobe-one/<prefix>` in the route contract must be in the regex of both files.
- In the sandbox that call is then answered 403 "disabled in the public sandbox", by design: CDN mirroring and a Cloudinary gateway need outbound HTTP, which the sandbox refuses.

## [Sandbox verify.sh deletes its test assets] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Reported on the `develop` sandbox: a normal upload under 5 MB answered "Asset limit reached" with no visible assets. Cause: `scripts/sandbox/verify.sh` uploads 50 files into `/sandbox-verify` to reach the limit and left them there (the DAM shows the root folder), so the sandbox was at its 50-asset limit until the next reset. The script now deletes `/sandbox-verify` on exit (`DELETE /api/v1/dam/folders`). The 50 files on `develop` were removed by the same call; a second run leaves 0 assets.

## [Sandbox verify.sh: pace the uploads under the nginx rate limit] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. On the `develop` server the 60 back-to-back uploads of `scripts/sandbox/verify.sh` hit the nginx limit (10 requests/s, burst 40) at upload 41 and got 429 before the asset limit (409) could be tested. The loop now waits 0.2 s per upload. Sandbox run on `develop` (host `sandbox.whoobe.local`, index 7, `SANDBOX_NO_NEW_PRIVILEGES=false`): all checks pass.
- Found while running it there: index 9 collided with another service on port 9090 (Garage's S3 port), and `docker compose build` stalled once on an npm download (ECONNRESET); both are environment issues, nothing in the code.

## [Sandbox: `no-new-privileges` made configurable (snap Docker)] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. First run on the `develop` server failed: Postgres restarted with `exec /usr/local/bin/docker-entrypoint.sh: operation not permitted`. Reproduced with a bare `docker run --security-opt no-new-privileges:true` (fails) and without the option (works): the snap-installed Docker (AppArmor) refuses the exec. `reset.sh` failed closed as designed (stack removed).
- `docker-compose.sandbox.yml`: `no-new-privileges:${SANDBOX_NO_NEW_PRIVILEGES:-true}` on every service; default unchanged. Documented in `docs/release/operations/sandbox.md`.

## [Public sandbox: limits, disabled outbound features, hardened stack, 3-hourly rebuild] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Design agreed in plan mode: shared demo account, AI/mail/outbound/publishing off, dedicated host, full rebuild every 3 hours with a banner. Documented in `docs/release/operations/sandbox.md`.
- **Product** (`packages/core/src/sandbox.ts`, new, with tests): `SANDBOX_MODE`, `ASSET_MAX_FILE_BYTES` (also usable outside the sandbox; default stays 50 MB), `ASSET_MAX_COUNT`, `SANDBOX_RESET_AT`. asset-processor: per-file cap from the variable, new `countAssets()`, a new asset (or `_edited` copy) over the count limit answers 409, JSON body 1 MB in the sandbox. core-api: `/ai/*`, `/mail-*`, Figma, migration sources, CDN mirror, PWA generation and gateway-key regeneration answer 403 in the sandbox; `/health` carries a `sandbox` block (no new route, route contract unchanged). `sendMail` and `safeFetch` refuse in the sandbox.
- **Admin dashboard**: `SandboxBanner.vue` (in `App.vue`) shows the time of the next reset and the limits when `/health` has the sandbox block.
- **Stack**: `docker-compose.sandbox.yml` (memory/CPU/pids limits, no capabilities, `no-new-privileges`, read-only root with tmpfs for `/tmp` and `/app/logs` on the Node services). `scripts/sandbox/reset.sh` removes the workspace with its volumes and provisions it again with new secrets (fails closed: on error the stack is removed); systemd units in `scripts/sandbox/systemd/`; `scripts/sandbox/verify.sh` checks a running sandbox from outside.
- **Edge**: `deploy/nginx/_locations.conf.template` got `__MAX_BODY__` and `__EDGE_LIMITS__` (empty/100M by default, so existing workspaces are unchanged); `provision-workspace.sh` renders them from `WS_MAX_BODY` / `WS_EDGE_LIMITS`; the reset script sets 6M, per-address request/connection limits.
- Checked: core 156 tests, typechecks of core-api, asset-processor, admin-dashboard, `check-docs.py`. A real sandbox was built and run locally (index 9, `sandbox.localhost`): `verify.sh` all green (5 MB+1 byte -> 413, 51st asset -> 409, AI/mail/Figma -> 403), container flags confirmed, 429 under rapid requests, a second reset changed the secrets, `ws-demo` unaffected (401 on `/auth/me`). The first attempt failed on the read-only root (log directory) and the script correctly removed the stack. The demo account may read users and roles but not change them (and cannot change its own MFA), so visitors cannot lock each other out: `isSandboxReadOnlyPath` in `packages/core/src/sandbox.ts`, answered 403 by core-api. Not done: the firewall/egress rules and TLS on a real host (to try when it is on a public server).

## [F-051 closed: the public website route returns an allow-list of `settings`] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. `GET /public/websites/:id` (no authentication) no longer returns the whole administration `settings` blob (logging, autosave, CDN mirror, Alpine presets, ...): only `themes` and `defaultThemeId` (`PUBLIC_WEBSITE_SETTINGS` in `apps/core-api/src/server.ts`). A setting added later is private by default.
- Checked the consumers first: the publish CLI and the mobile-app generator read themes from the separate top-level fields (`themes`, `defaultThemeId`) and the website columns (`name`, `rootPath`, locales, `pwaConfig`), never from `settings`; nothing they use changed. `GET /public/websites` already listed only id and name.
- Tests: `alpine-presets.test.ts` and `public-surface.test.ts` assert that no other key of `settings` is returned; run with `public-surface`, `alpine-presets` and `route-drift` against the rebuilt security stack (40 tests, all passing). Findings register and status summary updated (F-051 fixed).

## [Audio waveform: private assets, long tracks, and why it fell back to the plain player] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Report: in the mobile frame of the website preview a waveform player showed the waveform for a few seconds and then turned into the standard player; the browser console showed `GET http://localhost:3002/dam-asset/audio/76bpm-Amin.mp3 500`. It had worked before the title change.
- **What the 500 was: not the player, not the title.** `/dam-asset/audio/76bpm-Amin.mp3` answered 500 after exactly 5 s (`@smithy/node-http-handler ... did not establish a connection ... within 5000 ms`) to full and `Range` requests alike, while the object exists in the dev MinIO (HeadObject with the same SDK from a plain Node process: 17 249 712 bytes, audio/mpeg, 29 ms). The 5 s is the "few seconds". The dev servers the browser was talking to were the ones I had left running in the background after earlier tests, started with `stdbuf -oL` (to read their logs) and, for core-api, asset-processor and gateway, restarted by `tsx watch` at 10:29. Restarting them as `./dev-cms.sh` normally does: the same URL answers 200 in about 60 ms and 206 for ranges. I could NOT reproduce the S3 timeout with the SDK, with or without the `LD_PRELOAD` of stdbuf, so the exact cause of that process's state is not established; it is an environment fault of the processes I started, and the title/accordion/class changes do not touch loading. With healthy servers the real 17 MB file keeps its waveform for 18 s in the canvas and 12 s in the mobile preview iframe, title included, no page errors.
- **A real latent bug found while checking, fixed.** `loadPeaks()` fetched without credentials and retried with them only when the network failed, not when the server answered 401/403/404. A **private** asset of the library (anonymous requests get 404, the `<audio>` element sends the session cookie and plays) therefore always fell back to the plain player even though the file was reachable. Now it tries with the credentials first and without them next, and also on HTTP errors (CORS allows credentials only for allowed origins, a third-party `*` refuses them, so both orders are needed). Reproduced first with a local server that answers only with the cookie (fell back), then fixed: the private asset, a public URL with `*`, and the real 17 MB mp3 draw the waveform; a URL without CORS falls back.
- **Long tracks.** The track is now decoded at 8 kHz (`OfflineAudioContext(1, 1, 8000)`) instead of 44.1 kHz: 800 peaks do not need more, and it is about a fifth of the memory (an 18-minute stereo track is roughly 70 MB of samples instead of 380 MB), which is what decides whether a long track can be drawn. The real 17 MB mp3: waveform in 2.6 s.
- **Diagnosable fallback.** When the waveform cannot be computed the console now says why (`[AudioPlayer] waveform unavailable, showing the plain player: HTTP 404 / blocked by the browser (CORS) or no connection / audio file too large to draw ...`) plus the URL. The guide lists the private-asset case. `vue-tsc` clean.
- The dev servers are now running the normal way (no stdbuf) from this session's background shell, output in `logs/dev-cms.log`; to take them over from your terminal: `./dev-cms.sh --stop` then `./dev-cms.sh`.

## [Audio settings are a collapsible section of Advanced] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. The Audio settings were a block always open under the accordion of Advanced (Custom Classes, Style, Attributes, Animation, AlpineJS), which are all foldable. They are now a conditional accordion entry of the same list, like "Field" for checkboxes and selects: `AdvancedPanel.vue` adds `{ key: 'audio', label: 'Audio' }` first when the selected element is an audio, opens it when an audio element is selected, and falls back to Custom Classes when the selection moves to an element without the section. `AudioControl.vue` lost its own header and frame (the accordion provides them). Same behaviour as the other sections: one section open at a time, so opening Style folds Audio.
- Checked in the dev editor: no Audio section for a paragraph (Custom Classes open); selecting the audio opens Audio by itself and folds Custom Classes; sections in order Audio, Custom Classes, Style, Attributes; the header folds and unfolds it; opening Style folds it; a change (Loop) survives fold and unfold; back to the paragraph the section is gone and Custom Classes opens; selecting the audio again reopens Audio; no page errors. `vue-tsc` clean.

## [Audio title: style it with Tailwind classes or inherit the element's] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. The title of the Audio element had a fixed look (`text-sm font-medium opacity-80 mb-1`) that ignored the element's own classes. New option **Title style** (shown while the title is on): `default` (the old look; the colour is still inherited), `inherit` (the title has no style of its own, so it takes the font, size, weight, colour and line height of the Audio element, set in Advanced > Custom Classes; only `mb-1` for the gap) and `custom` (the Tailwind classes typed in `titleClass`, applied as they are; the runtime Tailwind engine generates the CSS, so any class and any arbitrary value works). Truncation (`truncate` / `break-words`) is added separately and keeps working with every style. Options `titleStyle` and `titleClass`; `audioTitleClasses()` in `scripts/audio-waveform.ts`.
- `titleClass` is cleaned (`cleanClasses()`): `<`, `>`, `"`, backtick, backslash and control characters removed, spaces collapsed, duplicates dropped, 300 characters at most; it reaches the DOM through a class binding, which cannot break out of the attribute, so this is hygiene, not the defence. Found while testing: the panel stored the value as typed (it normalized the other options and then overwrote with the raw one); it now normalizes the whole result when writing, so what is saved is already clean, and the height is saved clamped to 16-240 as well.
- Checked in the dev editor on an element with `w-64 text-xl font-bold text-red-600`: default = 14 px, weight 500, opacity 0.8, red; inherit = 20 px, weight 700, red, opacity 1; custom empty = inherits everything; custom `text-2xl italic text-emerald-600 mb-3` = 24 px, italic, emerald, 12 px margin, still one line cut with an ellipsis; same in the preview; the field appears only for `custom` and the hint only for `inherit`; options round-trip through JSON; markup typed in the field is stored as plain tokens. The pure functions were run under Node (defaults, junk values, cleaning, the 300-character cap, a non-string). `vue-tsc` clean. Still no title in the SDK adapters and the mobile shell.

## [Audio element: optional title (file name) with truncation] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Two new options of the Audio element: `showTitle` (default off) shows the file name above the player, in both looks, `truncateTitle` (default on, only meaningful with the title) keeps it to one line with an ellipsis; off lets it wrap. The name comes from the element's stored source (`image.url`, not the resolved URL, so it is stable): the last path segment without query, hash and extension, percent-decoded (`/audio/My%20Song.mp3` -> `My Song`; a final dot followed by 1-5 letters or digits is the extension, so `v1.2 final` keeps its dot); `audioTitleFromSource()` in `scripts/audio-waveform.ts`. The full name is the tooltip (`title` attribute) and the accessible name (`aria-label`) of the `<audio>`. Panel: two checkboxes in the Audio section of Advanced, the second only shown while the title is on.
- Not shown by the mobile-app shell and the SDK adapters (they render the plain `<audio>` only); stated in the guide. No custom title text yet: the name is always the file name.
- Checked in the dev editor with an element 192 px wide and a 60-character name: off by default; on shows `A very long track name that keeps going and going and going` (decoded, no `.wav`); with truncation it is one line (20 px) with `text-overflow: ellipsis` and `white-space: nowrap` and the text is cut; without it the title wraps to 3 lines (60 px); also present in the waveform look and in the preview; switching it off removes the title and the `aria-label`; the options round-trip through JSON; no page errors. The pure function was run under Node on paths, URLs with query and hash, stray `%`, empty and null. `vue-tsc` clean.

## [Audio element: player or player with waveform, from an asset or a URL] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. New editor element **Audio** (palette > Media). Source: a DAM asset or any URL (stored in `image.url` like Image and Video, resolved by `resolveDisplayUrl`). Look: `mode: 'player'` (the browser's `<audio controls>`) or `mode: 'waveform'` (play button, waveform of the track with the played part in a second colour, elapsed/total time; click or drag to seek, arrow keys +-5 s, Space/Enter to play and pause). Other options in `element.options`: `controls`, `loop`, `autoplay`, `preload`, `waveColor`, `progressColor`, `height`. Guide: `docs/release/guides/audio-element.md`.
- **Editor.** `ElementsClass.js` `Audio()` + the Media group entry + palette icon; `components/AudioPlayer.vue` (one component for the canvas, the preview and the public page, so the editor shows what visitors get); `scripts/audio-waveform.ts` (options with defaults and a colour whitelist, `computePeaks`, a stable placeholder, `loadPeaks`: fetch + `OfflineAudioContext.decodeAudioData`, cached per URL, 800 buckets, 40 MB limit); `advanced/AudioControl.vue` (source field with a picker, look, behaviour, waveform colours and height) shown in Advanced for audio elements; `MediaPickerModal` has an `accept` prop (by MIME type) and the Audio panel and the toolbar's "Browse assets" button use it. In the canvas a click on the audio selects it and never deselects it (own `onAudioClick`), so it can be played and scrubbed while editing.
- **Fallback.** If the waveform cannot be computed (a URL of another site without CORS headers, an undecodable format, a file over 40 MB) the plain player is shown: the sound is never lost.
- **Other renderers.** SDK: `normalizeKind` returns `audio`, `resolveNodeImage` resolves the source of an audio node like an image's (otherwise the generated Next/Nuxt sites would have rendered an unknown node as an empty container); React/Vue/Svelte get a plain `<audio>` default (`controls`, `loop`, `autoplay`, `preload` from `options`; a waveform node is marked `data-whoobe-audio="waveform"` for a project's own component). Mobile-app generator: an `<audio>` branch in both templates (plain player), audio excluded from the background-image logic. Not done: the waveform in the SDK adapters and the mobile shell; the AI page generator does not know the element.
- **Bugs found by testing in a browser while building it** (all fixed before the commit): `BlockElementPreview.vue`'s `displaySrc` resolved only `img` and `video`, so the preview had no source for audio; a comment placed before the root `<div>` of `AudioPlayer.vue` made Vue (development mode) treat it as a fragment, so `id`/class/style did not reach the element and Alpine's `initTree` received a text node; clicking the player in the canvas toggled the selection off.
- **Verified** with a generated 8 s WAV (quiet first half, loud second half) served with and without CORS, in Chromium on the dev editor: the palette lists Audio; player mode renders `<audio controls>` with the source; waveform mode draws the real peaks (6 px vs 64 px bars for the quiet and loud halves); Play/seek (a click at 75% lands at 6.2 s of 8)/Pause, element stays selected; height and colours reach the canvas; the preview shows the waveform; a no-CORS URL falls back to the plain player; `options` and `image.url` survive a JSON round trip; the picker (mocked DAM with an image and two audio assets) lists only audio for an audio element and everything for a paragraph; no page errors. Unit: `computePeaks`/`normalizeAudioOptions` run under Node (silence, empty input, a colour with injected CSS rejected, height clamped); SDK tests: renderer 46 (kind test added), React 4 and Vue 5 (an Audio render test each), `svelte-check` 0 errors; `vue-tsc` clean for the editor; the new mobile template line compiles with the Vue SFC compiler. The editor app has no test runner, so the browser checks are scripts and are not part of CI.

## [CI fix: four new dependency advisories (vue 3.5.43, prosemirror-view, proxy-addr, source-map-js)] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Run 37426871093 (commit a36ff35): isolation and both TLS jobs green; `pnpm audit` and `trivy image` failed on four advisories published after the previous green run, the same four in both: `proxy-addr` 2.0.7 (CRITICAL, IP spoofing through an IPv4-mapped IPv6 trust subnet; via express; fixed in 2.0.8), `@vue/server-renderer` 3.5.40 (HIGH, XSS through a gap in the attribute-name blacklist; fixed in 3.5.42), `prosemirror-view` 1.42.2 (HIGH, XSS in the paste handling; used by the TipTap editor; fixed in 1.42.3) and `source-map-js` 1.2.1 (HIGH, event-loop denial of service; fixed in 1.2.2). Not a regression of our code.
- Fix, the project's usual way for transitive advisories (`pnpm.overrides` in the root `package.json`, like `qs` and `postcss`): `proxy-addr ^2.0.8`, `source-map-js ^1.2.2`, `prosemirror-view ^1.42.3`, `vue ^3.5.42`, `@vue/server-renderer ^3.5.42` (`vue` and its renderer must move together). No `package.json` range of a package changed, and only these subtrees changed in `pnpm-lock.yaml`: the Vue family 3.5.40 -> 3.5.43, prosemirror-view 1.42.6 (+ prosemirror-model), proxy-addr 2.0.8, source-map-js 1.2.2. An earlier attempt with `pnpm update -r` also re-resolved unrelated packages (browserslist, rollup, brace-expansion...) and rewrote the `vue` ranges of seven packages: dropped for the smaller change.
- Checked on a clean copy of origin/master: `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm build` (19 tasks), `scripts/security/static` audit summary ok (only the baselined `xlsx` x2 and `braces`), tests of core (153), schema (26), renderer (45), react (3) and vue (4), `vue-tsc` of the editor. The `trivy image` step runs in CI.

## [Alpine transitions did nothing: the editor quoted the class lists] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. Report: a preset with the six `x-transition:*` directives rendered but had no effect. The rendered element carried `x-transition:enter="'transition ease-out duration-1300'"`, with single quotes inside the attribute. Two causes, both reproduced with the real Alpine in a browser:
- **Bug (old, in the editor): `safeAlpineValue()` quoted the class lists.** It wraps in quotes any directive value that does not parse as JavaScript, a safeguard written for expressions such as `x-bind:class="h-[600px]"`. The values of `x-transition:*` are lists of CSS classes, not expressions, so `transition ease-out duration-300` became a string literal with quotes and Alpine applied **no class at all** (mid-transition classes: empty with the quotes, `transition ease-out duration-500 opacity-100 scale-100` without). Any `x-transition:*` typed by hand was affected, not only presets, in the editor preview, the public page, and the mobile-app shell. New `isLiteralAlpineDirective()` (`x-transition:*`, `x-teleport`, whose value is a selector such as `#modals`, and `x-ref`) and `safeAlpineValue(value, name)` returns those values untouched; the name is passed by the six rendering components, `PublicPage.vue` and the four copies of the helper inside `packages/mobile-app/src/cli.ts` (template strings, no backslashes added). Checked in the editor preview with Alpine running: the attribute is now `transition ease-out duration-700` and the mid-transition classes include the transition ones. Pages need no change: the stored values were always the plain class lists.
- **Placement (not a bug, but easy to miss).** In the reported HTML the transition directives were on a container and `x-show="menu"` on its child. Alpine animates an element only through its own `x-show`, so the child got no transition classes in the test either. The Alpine panel now shows an amber warning when an element has `x-transition` directives but no `x-show`, and the guide has a note on it. A reminder for that example: `menu` must be defined by an `x-data` on the element or a parent (not visible in the pasted fragment).
- `vue-tsc` clean; `packages/mobile-app` builds. My earlier browser check of the presets feature only verified that the attribute was present in the preview, not what Alpine did with it: that is how this passed.

## [Alpine presets: named sets of directives applied from the editor] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. An administrator can define a named set of Alpine.js directives once (example: the six `x-transition:*` attributes of a fade-and-scale effect) and apply it to any element from **Advanced > AlpineJS > Presets**, instead of retyping the directives. Guide: `docs/release/guides/alpine-presets.md`.
- **Design.** Applying a preset COPIES its directives into `element.alpine`; the catalog is only a library. So the editor preview, the public pages served by whoobe-one and the mobile-app shell keep rendering from `element.alpine` alone, no renderer changed, a page never depends on the catalog, and renaming or deleting a preset cannot break a page (nor does editing it update pages that used it: apply again). The catalog is installation-wide and lives in the first website's `settings.alpinePresets`, the same place and pattern as `autosave` and `cdn-mirror` (no migration; each workspace is its own installation). Not done: a reference-by-name directive (`x-whoobe-preset`) resolved at render time, which would propagate edits but needs the catalog in every renderer.
- **Server.** `GET/PUT /whoobe-one/settings/alpine-presets` in `apps/core-api` (read `objects:read`, write `objects:write`, as editing a page: an editor can already put any directive on any element). Validation in `packages/core/src/cms/alpine-presets.ts` (exported by `@whoobe-cms/core/server`, 25 unit tests): name 1-40 characters of letters, digits, space . _ -, unique ignoring case; directives only `x-...` (with `:arg` and `.modifier`), `@event` and `:attr`, so `onclick`, `style` and `__proto__` are refused; string values up to 4000 characters, 1-40 directives per preset, 100 presets; unknown properties dropped; a refused write stores nothing (400 with the reason).
- **Editor.** `alpine-presets-api.ts`, `alpinePresets` in the store (loaded when the editor opens), and the panel in `AlpineControl.vue`: pick a preset and Apply (a check mark marks the presets already fully on the element), "Save as preset" from the element's current directives (an existing name is updated), and Manage: use current / edit (description and directives as JSON) / delete.
- **Security artifacts.** Two routes added to `scripts/security/routes.expected.json` (198) and to the API docs (198/198, `api-reference.md` and `openapi.yaml` regenerated). New suite `scripts/security/api/alpine-presets.test.ts` (20 tests: read-only can read not write, anonymous refused, 13 refused bodies leave the stored catalog untouched, markup in a value is data, catalog absent from the public payload). Run on the disposable stack with `authz-matrix`, `route-drift` and `public-surface`: 887 tests pass.
- **F-051 (new, Low).** That last test failed first, and rightly: `GET /public/websites/:id` returns the whole `website.settings` blob without authentication (also `logging`, `autosave`, `cdnMirror`, `defaultPlaceholderImage`; no secret, which `public-surface` already asserts). The catalog is now removed from that payload; the broader fix (return an allow-list of settings) is registered as open in `docs/release/security/findings.md` and the status summary (51 findings).
- Checked in a browser on the rebuilt test stack: add the six transitions through the panel, save as "fade-scale" (6 directives, values kept on the server), select another element, apply from the list (6 directives, the list marks it applied), the preview DOM carries `x-transition:enter` on both elements, delete the preset: catalog empty, the page keeps its directives; no page errors. `vue-tsc` clean, core suite 153 tests.
- Limits to know: Alpine does not run in the editing canvas, and the headless SDK / generated Next.js and Nuxt sites do not render `element.alpine` at all, so presets have no effect there (stated in the guide).

## [Dev console: no more "externalized for browser compatibility" warnings] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. The browser consoles of the editor (3006) and the admin dashboard (3000) showed 12 distinct warnings of the form `Module "path"/"url"/"fs"/"source-map-js" has been externalized for browser compatibility. Cannot access "..." in client code`. Cause: both apps call `sanitizeRichText()` from `@whoobe-cms/schema` (the XSS defence), which uses `sanitize-html`, which depends on `postcss`; postcss's file-path and source-map code imports those Node modules at load time. Vite's dev server answers with a proxy that logs on every property access. Not a Vue-compiler problem (the first lead, `source-map-js` under `@vue/compiler-core`, is a Node-side dependency of the build and is not in the client bundle).
- Not a functional problem: the sanitizer only calls postcss's `parse()` on a `style` attribute, which touches none of that code. Checked in Chromium on the running editor: `color`/`background-color`/`text-align` with plain values are kept, `position:fixed`, `url(javascript:...)`, `onerror`, `<script>` and `javascript:` links are removed, before and after this change. **Production builds never printed these warnings** (0 in both build logs), so deploys are not affected.
- Fix, dev server only: `resolve.alias` in `apps/whoobe-one-spike/vite.config.ts` and `apps/admin-dashboard/vite.config.ts` maps `fs`, `path`, `url` and `source-map-js` to an empty module (`src/node-builtin-stub.ts`, what Vite's proxy stands for anyway) when `command === 'serve'`; builds are unchanged. After it: 0 warnings in both consoles, no page errors, the production bundles have the same size. Clear `node_modules/.vite` (and `.vite/`) of the two apps after pulling this, Vite's dependency cache is not invalidated by the alias.
- Left alone on purpose: `postcss` and its friends still travel in the client bundle just to parse `style` attributes. Replacing `sanitize-html` in the browser with a browser-native sanitizer (or a tiny style parser) would remove them, but it changes the XSS defence's implementation and needs the whole sanitizer and browser XSS suites redone: a separate decision.

## [dev-cms.sh: stop without lsof, refuse busy ports, build every dependency of the apps] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. After the switch to the new working copy `./dev-cms.sh` failed with a cascade of errors. Two causes, reproduced and fixed:
- **Old dev servers kept running.** `--stop` ended with `lsof -ti:... | xargs kill -9`, and `lsof` is not installed here, so the processes started from the previous (renamed) folder survived and held ports 3001, 3002, 3003 and 3006; the new instance then failed on every port it could not bind. `--stop` now finds the listeners with `lsof` or, failing that, `ss`, stops them (TERM, then KILL) and reports any port still held. At start-up the script now refuses to run when one of the five ports is busy, naming the port, the pid and its working directory, instead of printing the errors.
- **`--build` built too little.** Behind the EADDRINUSE noise the real failure was `Cannot find module '@whoobe-cms/logger/dist/index.js'`: core-api, gateway, asset-processor and the dashboard import `@whoobe-cms/logger` directly, and `--build` compiled only core and migration-tool. It now builds everything the five dev apps depend on (`pnpm --filter '<app>^...' ... build`: schema, renderer, logger, core, migration-tool, figma-converter, publish), dependencies first.
- Checked from a tree with no `dist`: the build block succeeds; on the real copy `./dev-cms.sh --build` starts all five services and `/health` answers 200 on 3001, 3002 and 3003, 3000 and 3006 answer 200; `--stop` frees the ports; a second start while running is refused with the message above. shellcheck clean.

## [Fresh clone: build the dependencies first] — 2026-10-06
- Branch: `master`. After switching to a fresh clone, `./dev-cms.sh --build` failed: `pnpm --filter @whoobe-cms/core build` stopped with `TS2307: Cannot find module '@whoobe-cms/schema'`. A clone has no build output, `core` imports `@whoobe-cms/schema` from its `dist`, and a filtered build compiles only the named package. The same error had been fixed in the `Dockerfile` (2026-10-05) but not in the other places that build one package alone. Reproduced from a tree with no `dist` directories: `core`, `publish` and `mobile-app` fail alone (the last two on `@whoobe-cms/renderer`); `migration-tool` and `figma-converter` happened to pass.
- The filter now ends with `...` (pnpm builds the package's dependencies first): `dev-cms.sh` (core, migration-tool), `package.json` (`publish:site`, `publish:sync-cdn`), `publish-site.sh` (publish, nuxt), `publish-mobile.sh` (mobile-app), the commands in `README.md` and `docs/release/guides/publish/capacitor-mobile-app.md`, and the hint in `PwaSettingsCard.vue`. Checked from a copy with no `dist`: the four builds succeed, the old form reproduces the error. `README.md` now says that the first start after a clone needs `./dev-cms.sh --build`.
- `.vite/` (Vite's dependency-optimizer cache, created in the app folders) is git-ignored.

## [Documentation: current docs moved to docs/release] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. Reorganisation so that `docs/release/` holds the documentation that describes the product as delivered and operated, with `docs/plans/` kept as design history (many code comments point to it, so it was not moved). 27 files moved with `git mv`: `getting-started/` (manual, executive summary), `guides/` (DAM, dynamic website paths, headless SDK, API Gateway security, Brevo, website publishing, Capacitor), `operations/` (deployment, mini-guide, storage backends, creating workspaces), `security/` (security CI for forks, test suite, status summary, findings, test plan, manual checklist), `api/` (API reference, OpenAPI, generator). New `docs/release/README.md` (index, rules, status).
- Selection by evidence, not by date: tracked docs were audited for paths, `pnpm` scripts, environment variables and links that no longer exist, and API routes compared with `scripts/security/routes.expected.json`. Left out: all of `docs/plans/` and the early/one-off documents (`Initial-*`, `DAM_implementation_plan.md` with 7 dead links, `WhoobeStudio-Integration.md`, `WhoobeCMS-AssetsManagements.md`, `figma-travel.json`, `errors/console.error.publish.mobile.md`, which is a pasted 784-line console log).
- Fixed before moving: API docs were 18 core-api routes behind (object search/transitions/preview links/batch/query/translations, import/promote/extract of migrated documents, staged-page delete, search reindex, the page and snippet thumbnail endpoints): added to `gen-api-docs.py`, `api-reference.md` and `openapi.yaml` regenerated (244 entries; every one of the 196 core-api routes is documented). `guides/WCMS_asset_management.md` was written for MinIO and direct S3 URLs: sections 2, 3, 4, 5.1 and 10 rewritten for Garage/external S3, assets served through the asset-processor at `/dam-asset/...`, read-only server-side storage settings, the `/public/**` rule and the visibility flag.
- References updated by a path map in 31 files (README, compose and nginx comments, `.env.example`, the security workflow, `scripts/provision-workspace.sh`, `scripts/security/*` including the DAST `prepare.ts` that reads `docs/release/api/openapi.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, the plans that link to moved files); `gen-api-docs.py` now writes `api-reference.md` next to itself. A repo-wide check of `docs/...` paths and relative links found no new broken reference (11 older ones remain, all in documents that stay in `plans/`). `CHANGES.md` history keeps the old paths on purpose. Note: the DAST (ZAP) spec now includes the 18 added routes, so the next monthly/manual scan covers them.

## [CI third run: perl-base in the Debian runtime] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. Run 37323361765 (commit 29f3d2e): static, isolation and both TLS jobs green; API + browser suites passed, only `security:images` failed: `perl-base 5.36.0-7+deb12u3` (CVE-2026-13221, -42496, -8376 CRITICAL; -42497, -48962, -57432, -57433 HIGH; fixed in deb12u4) in core-api, gateway and asset-processor. New advisories published after the previous green run, not a regression of our code.
- `Dockerfile`, `runtime` stage: the `apt-get install --only-upgrade` line now covers `libpcre2-8-0 perl-base`. Upgrade checked on the pinned base image (deb12u4). Image scan to be confirmed by the next CI run. This is the second time in a day that the pinned digest lags Debian's security updates: if it keeps happening, consider `apt-get upgrade` in the runtime stage (always current, at the cost of a build that changes over time) or a more frequent Dependabot cycle for the base image.

## [Security CI for forks: English page] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. New `docs/security/security-ci-for-forks.md` (English): what the `security` pipeline gives a team that forks or clones the repository, in business and technical terms - jobs and triggers (read from `.github/workflows/security.yml` and `dependabot.yml`), what is checked, benefits, stated limits (not a certification; known gaps; TLS on the template only; baseline entries belong to this project) and how to use it in a fork (enable Actions, runner minutes, other Git hosts, review the baseline). Facts checked against the workflow, `package.json` scripts and `authz-matrix.test.ts` (five identities: anonymous, none, read, write, users manager).

## [Thumbnail from the Mobile/Tablet preview] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. "Capture thumbnail" existed only in the Desktop preview (a landscape 16:10 crop, 640px). In the Mobile/Tablet preview the page lives in an iframe, so there was no way to get a mobile-format image (marketing/commercial use).
- `DocumentPreview.vue`: the button is now shown in every device mode. On Tablet/Mobile it captures the page inside the preview iframe (same origin; rendered at the device viewport width, so responsive classes resolve as on the device) as exactly the visible device screen: frame width x height (375x667 portrait, 667x375 landscape per the toolbar toggle), taken at 2x (mobile 750x1334; capped at 800px wide), with the background the page really shows (`pageBackgroundColor`: body, then html, so a dark theme stays dark). Same save path as Desktop: the result replaces the page's thumbnail (`PUT /whoobe-one/pages/:id/thumbnail`).
- `scripts/capture-thumbnail.ts`: `captureElementThumbnail(el, opts)` with `width`, `aspect`, `fill`, `outputWidth`, `backgroundColor`; works on the element's own document (the offscreen clone is attached to the iframe document, not the editor's); renders at the pixel ratio needed for the output width; steps WebP quality down (0.8 -> 0.35) until the data URL fits the server's 400k-character limit (`MAX_THUMBNAIL_CHARS`). Desktop behaviour unchanged.
- `apps/admin-dashboard` `ThumbnailModal.vue`: the enlarged thumbnail is fitted to the screen (`max-h-[75vh]`, `w-auto`) instead of full width, which would run off the bottom for a portrait image. The list column keeps its 16:10 box (shows the top of a portrait image).
- Checked in the real editor (Vite against ws-demo, Chromium, PUT intercepted so nothing was written): mobile capture is 750x1334 WebP, ~75k characters, dark theme background, `md:` classes not applied; `vue-tsc` clean. Known limit, as for Desktop: web fonts are not embedded (`skipFonts`), headings fall back to a system font, and cross-origin images without CORS show a placeholder.

## [AI page generation: truncation detected, full reply kept, larger page limit] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. "AI Generate Page" ("a mobile landing page using tailwind for a music artist") failed with "The AI provider did not return valid JSON". The reply was never saved (only a 3000-character head in the error), so the cause could not be read back; a whole page as a verbose JSON tree is the usual case of a reply cut at the output limit (page generation used 8192 tokens), and nothing in the code could tell a cut-off reply from malformed JSON.
- `packages/core/src/ai/providers.ts`: the three adapters now read the provider's stop reason (OpenAI-compatible `finish_reason: length`, Anthropic `stop_reason: max_tokens`, Google `MAX_TOKENS`) and `parseJsonText(text, truncated)` reports "stopped at its output limit" instead of "invalid JSON". The error message keeps the head (2000) and the tail (800) of the reply, with the same `Raw output:` marker the website-structure retry loop parses; `AiGenerationError.rawText` carries the full reply. New `providers.test.ts` (3 tests); core suite 128 green, core-api `tsc` clean.
- `apps/core-api/src/server.ts` (`POST /ai/generate`): page generation asks for 16384 tokens (snippets and the rest stay 8192); a failed generation now stores the full reply in `whoobe_ai_generated.raw_response`, so the next failure can be diagnosed from the History/AI tab or the table.
- Not verified against a real provider (no call made with the configured OpenRouter key). Running stacks need their core-api image rebuilt to pick this up.

## [App Mode: theme background in the frame and in the mobile preview] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. Page with the "Whoobe Website" theme (dark `body` background, website template body `bg-[#0F1115] ...`) showed white in App Mode, both editing and preview.
- Editing: the phone frame in `BlockEditor.vue` had a fixed `bg-white`; the document root (min-height 600px) was dark but the remaining ~244px of the 844px frame were white. The frame now takes the page background by the same rule as the root (`appFrameBackground`: website template body className/style, or the document's own `canvasBackground`; white only when nothing is configured).
- Preview (Tablet/Mobile, an iframe = second app instance with an empty editor store): the document was passed through `sessionStorage`, the theme and the template body were not, so the iframe had neither the theme CSS/fonts nor the body background. `DocumentPreview.vue` now also stores `whoobe-preview-ctx` (`websiteTheme`, `templateBody`) and `views/PreviewView.vue` applies it before rendering. Pre-existing for Tablet/Mobile preview of any page, not only apps. `const editor` in `DocumentPreview.vue` moved above `storeDocForPreview`, which the immediate watch calls (it would have thrown inside the try/catch and stored nothing).
- Checked in the real editor with the theme and body read from the dev database: frame `rgb(15,17,21)`, preview iframe body `rgb(15,17,21)` with Montserrat headings; `vue-tsc` clean. Not changed: the theme's own `body { background }` rule still does not show through the editing canvas (the root's white fallback applies when the template has no body background).

## [App Mode: responsive classes follow the phone frame] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. In App Mode the canvas was narrowed to 390px but Tailwind's `sm:`/`md:`/`lg:` are media queries on the *window*, so a desktop window kept applying the desktop classes while editing. An iframe would fix it but breaks the single shared dragula instance (palette/tree -> canvas), the floating toolbar and the theme styles, so the variants are redefined instead.
- New `composables/useAppModeBreakpoints.ts`: while `appMode` is on (and the preview overlay is closed) a `<style type="text/tailwindcss" id="whoobe-appmode-breakpoints">` holds `@custom-variant sm|md|lg|xl|2xl` and `max-*` as `@container app (width >= / < Nrem)`. Off = empty text (Tailwind's own media queries). The element is kept and its text toggled: removing it does not make the runtime rebuild.
- `BlockEditor.vue`: the phone frame declares `container: app / inline-size`; the composable is wired next to `useThemeStyles`.
- Checked in the real editor (Vite against ws-demo, Chromium): `bg-red-500 md:bg-green-500 lg:bg-blue-500` is blue with App Mode off in a 1500px window, red inside the 390px frame, green with the frame at 800px, and back to blue on leaving App Mode; `vue-tsc` clean. Limits: `vh`/`h-screen` still use the window height; `min-[...]`/`max-[...]` arbitrary variants and custom theme breakpoints are not redirected; fixed-position elements are now positioned inside the frame (as on a phone).

## [First CI run: three fixes] — 2026-10-05
- Branch: `master`. The first `security` workflow run (run 35, commit 1d8b8a1) failed in 4 jobs for 3 causes, all only visible on a fresh checkout. Static scans passed.
- `Dockerfile`: `pnpm --filter @whoobe-cms/core build` -> `'@whoobe-cms/core...'` so `@whoobe-cms/schema` is built first (core imports it; on a clean tree `tsc` failed with TS2307). Fixes the isolation job; reproduced and verified on a clean worktree.
- `scripts/security/tls/run-testssl.sh`: `chmod 777` on the temp work dir; the testssl container runs under another uid and could not write `testssl.json` on the runner (`mktemp -d` is 0700), so `summarize.py` found no report. Fixes both TLS jobs; local run on `nginx:alpine` PASS.
- `scripts/security/unit/compose-hardening.test.ts`: creates an empty root `.env` for the test if missing (`docker-compose.apps.yml` has `env_file: .env`, absent in CI) and removes it afterwards. Not re-run locally (needs the stack); logic only.

## [Rich text: colour, highlight, alignment, links; lists survive] — 2026-10-04
- Branch: `master`. TipTap was not the limit, its configuration was: `TipTapPanel.vue` only had StarterKit + images.
- `apps/whoobe-one-spike/src/components/TipTapPanel.vue`: new extensions (text colour, highlight, underline, link, text alignment) with toolbar buttons; new dependencies `@tiptap/extension-{text-style,color,highlight,text-align,underline,link}` (2.27, same line as the rest).
- `packages/sdk/schema/src/sanitize.ts` (+ tests): `style` is now allowed on rich text but cut down to `color`, `background-color` and `text-align` with plain colour/keyword values (no `url()`, `var()`, `expression()`). Everything else in `style` is still removed. Applies to editor, previews, gateway output and SDKs; `dist` rebuilt.
- `apps/whoobe-one-spike/src/helpers.ts`: `stripBlockLevelTags` (run on blur of a canvas `p`) no longer unwraps `ul`/`ol`/`li` (and the `<p>` TipTap puts in each `li`), a suspected cause of lists vanishing from the canvas. **Not confirmed in the browser**.
- Rich text modal: header and Close button went off-screen with long text. `TipTapPanel.vue` root was `h-full` inside a flex column that already holds the header, so it overflowed the modal and the browser scrolled the `overflow-hidden` box to follow the caret. Now `flex-1 min-h-0`; `FloatingToolbar.vue` modal is `h-[70vh] max-h-[40rem]` (was `h-96`), the text area scrolls inside it.

## [Postgres restarts by itself after a reboot] — 2026-10-04
- Branch: `master`. Found while preparing a WSL shutdown: every service of `docker-compose.yml` had `restart: unless-stopped` except `postgres`, so after a host/daemon restart the apps came back and the database did not. `postgres` now has `restart: unless-stopped` in `docker-compose.yml` and `docker-compose.data.yml`. **Existing containers keep their old policy until recreated** (`docker compose -p <project> --env-file <env> up -d`; the data volume is kept).

## [Workspace isolation tests; images, TLS and isolation in CI] — 2026-10-04
- Branch: `master`. Closes two open items of the security status summary.
- **Isolation suite** (`scripts/security/isolation/`, `pnpm security:isolation`, 14 tests): stack A + a second disposable stack B (`SEC_STACK=b`: project `whoobe-sectest-b`, `.env.sectest-b`, ports +200, own DB/bucket/secrets; `gen-env.sh --offset N --name SUFFIX`; `stack.sh` `SEC_STACK` and `SEC_SERVICES`). Checks sessions, credentials, accounts, roles, assets, service token, direct S3 keys (via `@aws-sdk/client-s3`, new dev dependency of the security package), PostgreSQL passwords, Docker networks/name resolution, secrets and the gateway: nothing crosses between the two. First run found no isolation defect. Sanity check by mutation: giving B the same `DAM_SERVICE_TOKEN` as A makes two tests fail (secrets-differ and service-token), restoring it makes all pass. `lib/png.ts` is a shared tiny-PNG helper.
- **CI** (`.github/workflows/security.yml`, actionlint clean): `stack-tests` now also runs `pnpm security:images`; new job `tls` (testssl on `nginx:alpine` and `nginx:1.18-alpine`; PRs, push, nightly) and job `isolation` (push, nightly, manual). Still **not run on GitHub**.
- Docs: `docs/security/security-test-suite.md` (2.5.18), `scripts/security/README.md`, status summary. Note: the suite does not cover the parts a real host shares between workspaces (reverse proxy, the machine itself).

## [Asset visibility enforced by default; /public/** built in] — 2026-10-04
- Branch: `master`. Decision: private assets must be private everywhere, and the simple model is "public = in a public folder": creating `/public` (sub-folders included) makes every child public.
- `apps/asset-processor/src/server.ts`: `DAM_ENFORCE_ASSET_VISIBILITY` now defaults to **on** (`=false` switches it off); `/public/**` is a built-in public rule (lowest priority: a rule sent by the client for the same or a more specific path wins) used by uploads, moves and copies; an upload with no `isPublic` field is decided by the rules (an explicit `true`/`false` from the client still wins, which the admin always sends from the same rules). `scripts/provision-workspace.sh` no longer needs to set the flag; `scripts/security/gen-env.sh` no longer sets it, so the suites now run on the default.
- **Behaviour change** for existing installs (dev stack and workspaces, after rebuilding the asset-processor): assets recorded as private answer 404 without a session. Move what public pages and published sites use under `/public` (the DAM move keeps the files and updates the record), or set the flag to `false`. The record's `is_public` of already uploaded assets is not rewritten by this change.
- Test: `api/uploads.test.ts` (20): `/public/<x>/a/b/c` asset and its thumbnail are public without any client rule, an asset elsewhere is 404 anonymous and 200 with a session. Docs: `.env.example`, workspace guide, checklist, findings F-024, status summary.

## [New workspaces enforce asset visibility (F-024)] — 2026-10-04
- Branch: `master`. On `ws-demo` an image uploaded as private was reachable from an anonymous window: `DAM_ENFORCE_ASSET_VISIBILITY` is off by default and the provisioning did not set it (the flag was kept off pending a decision, F-024).
- `scripts/provision-workspace.sh` now writes `DAM_ENFORCE_ASSET_VISIBILITY=true` into every new workspace's env file. **Behaviour**: an asset whose record is not public answers 404 without a session; assets used by public pages and published sites must be public (`/public/**` folder rule or per asset). Existing workspaces keep their setting: add the line and run `docker compose -p ws-<slug> --env-file .env.<slug> up -d asset-processor`.
- Applied to `ws-demo` and verified through the proxy: private asset anonymous 404 (was 200), logged in 200, `/public/` asset anonymous 200. Docs: guide (feature, verification step, troubleshooting), `.env.example`.

## [Provisioning: URL scheme follows the host name (fix for http-only workspaces)] — 2026-10-04
- Branch: `master`. Found on the first workspace created with the new scripts (`ws-demo`, `wsdemo.local`): login showed "Could not reach core-api to check session: Failed to fetch". `provision-workspace.sh` always baked `https://<hostname>` into the SPA build (`VITE_*_URL`) and into `S3_PUBLIC_BASE_URL`, `ASSET_PROCESSOR_URL` and `ADMIN_PUBLIC_URL`, but a `.local` host cannot get a certificate and only answers on HTTP.
- Fix: the scheme is now `https` for a public-looking domain and `http` for local/LAN names (localhost, `*.local`, `*.lan`, `*.internal`, single-label, IP, `host:port`); `--scheme http|https` overrides; the summary shows it. `docs/guides/system/creating-workspaces.md`: the option and a troubleshooting entry.
- Applied to the existing `ws-demo` (env corrected, `up -d --build`): the served bundle has only `http://wsdemo.local`, login through the proxy returns 200 with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: http://wsdemo.local` and an HttpOnly session cookie. A workspace later moved to HTTPS with `enable-tls.sh` keeps the baked scheme: for a local name that must be switched, change the URLs in its env file and rebuild.

Deleted from the repository: `deploy/nginx/aura.conf`, `deploy/nginx/_locations-aura.inc`. The untracked `.env.test` (a copy of its env file) and the `ws-aura-*` images were removed locally. New workspaces: `./scripts/create-workspace.sh` (docs/guides/system/creating-workspaces.md).

## [Workspace guide rewritten for the current deploy settings; provisioning/removal fixes] — 2026-10-04
- Branch: `master`. `docs/guides/system/creating-workspaces.md` rewritten: interactive `create-workspace.sh` first, Garage as the workspace store (no bucket step, no MinIO console), shared containerized proxy vs host nginx, hardened HTTPS (`enable-tls.sh`, staging first, HSTS commitment, renewal), verification, external S3 switch (`--profile local-s3 rm -sf garage garage-perms`, checked on a throwaway project), how to treat workspaces created before the Garage default (such as `aura`), backups (Garage volume not to be copied live; S3 client against the loopback port), removal and troubleshooting.
- `scripts/provision-workspace.sh`: sets `TRUST_PROXY=1` (behind the proxy every client used to look like 127.0.0.1 to the login lockout and rate limiters) and `ADMIN_PUBLIC_URL=https://<hostname>` (password-reset links); header comments no longer mention MinIO. **Existing workspaces** do not get these automatically: add the two variables to their `.env.<slug>` and recreate core-api/asset-processor/gateway.
- `scripts/remove-workspace.sh`: also removes `<slug>-ssl.conf` and `_locations-<slug>.inc` (the proxy kept serving the HTTPS host to dead ports); with `--keep-volumes` it no longer deletes `.env.<slug>` (the only credentials for the kept volumes).
- Not run end to end here: a full `create-workspace.sh` (the host already runs the `whoobe-proxy` container and a workspace `aura`; a trial would have reloaded the live proxy). The Garage path itself is covered by the stack tests and the compose unit tests.

## [Garage is the default S3 store; MinIO optional; external S3 by configuration] — 2026-10-03
- Branch: `master`. Supersedes the Garage overlay of the previous entry. Decision: localhost/LAN/CI use Garage (instances structurally replicable to production), production picks any cloud/enterprise S3 through configuration, self-hosted MinIO is documented as discouraged (F-050).
- **Image** `docker/garage` (`whoobe-garage:v2.4.1`: official Garage binary on alpine, pinned by digest, uid 10001): `entrypoint.sh` creates the single-node layout, the access key and the bucket on every start (idempotent), validates the key format (`GK` + 24 hex / 64 hex) with a clear error, derives the RPC secret from the S3 secret unless `GARAGE_RPC_SECRET` is set. Removed `docker-compose.garage.yml` and `scripts/garage-init.sh` (no manual step any more).
- **Compose**: `docker-compose.yml` runs `garage` (+ `garage-perms` one-shot) under profile `local-s3` (`COMPOSE_PROFILES=local-s3` in the env file); the apps call `S3_CONTAINER_ENDPOINT` (default `http://garage:3900`) so an external S3 needs no local store (drop the profile). `docker-compose.minio.yml` is the opt-in MinIO overlay (replaces Garage). `docker-compose.data.yml` = Postgres + Garage only, runnable on its own, separate from apps and SPAs. Host port variable is now `WS_S3_API_PORT` (was `WS_MINIO_API_PORT`; `WS_MINIO_CONSOLE_PORT` only for the overlay). **Existing installs** keep MinIO data in the `miniodata` volume: to move to Garage copy the bucket (docs/storage-backends.md) and set Garage-format credentials; to stay on MinIO add `-f docker-compose.minio.yml` and rename `WS_MINIO_API_PORT` to `WS_S3_API_PORT` if it was customised.
- `scripts/provision-workspace.sh`: Garage-format credentials, `COMPOSE_PROFILES`, `WS_S3_API_PORT`, waits for the store to be healthy (no `mc`). `scripts/security/stack.sh`: Garage default, `S3_BACKEND=minio` for the overlay, `down` removes both variants. `gen-env.sh`, `.env.example` updated.
- Tests: `unit/compose-hardening.test.ts` now covers Garage default, external S3 (no local store, endpoint from env), MinIO overlay and the data stack (11 tests). Verified: Garage default `pnpm security:api` 1038 + `pnpm security:browser` 8; MinIO overlay `pnpm security:api` 1038; data stack on its own (Garage healthy, bucket created); `trivy image whoobe-garage:v2.4.1` 0 fixable HIGH/CRITICAL.
- Docs: `docs/storage-backends.md` (rewritten), `docs/deployment.md` (MinIO marked discouraged, example credentials replaced by placeholders), `docs/miniguide-deployment.md`, `README.md`, `docs/guides/system/creating-workspaces.md`, security docs and findings F-050.

## [Garage as an alternative S3 store; storage backends doc] — 2026-10-03
- Branch: `master`. Follow-up to F-050 (MinIO's own dependencies carry CVEs).
- `docker-compose.garage.yml` (overlay: Garage `dxflrs/garage:v2.4.1` pinned by digest, unprivileged via a `garage-perms` one-shot, loopback port, healthcheck; MinIO not started), `docker/garage/garage.toml` (single node, secrets from env), `scripts/garage-init.sh` (idempotent layout, key import, bucket, grants). `scripts/security/stack.sh`: `S3_BACKEND=garage` selects it; `down` now removes both variants and orphans (a plain `down` after a Garage run used to leave Garage running). `scripts/security/gen-env.sh`: S3 credentials in Garage's format (valid for MinIO too) and `GARAGE_*` secrets.
- `packages/core/src/dam/s3-provider.ts`: a store answering `NotImplemented` to `PutBucketPolicy` (Garage) is detected once and not asked again (it used to log a stack trace on every upload). No change for MinIO/AWS.
- Docs: `docs/storage-backends.md` (MinIO / Garage / AWS S3 or enterprise S3 via env only), `.env.example`. `run-testssl.sh` shellcheck fix.
- Verified on both backends: `pnpm security:api` 1034 and `pnpm security:browser` 8 pass; `trivy image dxflrs/garage:v2.4.1`: 0 fixable HIGH/CRITICAL; `pnpm security:static` PASS.
- **Not changed**: MinIO stays the default. Existing installs need nothing; to switch, copy the bucket first (see the doc).

## [Security: testssl (TLS edge) and trivy image; image hardening] — 2026-10-03
- Branch: `master`. Findings F-049 (TLS) and F-050 (images); F-048 bumped.
- **TLS** (`scripts/security/tls/`: `run-testssl.sh`, `summarize.py`, `baseline.json`, `pnpm security:tls`): `deploy/nginx/workspace-ssl.conf.template` now sets `ssl_protocols TLSv1.2 TLSv1.3`, an AEAD+ECDHE cipher list, session cache (tickets off), `server_tokens off` and HSTS (1 year, no includeSubDomains/preload). Before: nginx 1.18 offered TLS 1.0/1.1 and no TLS 1.3, all versions offered CBC/no-PFS ciphers and no HSTS. **Behaviour change**: TLS 1.0/1.1-only or CBC-only clients are refused; browsers pin HTTPS for the host for a year. Applies to existing workspaces when their `-ssl.conf` is re-rendered (`scripts/enable-tls.sh`) and nginx reloaded.
- **Images** (`scripts/security/images/`: `run-trivy-image.sh`, `summarize.py`, `.trivyignore`, `pnpm security:images`): 163 fixable HIGH/CRITICAL per Node image before; 0 now. `apps/asset-processor`: `multer` 1.x -> ^2.4 (lockfile updated). `packages/logger`: `pino-pretty` moved to dependencies. `Dockerfile`: new `prod` stage (`pnpm prune --prod`, esbuild binaries removed) and `runtime` base (no pnpm/corepack/npm) for core-api, asset-processor and gateway; SPA images `apk upgrade pcre2`. MinIO `RELEASE.2025-09-07T16-13-09Z` -> `RELEASE.2025-10-15T17-29-55Z` in `docker/minio/Dockerfile`, both compose files and docs (fixes CVE-2025-62506). Advisory only: postgres (Go stdlib of gosu) and MinIO's own go.mod (62) - decision on replacing MinIO is open.
- **On servers**: `docker compose build && docker compose up -d` (MinIO is rebuilt, ~3 min; volume untouched); re-run `scripts/enable-tls.sh` per workspace and `nginx -s reload` to pick up the TLS policy. Verified on the test stack: `pnpm security:api` 1034, `pnpm security:browser` 8 pass.
- Docs: `docs/security/security-test-suite.md` (2.5.16, 2.5.17), `scripts/security/README.md`, `findings.md`.

## [Security 3.6: browser XSS suite (Playwright) and rich-text sanitising] — 2026-10-03
- Branch: `master`. Findings F-045 (High), F-046; F-008 re-rated and mostly fixed (see `docs/plans/security/findings.md`).
- **Found with Chromium**: stored rich text executed script in the editor (trusted origin, admin session) and on published pages (`<img onerror>`, `<svg onload>`, `srcdoc` frames, `javascript:` links, mXSS).
- `packages/sdk/schema/src/sanitize.ts` (new, `sanitize-html`): `sanitizeRichText()` and `sanitizeSvg()` with an allow-list (25 tests, payload corpus). Used by: spike `BlockElement`, `BlockTable`, `BlockTablePreview`, `BlockElementPreview`, `BlockContainerPreview`; admin `BlockNodePreview`, `SnippetNodeRenderer`, `MarkdownEditor` (the `marked` preview); Studio `StudioBlockPreview`, `MokaSvgElement`; SDK `react` and `vue` defaults; `core`'s `sanitizePublicGatewayContent` (element `content` of every public page/snippet/template, 3 tests). `stripBlockLevelTags` parses in an inert document. `@whoobe-cms/schema` added as dependency of the spike, admin, studio, core-api and core.
- `nginx/spa-fallback.conf`: `absolute_redirect off;` and `location = /assets` -> `index.html` (the admin SPA route `/assets` collided with the build's static directory and redirected to `:8080`) - F-046.
- Harness: `scripts/security/playwright.config.ts`, `browser/fixtures.ts`, `browser/xss-sinks.spec.ts` (5 tests with positive controls), root script `pnpm security:browser`, timestamped JSON in `reports/<date>/browser-*.json`; `@playwright/test` dev dependency; `.gitignore` for Playwright output. `pnpm security:api` 1027 pass, browser 5 pass, `pnpm security:static` PASS, `pnpm build` 20 tasks.
- **Behaviour change**: rich text is filtered when rendered: iframes/embeds, `<style>`, inline `style=`, `id`/`name`, forms and `data-*` attributes are removed (formatting, links, images, tables, lists stay). Stored content is not modified. If a site relies on embedded HTML, tell me which elements to add to the allow-list.
- Not browser-tested: editor tables, form-result messages, the Studio app, generated Next/Nuxt sites and the mobile/PWA templates (covered by the sanitised public API).
